  a test host such as `www.example.com.test.local` reaches the siteaccess of `www.example.com` without entries of its
  own. Both probes use the new `eZSiteAccess::hostMatches()`. As for `host_uri`, the static cache only knows strict
  matching.
- An address that no probe of `MatchOrder` matches, where `DefaultAccess` would apply (typically the one without
  siteaccess or language segment), can get its siteaccess from the new setting `site.ini [SiteAccessSettings]
  DefaultHostUriMatchMapItems[]` (`host;uri;siteaccess[;method[;language]]`), with any `MatchOrder`: the first
  entry for the host whose language the browser accepts (`Accept-Language`, the most wanted first, `de` also for
  `de-CH`), else the first entry without a language; an empty host with `part` matches any host. A GET or HEAD
  request is sent on (302), once, to the same address with the uri part, kept as it was asked for, also for a site
  with one language: `/` to `/ger`, the URL alias `/news/an-article` to `/ger/news/an-article`, with its query. So
  every cached page has one address, and an address a probe matches (`/ger/...`, `/admin/...`, after switching the
  language too) is never sent on. The redirect needs a probe that takes the address with the uri part for that
  siteaccess; without one, for other requests or with `DefaultHostUriRedirect=disabled` the page is shown at the
  address itself with links carrying the uri part. When the host has more than one language variant, the redirect,
  and a page shown this way, are sent with `Vary: Accept-Language` and `Cache-Control: private`, and the role-aware
  HTTP cache does not store them. New `eZSiteAccess::matchDefaultHostUri()`, `acceptedLanguages()`,
  `reachesSiteAccess()` and `ezpKernelWeb::languageRedirectURI()`. Guide: [Siteaccess by host prefix, and the
  redirect to the language](../../eng-us/features/6.0/siteaccess-host-and-language-matching.md). Test:
  `eZSiteAccessMatchTest` (no database).

### Updated

- The redirect to the language sends a path on the host that was asked for (`Location: /ger/news?x=1`), not a URL
  built from the `Host` header, so a forged host is never written into it. The path, which the kernel keeps
  decoded, is encoded again: an encoded line break cannot split the header, `%2F%2F` or a backslash cannot turn it
  into an address on another host, and empty and dot segments are dropped. It starts with the index file and the
  segment of the entry instead of the address built for links, which `RemoveSiteAccessIfDefaultAccess=enabled`
  shortened by the segment of the default siteaccess: with `DefaultAccess` the language siteaccess, the browser was
  sent to the address it had asked for, endlessly. New `ezpKernelWeb::languageRedirectLocation()`, which never
  answers with the address asked for itself.
- The answer at an address without segment also varies by `Accept-Language` where an entry names a language and
  the entries lead to another siteaccess with the same segment, or where no entry applies without a language (a
  German browser went to `/ger`, any other got `DefaultAccess` at the same address without `Vary`, and a shared
  cache could hand the one answer to the other). A `Vary` from `[HTTPHeaderSettings]` is kept and extended.
- The HTTP cache's early exit (`ezpHttpCacheContract::resolveSiteAccess()`) knows `HostMatchMethod`, the method of
  a host map entry and the hosts of `DefaultHostUriMatchMapItems`; before, it served the page of `DefaultAccess`
  kept for `/` instead of letting the kernel choose by language.
- Host matching (host map, `host_uri`, the language default) compares host names without regard to case and to a
  trailing dot, ignores the port unless the entry names one, reads a Unicode entry as its ASCII form, and `end`
  matches the end of the host also where the listed host comes twice (`example.com.example.com`). An entry of
  `DefaultHostUriMatchMapItems` whose siteaccess is not in `AvailableSiteAccessList` is ignored and logged.
  `acceptedLanguages()` reads at most 32 languages, skips what is not a language tag and counts a `q` above 1 as 1.
  `RedirectOnNormalize` no longer sends a redirect while `reachesSiteAccess()` only asks where an address leads.
  Tests: `eZSiteAccessMatchTest`, `ezpHttpCacheContractTest` (no database).
- Only the web page request uses the language default: `eZSiteAccess::match()` takes `DefaultHostUriMatchMapItems`
  into account only when its new fifth parameter `$languageDefault` is `true`, which `ezpKernelWeb` alone passes.
  The REST interface (`ezpKernelRest`), the tree menu (`ezpKernelTreeMenu`), extensions and scripts match as before
  #131: the probes, then `DefaultAccess`, never by the browser's language, without redirect or `Vary`. Test:
  `eZSiteAccessMatchTest` (no database).

## 6 October 2026: the users and groups of a role a page at a time

### Updated

- `role/view` shows the users and user groups a role is assigned to a page at a time, sorted by name, on the
  parameter `(assignment_offset)`. The new setting `site.ini [RoleSettings] AssignmentsPerPage` (default `50`, `0`
  lists all) sets the page size. A role given to thousands of users loaded every one of them with its object. The
  heading counts all of them (`$assignment_total`; `$assignment_count` is what the name filter keeps); the admin designs page with the navigator, `design/standard` with
  previous and next links. New `eZRole::assignmentCount()` and `eZRole::assignmentPage( $offset, $limit )`;
  `fetchUserByRole()` still returns all. A design that overrides `role/view.tpl` must add a pager, see
  [behaviour changes](../../eng-us/bc/6.0/behaviour-changes-2026-10.md). Test: `eZRoleAssignmentPageLiveTest` (live).
- A page of `role/view` costs the same few queries whatever its size: the objects, main nodes and limitation nodes of
  a page come in one query each, and the name shown is the one fetched with the object (a role with 114 assignments:
  106 queries before, 10 now). The sorting is the same on every database: assignments whose object is gone first,
  then by name without regard to the case of a-z, then by assignment (checked on SQLite, MySQL, PostgreSQL, Oracle and
  MongoDB, which sorts in php).
- An assignment whose user or group no longer exists is listed first as such and counted above the list, so it can be
  removed; it was an empty link. A limitation whose node or section is gone says "not found".
- Paging or sorting the policies keeps the place in the assignments and the other way round, and the form (Remove
  selected, Assign) comes back to the same page; past the last page shows the last page.
- The audit of removing assignments reads the rows of the role without loading their objects (it loaded every object of
  the role once per removed assignment) and records the user id of a gone object instead of 0.

### Added

- A name filter on the assignments of `role/view`: `(assignment_filter)/<text>`, without regard to case, `%` and `_`
  matched as themselves. New `eZRole::assignmentOrphanCount()`, `eZRole::assignmentRows()` and the optional `$filter`
  of `assignmentCount()` and `assignmentPage()`.
- `role/list` shows how many users and groups each role is assigned to (column Assigned, one query per page:
  `eZRole::assignmentCounts()`). The role name in the `design/standard` role list is escaped.
- Translations (eng-US, ger-DE, untranslated) of the new texts. Guide:
  [Role assignment paging](../../eng-us/features/6.0/role-assignment-paging.md). Tests: `eZRoleAssignmentSortTest` (no
  database) and new cases of `eZRoleAssignmentPageLiveTest` (gone object, filter, role list counts, query count).

## 6 October 2026: links, URL aliases, wildcards and search statistics

### Updated

- `url/list` is an overview (links in published content, valid, invalid, never checked, the last link check), a
  search over the addresses (`?q=`), the lists all, valid, invalid and the new `unchecked`, an order (`(sort)/checked`,
  `(sort)/modified`, address A to Z by default) and one card per link with its state, how the link check treats it
  (e-mail, on this site, link to content, not tested, other address), its times, the published objects that use
  it (two queries per page) and View, Edit and Open. **Mark selected valid** and **Mark selected invalid** use the
  `SetValid` and `SetInvalid` actions the module always had but no template offered, and say how many links changed.
  The page size comes from `admininterface.ini [PaginationSettings]` (`url/list`).
- Fixed: `url/list` printed the address unescaped and made it a link whatever its scheme; Open is now offered for web, mail and site addresses
  only, with `rel="noopener noreferrer"`.
- `content/urltranslator` leads with the creation form, each field explained and its error on the field; what was
  typed stays in the form when an alias cannot be created (it was emptied). The list searches the last segment and destination
  (`?q=`), filters redirecting or direct aliases (`(kind)/...`), shows each alias's whole path and what its
  destination resolves to, and marks an alias whose module no longer exists. **Remove all** asks in place how many
  aliases go. The destination of an alias is escaped.
- `content/urlwildcards` refuses a destination with a placeholder the pattern has no `*` for (new info code
  `error-wildcard-placeholder`), searches pattern and destination, filters by kind, and **Try an address**
  (`?test=`) says which wildcard an address matches, what it becomes and what that resolves to, as the wildcard
  cache would. **Remove all** asks in place.
- `search/stats` says which siteaccesses record searches (`site.ini [SearchSettings] LogSearchStats`, disabled by
  default) and warns when none does, counts phrases, searches and those that found nothing, searches the phrases,
  filters those that found nothing (`(show)/none`), orders by count, phrase or fewest results (`(sort)/...`), draws
  each count as a bar and runs a phrase again with **Search now**. **Reset statistics** asks in place first (it had
  no confirmation). The page size the template used is now the one the view pages with.
- The four pages share the look of the section and cronjobs pages, in `design/admin` and `design/admin4`
  (the url and search sections of `stylesheets/modules/workflow.css`), in the light and dark modes, and work without javascript. Every
  field name, button and template variable they had is kept.
- `eZURL::fetchList()` and `fetchListCount()` take `last_checked` (`never`, `checked`), `search` (with
  `only_published`) and `sort` (`address`, `checked`, `modified`, `id`) on every database; on MongoDB the published
  listing now also returns `created` and `modified`.
- The link check (`linkcheck.php`, part `infrequent`) decides with the new `expLinkCheck`. `https://` is requested
  like `http://` (it was never tested): HEAD, then GET when HEAD fails, redirects followed one by one up to
  `MaxRedirects`, TLS verified, a timeout and a User-Agent of its own. 429 and 503, and 401 and 403 (a site
  refusing automated requests), decide nothing and keep the state. Links to content (`ezlocation://`, `eznode://`,
  `ezobject://`) are valid while their node or object exists, is published and is not hidden; they were looked up
  as paths and always marked invalid. A path of the site is found behind its siteaccess name, query and fragment,
  and a module view counts. `file:` addresses are no longer tested. `mailto:` and other schemes are treated as
  before.
- The link check never requests an address on a private, loopback, link-local or reserved network, also behind a
  redirect, unless `AllowedPrivateHosts[]` names its host, address or range, and pins each request to the address it
  checked (SSRF). It waits `HostDelay` between requests to one host, skips links checked less than
  `RecheckInterval` seconds ago, checks the ones checked longest ago first and ends with its counts, requests and
  time. New keys in `cronjob.ini [linkCheckSettings]`: `Timeout`, `ConnectTimeout`, `MaxRedirects`, `HostDelay`,
  `RecheckInterval`, `MaxURLsPerRun`, `UserAgent`, `AllowedPrivateHosts[]`. On the demonstration server the first run checked 350 links
  in 9.5 minutes with 725 requests (valid 284 to 254, invalid 66 to 96, never checked 21 to 0).
- The badges of `url/list` follow the new rules ("https: not tested" is gone; "Other address" for other schemes).
- The search and kind filter of `content/urltranslator` and `content/urlwildcards` are conditions of the database
  query (they read every row and filtered in PHP): new `eZURLAliasQuery` properties `search` (last segment or
  action) and `redirects`, new `eZURLWildcard::fetchFilteredList()` and `fetchFilteredListCount()`, on every
  engine and MongoDB.

### Added

- Guide: [Links, URL aliases, wildcards and search statistics](../../eng-us/guides/urls-and-aliases.md). Test:
  `expUrlAdminPagesTest`, `expLinkCheckTest` (no database, no network).

## 6 October 2026: RSS feeds, triggers, workflow groups and class groups

### Updated

- `rss/list` is an overview (exports, active exports, imports, active imports, imported objects), a search and a
  filter, then one card per export with its public feed address, format in words, sources with their class,
  number of items, when the feed was last written (the newest cached copy) and what needs attention, and one card
  per import with its destination, class, owner, the objects it has created and the newest of them. A status bar
  names the cronjob part that runs `rssimport.php`, its schedule and next run. Sorting, paging and the page size
  are as before; new template variables `rssexport_info`, `rssimport_info`, `rss_summary`, `rss_import_cronjob`,
  `rss_feed_cache_time`, `rss_feedback`. Guide: [RSS feeds](../../eng-us/guides/rss-feeds.md).
- Removing RSS exports or imports takes two steps: Remove selected shows `design:rss/confirmremove.tpl` with what
  removing means, and only `ConfirmRemoveButton` posted with the same ids removes. A removed export now takes its
  sources and OPML lines with it (`removeThis()`; they were left behind).
- `rss/edit_export` and `rss/edit_import` are forms in the same look, each field with what it is used for: the
  export in the feed, what it lists (one card per source with its location, class and attribute mapping) and the
  Apple Podcasts or OPML part; the import in four steps (feed, where the items go, what each item becomes, active).
  The export form shows the public feed address (new template variable `rss_feed_url`); validation messages are
  escaped. An import is stored only with a name and an http or https source address
  (`\Exponential\View\Kernel\Rss\EditImport::validate()`, `validation_errors`), and says how many objects it has
  created (`rss_import_imported`). The content browser descriptions of the RSS pickers and the lock pages
  (`edit_export_denied.tpl`, `edit_import_denied.tpl`, new in `design/admin` and `design/admin4`) use the same look.
  Every field and button name is unchanged.
- `trigger/list` names every operation in words ("Before publishing content"), grouped by module, with the workflow
  it runs, its events, whether it is disabled and its waiting processes; triggers without a workflow are marked
  "Not set". A trigger whose workflow is no longer offered by the select keeps it as an extra choice, so Apply
  changes no longer drops it unnoticed; one whose workflow was removed is marked. Stored triggers for operations no
  longer in `AvailableOperationList` are listed and can be removed (the existing `RemoveButton`). Apply changes says
  how many triggers changed after the redirect. Workflow names are escaped. Guide:
  [Workflows, section 11](../../eng-us/guides/workflows.md#11-the-trigger-list-page).
- `workflow/grouplist` shows each group with its workflows, their triggers, waiting processes, other groups and last
  change, and what removing the group does. Removal asks first (`design:workflow/confirmremovegroup.tpl`,
  `ConfirmRemoveButton`). A workflow that is also in another group now stays there instead of being removed, a
  disabled workflow of the group is removed too instead of being left without a group, and a removed workflow's
  events and temporary version go with it, as in `workflow/workflowlist`. Guide:
  [Workflows, section 12](../../eng-us/guides/workflows.md#12-the-workflow-group-list-page).
- `class/grouplist` shows each group with its classes and their published objects, the last change of the group or
  its classes, and how many classes and objects removing it would remove; an overview counts classes in more than
  one group and in none. Removal still goes through `class/removegroup`. Guide: [Class groups](../../eng-us/guides/class-groups.md).
- The other class pages use the same look: `class/classlist` (the classes of a group with their objects, the other
  groups they are in, View, Edit and Copy, a search, New class in a chosen language), `class/view` (settings,
  attributes as cards, the class groups, translations and override templates behind chips), `class/edit` (the class
  settings and one card per attribute with every field explained; the attribute order script, Apply and Cancel back
  to the page the form came from work as before), `class/groupedit`, the language choice, the edit lock page and
  the confirmations of `class/removeclass`, `class/removegroup` and translation removal, which now say how many
  objects go. Class names, override template paths, translation names and validation messages that were printed
  unescaped are escaped. Copy on the class list is a form button that posts to `class/copy/<id>`. Every field, button
  and template variable is unchanged.
- `class/copy/<id>` copies only for a POST that carries `CopyClassButton` or `ConfirmCopyButton` (the form token
  protects it). Opened by a link it shows what the copy will be (`design:class/copy_confirm.tpl`, new in
  `design/admin`, `design/admin4` and `design/standard`) with a Copy button; before, following a link made a copy.
  The copy links of `design/standard` are form buttons now. Test: `expAdminListsRedesignTest`.
- The four pages use the look of the redesigned section pages in `design/admin` and `design/admin4`, light and dark,
  with module-local `exp_list_script.tpl` and the module stylesheets under `stylesheets/modules/`. Every existing template variable, field and button
  name is unchanged. `Processlist::scriptCronjob()` finds the cronjob part of any script (`workflowCronjob()` uses
  it). Test: `expAdminListsRedesignTest` (no database).

## 6 October 2026: sessions and collected information

### Updated

- **Setup > Sessions** (`setup/session`) is redesigned for the admin and admin4 designs. With a session table
  (`ezpSessionHandlerDB`): figures (sessions, users active within `ActivityTimeout`, sessions of signed in users,
  anonymous sessions, timed out sessions still stored), a search over names, logins and e-mail addresses beside the
  user filter, one card per user or per session with last activity and idle time in words, sorting by last activity,
  name, login, e-mail and count (`(sortby)`, `(order)`), and page sizes (`admininterface.ini [PaginationSettings]
  ItemsPerPageList_setup_session[]`, 50, 25, 100). Remove selected and Remove all ask first on a confirmation page
  (`ConfirmSessionRemoval`); the session the administrator is using is never removed by a selection, and removing
  one's own user keeps it. Session keys no longer reach the page: one user's sessions are posted as references
  (`SessionRefArray[]`), `SessionKeyArray[]` from older overrides is still accepted for that user's keys only.
  Every button and field name is kept. The list reads names, logins and e-mail addresses in its one query instead of
  loading every user, and a page past the end shows the first page instead of redirecting.
- Without a session table (`ezpSessionHandlerPHP`, the default) the page said only that the handler is not
  supported. It now names the handler and PHP's storage, lists who signed in within the last hour, day or session
  lifetime from `ezuservisit` with a search, sorting and paging, and explains how to switch to the database handler.
- **Setup > Collected information** (`infocollector/overview`) is redesigned: figures (objects with collections,
  collections, the last 30 and 7 days, the latest collection, objects removed or in the trash whose collections are
  not listed), a search by name, sorting by name, collections, last or first collection and type, one card per object
  with its recent collections and links to the collections, the object and the CSV export, and the published objects
  that can collect but have nothing yet. A page is one query instead of three per row. The removal confirmation
  (also used by `infocollector/collectionlist`) lists what goes, says what stays and that the data can be exported
  first, and the overview says how many collections were removed or that nothing was selected.

### Added

- `infocollector/export/<ObjectID>`: everything an object collected as CSV (collection ID, sent, changed, user ID and
  one column per information collector attribute), formula cells guarded, at most 50 000 rows, audited as
  `data.export.csv` without values. Policy `infocollector/read`.
- Guides: [Sessions](../../eng-us/guides/sessions.md) and [Collected information](../../eng-us/guides/collected-information.md).
  Tests: `SessionPageTest` (no database).

## 6 October 2026: PDF exports

### Updated

- A PDF export generated once wrote its file and then stopped with "[] operator not supported for strings" (PHP 8):
  the second template of the generation was handed a string where the template engine appends to an array. The
  export was never stored and its file stayed behind with nothing pointing at it. A PDF generated on the fly was
  streamed from inside a template (`pdf(stream)`: the whole file in a string, `ob_clean()` on a buffer that may not
  exist, no file name, `cleanExit()` in the middle of a template); it is now written to a temporary file and sent in
  chunks with `Content-Disposition`, after the page's buffers are dropped and the session is closed, with
  `cleanExit()` outside any catch block, so it works under Velocity's persistent workers.
- The list linked a stored file at `var/<site>/storage/pdf/<name>`, which the web server does not serve (only
  images are served from the storage directory): the link was always a 404. Every download goes through
  `pdf/edit/<id>/generate`, which now sends the stored file of an export generated once instead of an empty page.
- The file name came from the form through `basename()` only: an empty name, a hidden file or any character reached
  the storage path. It is now letters, digits, `.`, `_` and `-`, at most 100 characters, ending in `.pdf` (added
  when missing); removing an export deletes its file only for such a name.
- The edit form ticked class 1 when class 12 was chosen (a substring test on `"12:16"`), kept the old classes when
  every class was unticked, and labelled the file name "if generated on the fly" while it is the stored file's name.
- `pdf/edit` without an id made a new draft on every reload; it now redirects to `pdf/edit/<id>`.
- A stored PDF is public at `var/<site>/storage/pdf/<name>.pdf`, linked from its card: one rule for storage/pdf in
  `.htaccess_root`, `.htaccess_root_static` and the nginx example (`storage/pdf/*.pdf` only).
- **Setup > PDF export** (`pdf/list`) is redesigned for the admin and admin4 designs: what an export is, figures
  (exports, generated once, on the fly, stored files, their size, needing attention), the drafts of new exports that
  were never saved, a search over title, file name, ID, source node and classes, filters (generated once, on the fly,
  with a stored file, needing attention), sorting by title, modified, generated, file size and ID, page sizes
  (`admininterface.ini [PaginationSettings] ItemsPerPage[pdf/list]`, `ItemsPerPageList_pdf_list[]`, remembered per
  user), and one card per export: its source node and whether it still exists, what it contains, its stored file
  with size and date, Download, Regenerate (writes the file anew; greyed out with the reason when the source is
  gone) and Edit. Remove selected asks first and lists each export with the file that goes with it.
- **pdf/edit** explains every field in five groups (title, content, front page, footer, export type), checks them
  (a title, an existing source node, at least one class for a tree, a safe file name no other stored export uses) and
  names the field of each error; it says when somebody else has the export open. A new file is written to a
  temporary name first and replaces the old one only when complete. Cancel goes back to the page the form was
  opened from (safe redirect rules), else to the list.
- Every field and button name (`Title`, `SourceNode`, `ExportType`, `ClassList[]`, `DestinationType`,
  `DestinationFile`, `ExportPDFButton`, `DiscardButton`, `DeleteIDArray[]`, `RemoveExportButton`, `NewPDFExport`)
  and every template variable is kept; new are `ConfirmRemoveButton` and `RegenerateButton`. `generatePDF()` is kept
  and calls the new classes.

### Added

- `expPDFExportFile`, `expPDFExportGenerator`, `expPDFExportInfo` and `expPDFExportForm` (`kernel/classes/pdfexport`):
  the file name and stored file, the generation, what the pages say about an export, and the form. Tests:
  `expPDFExportTest` (no database).
- Guide: [PDF exports](../../eng-us/guides/pdf-exports.md).

## 6 October 2026: system information

### Updated

- **Setup > System information** (`setup/info`) is redesigned for the admin and admin4 designs. It leads with the
  server that answered (Apache with PHP-FPM and its pool, Exponential Velocity with its release and worker model,
  FrankenPHP, PHP's built-in server), then health checks with ok, warning, failure and note and what to do for each,
  overview cards (Exponential, server, PHP, OPcache and APCu, database, storage, caches, cronjobs, mail, locale and
  time, machine), and the detailed parts folded below them (extensions with their versions, PHP extensions and
  settings, site addresses, OPcache and APCu, Velocity, the engine archive, the HTTP cache, database queries, the
  response cache, the database connection). Every template variable of the earlier page is kept.
- OPcache was reported as "not installed" under PHP-FPM when `opcache_get_status` is in `disable_functions` (Plesk
  sets it per domain); it is now "enabled, statistics not available".
- Velocity's own server was said not to be started by exp:velocity when its worker could not read the TLS
  certificate; the configured HTTPS port is compared instead.
- The PHP settings no longer report `safe_mode` and `register_globals`, removed from PHP (the template keys stay,
  always off).
- `setup/info/php` calls `phpinfo()` without the request variables and the environment, which carried the HTTP
  sign-in and the session cookie, and as plain text under Velocity.
- Full server paths (installation root, engine archive, cache directories, Velocity's log) are shown relative to the
  installation.

### Added

- `expSystemReport`: the facts of the process that runs it, read only and in about 10 ms, the health checks and the
  cards; directory and database sizes only on request (`setup/info/sizes`, three seconds at most).
  `expSystemReportMask`: the masking rules (secret names, credentials in addresses, `password=...` pairs, session
  ids and tokens, paths).
- The report for a support request: `setup/info/report` (text) and `setup/info/json`, and **Copy report as text**.
- `./console exp:system:info [--json] [--sizes]` (`bin/php/systeminfo.php`), exit code 1 when a check fails.
- Guide [System information](../../eng-us/guides/system-information.md). Tests: `expSystemReportTest`,
  `expSystemReportMaskTest` (no database).
- The buttons of the HTTP cache (purge, remove dead entries, reset counters), the query cache (clear, reset
  counters) and the SQL profile (on, off) are on **Setup > Caches** as well, in a block of their own. Both pages post
  the same fields to one code path, `expCacheManager::sharedActionFromPost()`, which checks `setup/managecache`;
  every post carries the form token.
- `expSecretRule`: the one rule of what is a secret, shared by exp:ini (`expIniEditor::isSecret()`), the audit log,
  the settings pages (`expSettingsSecretRule`) and the system report (`expSystemReportMask`); names ending in `pwd`
  or `dsn` now count as secrets everywhere. A password inside an address keeps its user name on every page
  (`mysql://user:***@host`). Tests: `expSecretRuleTest`.

## 6 October 2026: the history of a draft for its editors

### Updated

- `content/history` opens for who may edit the object as well as for who may read it
  (`\Exponential\View\Kernel\Content\History::canOpen()`, with `eZContentObject::editAccess()`). A draft that was
  never published is readable for its owner only, so an approver or a further editor that an extension lets in
  through the filter `content/edit/access` could not reach the versions to make the next one from a rejected one.
  The forms of the page post without a version, which the check does not need; the diff still asks `can_diff`. Test:
  `eZContentHistoryAccessTest` (no database).
- The view's policy functions are `read or edit` (they were `read` and `edit`, so a user without any read policy
  never reached the view). `History::canOpen()` lets in who may edit the object, and who may read it with an edit
  policy for some content, as before; the anonymous user and other readers without an edit policy stay out.
- Fixed: the page showed the content of versions the user may not read. It now compares, copies and links to the
  version view only for versions whose content the user may see (`History::canSeeVersionContent()`): the published
  and archived versions and one's own, a rejected version for who may edit the object, and someone else's draft or
  pending version only with `content/versionread` for it. This also closes the comparison of other users' drafts
  for readers, which `content/diff` alone allowed. A comparison or copy of such a version sent in a request is not
  done, and the page says so (`design:content/history_access_messages.tpl`).
- Fixed: removing versions takes only versions of this object in the statuses the page offers (draft, archived,
  rejected, untouched draft) and says which versions were not removed; a pending version in a running workflow can
  no longer be removed by a made-up request. Copying starts the new draft in one of the version's own translations
  and reports a language the user may not edit on the page instead of a 403. An unknown version in the edit action
  returns to the list instead of ending in an error, and a comparison skips an attribute one version lacks.
- An editor who may edit but not read the object gets a note on the page; the copy button of a version they may not
  see says why it is disabled. New strings in eng-US, ger-DE and the untranslated catalogue.
- Guide: [Edit access to objects that were never published](../../eng-us/bc/6.0/draft-edit-access.md), section
  "The versions of such an object". Tests: `eZContentHistoryAccessTest` (10 cases, no database) and
  `eZContentHistoryAccessLiveTest` (8 cases with its own users, roles and drafts).
- Fixed: the Back button of `content/history` opened directly went to the edit the session had last sent to the
  history ("Manage versions", even of another object, an edit that makes a new draft), or to the page viewed last.
  It now goes to where the page was opened from: the edit of a version of this object that opened it with "Manage
  versions" (taken once), else the page the browser came from, each only when `eZRedirectManager::safeURI()` and
  the return rules take it (never the history itself, never `content/edit/<object>` without a version, never an
  edit of another object). Without an origin it goes to the object's own location in the full view
  (`/content/view/full/<main node>`), or to the content dashboard for an object never published. The origin is
  carried in the form (`RedirectURI`, now escaped), so comparing, copying and removing keep it. All four designs.
  `History::originURI()`, `History::originCandidate()`; test `eZContentHistoryAccessTest` HA-11.

## 6 October 2026: My drafts and My pending items in the look of the redesigned pages

### Updated

- Fixed: `content/draft` removed any version whose id a request named (`DeleteIDArray[]` with **Remove selected**),
  someone else's draft or a published version included, for every user who may open the page. It now removes only
  the current user's own drafts (and untouched drafts), each checked again at the moment it is removed
  (`expContentDraftList::removable()`); every other id is left as it is and the page says how many.
- `content/draft` is figures (drafts, of new objects, not modified for 30 days, translations, classes), a search
  (`?q=`), filters by translation, class and age (7, 30, 90, 365 days), an order (last modified, oldest, name, class),
  paging (`admininterface.ini [PaginationSettings]`, key `content/draft`, preference `admin_draft_list_limit`) and one
  card per draft: name, class, location or "new object", translation, version, created, modified and age, with Edit,
  View and Remove confirmed in place. **Remove selected**, the new **Remove old drafts** (`RemoveOldButton`,
  `OldDraftDays`) and **Remove all** are confirmed in place; one draft is removed with `RemoveDraftButton[<id>]`.
  `RemoveButton`, `DeleteIDArray[]` and `EmptyButton` are kept.
- `content/pendinglist` lists the user's own pending versions and, new, the versions waiting for the user's approval
  that the user may read (`content/versionread`), each with the state of its approval and its approvers, the
  workflows whose process holds it, who sent it and when, its location, and links to the version, the approval (when
  the user takes part in it) and the published page; figures, filters by list and class, an order and paging (key
  `content/pendinglist`, preference `admin_pending_list_limit`).
- Both pages are in `design/admin` and `design/admin4` with the look of the versions page
  (the history and draft sections of `stylesheets/modules/content.css`); `design/standard` keeps its pages. The views
  are thin; the lists are `expContentDraftList` and `expContentPendingList`, which need no database.

### Added

- Guide: [My drafts and my pending items](../../eng-us/guides/drafts-and-pending.md). Test: `expContentDraftListTest`
  (no database).

## 6 October 2026: the versions page in the look of the redesigned pages

### Updated

- `content/history` is an introduction to versions and to making a new draft from one, the notices of the last
  action, figures (all versions, each status, translations, your drafts), filters by status, translation and
  creator, an order (newest, oldest, last modified), paging (`admininterface.ini [PaginationSettings]`, key
  `content/history`, preference `admin_history_list_limit`) and one card per version: its number, status,
  translations, creator and dates, and the actions you have on it (View, Edit, Compare, Make a new draft in a
  translation you may edit, select for removal). An action you do not have says why on the card, or once above the
  list when no version on the page allows it.
- Comparing is a two-picker panel offering only the versions whose content you may read, and a Compare button on
  each card (new POST `CompareButton[n]`: with the current version, or for the current one with the newest other
  version you may read). The differences are shown above the list as inline changes, block changes, the old or the
  new version, in colours that read on white.
- Removing ticked versions is confirmed in place with the list of versions; the page then says which were removed
  and which were not (new refusal `remove-none` when nothing was ticked). Making a new draft says which draft was
  made; untick "Stay on this page" (`DoNotEditAfterCopy`) to open it in the editor at once.
- The edit warnings and the access notices (`content/history_access_messages.tpl`, now also in `design/admin` and
  `design/admin4`) are notices in the look of the redesigned pages. The same page is in `design/admin`,
  `design/admin3` and `design/admin4` (the history section of `stylesheets/modules/content.css`); the
  `design/standard` page is unchanged and keeps working. Every POST name and template variable of the page is kept;
  new variables `history_rows`, `history_overview`, `history_choices`, `history_links`, `history_filters`,
  `history_suffix`, `history_count`, `history_offset`, `history_limit`, `history_limit_choices`,
  `history_status_names`, `history_edit_languages`, `history_feedback`, `diff_language`.
- The view is a sequence of steps (Back, compare, version limit warning, remove, edit, copy, list); the list, its
  filters and the actions of each version are `expContentHistoryList` (`kernel/classes/expcontenthistorylist.php`),
  which needs no database. Who may open the page and whose content they see is unchanged
  (`History::canOpen()`, `History::canSeeVersionContent()`).

### Added

- Guide: [Versions of an object](../../eng-us/guides/content-history.md). Test: `expContentHistoryListTest` (no database).

## 6 October 2026: My bookmarks

### Updated

- `content/bookmark` is an overview and a list in the look of the section, link list and session pages: figures
  (bookmarks, folders, not in a folder, hidden, no longer available), the folders with their counts beside the list,
  the folder shown with its path, counts and Rename, Move (with Move up and down) and Remove folder, a search over
  names, classes, locations and folders, five orders (your own, name, recently added, type, recently modified) and
  paging (`admininterface.ini [PaginationSettings] ItemsPerPageList_content_bookmark[]`, 25, 50 and 100 by default).
- The bookmarks are grouped by folder, one card each with the class icon, the location, the last change, Hidden or
  Hidden by a parent, and Not found or No access for a bookmark whose item is gone or no longer readable (those were
  left out of the list before, so they could not be removed). View, Edit where allowed, Move up and Move down.
- Bookmarks move between folders by selecting them (Move the selected bookmarks to) or, as an extra, by dragging them
  onto a folder. Remove selected and Remove folder ask first; Remove folder says whether what is inside moves to the
  folder it was in or is removed with it. Add items adds to the folder shown and only items the user may read.
- Every POST answers with a redirect to the same folder, order and search and a notice. The Remove of the context
  menu (`NeedRedirectBack`) goes back only to a page of this site. Every POST name and the template variables
  `view_parameters` and `bookmark_notice` are unchanged; the page works without JavaScript, has 4.5:1 contrast in the
  light and dark modes and holds from 390 pixels and at 200% zoom.

### Added

- Arranging on `content/bookmark`, in Your order: a grip on every bookmark and every folder of the folder list is
  dragged to a new place in its folder (the folder is outlined) or a bookmark is dropped on a folder to move it there
  (labelled "Move into this folder"); the arrow keys on a focused grip move it one place and keep the focus; a
  position field moves a bookmark to any place in its folder, also across pages; Move up and Move down remain without
  JavaScript. One POST saves the new order (`BookmarkOrderButton`, `OrderType`, `OrderFolderID`, `OrderIDs`) through
  `eZContentBrowseBookmarkFolder::setOrder()`, which refuses ids of another folder or user and repeated ids;
  `moveToPosition()` places one entry. In the other orders the page says "Switch to Your order to arrange". Tests:
  `expBookmarkPageTest` BP-14 to BP-17, `BookmarkFoldersTest` BF-14.
- `expBookmarkPage` (`kernel/classes/expbookmarkpage.php`): what the page shows, from plain arrays;
  `eZContentBrowseBookmarkFolder::shift()`: one place up or down within a folder; the button `BookmarkShiftButton`.
- Guide: [Bookmarks](../../eng-us/guides/bookmarks.md). Tests: `expBookmarkPageTest` (no database), `BookmarkFoldersTest`
  BF-13.

## 6 October 2026: the INI settings page (settings/view, settings/edit)

### Updated

- Security: `settings/edit` and the remove action of `settings/view` took the INI file, the siteaccess and the place
  to write from the request as they were, so a POST with `../` in `INIFile`, `SiteAccess` or `SettingPlacement` could
  write an `.append.php` file outside the settings tree. Every name is now checked against what the installation lists
  (the INI files found, `RelatedSiteAccessList`, the active extensions), block and setting names must be ones eZINI
  reads back, read-only settings (`[eZINISettings] ReadonlySettingList`) are refused, and the values shown on the edit
  page are escaped. `eZINI::save()` already refused line breaks and `*/` in values.
- Security: secrets were shown in plain text (the values of six settings named like Password, Token or Key in
  `site.ini` and one in `velocity.ini`), also in the edit field. Their values are now masked everywhere on both pages, never searched and
  never put back into a form; a secret left empty on the edit page keeps its value.
- A saved setting did not take effect: `config.php` switches eZINI's file time check off, and the page never cleared
  the INI cache, so neither PHP-FPM nor Velocity used the new value. Saving and removing now clear the INI cache and say
  which caches were cleared, and whether Velocity must be restarted for the setting.
- The origin of array elements was wrong after a reset (`Name[]`) in a later file: eZINI's placements keep counting
  past a reset, so 1833 of 21542 array elements on the demonstration server were attributed to a file they do not come from. The origin
  is now worked out from the files themselves in eZINI's load order (`expSettingsChain`), checked against eZINI for
  every setting of every file in every siteaccess (41728 settings, no difference).
- The page is redesigned for the admin and admin4 designs: figures for the file, the files read in their order, a card
  per block with a jump list, every setting with its value in effect, its origin, its default when changed and its
  whole override chain (what each file does: sets, empties the array, adds an element, sets a key; in effect or
  overridden), arrays as numbered lists with the origin of each element. The edit page shows the chain before the form,
  explains each place to save, and fills an array's field with an empty first line so saving it as shown replaces the
  array instead of adding the same elements again. Every form field, button and template variable of the old pages is
  kept; the pages work without JavaScript.
- `settings/edit` accepts the setting names eZINI accepts (`Class_identifier`, names with `-`, `@`, `*`), and an empty
  array can be saved.

### Added

- Search across settings (block, name and value) in one file or in every INI file of a siteaccess, a filter for the
  settings changed from the default, and a comparison of a file between two siteaccesses.
- "Not in effect yet": the page compares the files with the values the serving process runs with (PHP-FPM on 443,
  Velocity on 8080) and lists the settings still waiting for an INI cache clear. Files of the load order the server
  cannot read are named.
- `site.ini [SettingsViewSettings]` `MaskedNameList[]`, `UnmaskedNameList[]` and `MaskRevealLastCharacters` (which
  names are secrets, on top of the rule of `exp:ini` and the audit log), and `RestartSettingList[]` (the settings
  Velocity reads only when it starts).
- Classes `expSettingsChain`, `expSettingsSecretRule`, `expSettingsTarget` and `expSettingsPage`
  (`kernel/classes/ini/`). Guide: [The settings page](../../eng-us/guides/settings-page.md). Tests: `expSettingsPageTest`
  (no database).

## 6 October 2026: the cache preloader

### Updated

- **Setup > Preload** (`setup/preload`) is redesigned for the admin and admin4 designs: a status bar with the run
  going on (site, start, a progress bar of pages warmed against the page limit, the address being requested, Stop)
  or the last run, the live output with the figures of the run when it ends, a form that explains its fields, which
  server's caches a run warms (Apache/PHP-FPM and Velocity share the view cache, image aliases and compiled templates;
  Velocity's response cache keeps a page `DefaultTtl` seconds only), the command and a crontab line for the choices
  made, and the last runs with duration, pages, image aliases made and failures with their addresses, status codes
  and linking pages. The page works without JavaScript (the form posts `Action=start|dryrun|stop`, the names
  `setup/preloadjob` takes) and picks a running run up again when it is opened. Every template variable of the
  earlier page is kept.
- One preload runs at a time: a run holds a lock (`var/<var dir>/preload/run.lock`) from the page, the shell and cron
  alike; a second one is refused (409 from `setup/preloadjob`, exit code 2 from the command). Each run writes a status
  file (`<id>.json`) beside its events, so a run whose process is gone is reported as ended instead of after two
  minutes without output, a fatal error is recorded with its message, and the runs of the shell are listed with the
  others. The last 20 runs are kept.
- `bin/php/preload.php` runs the same crawler as the page (`expPreloadJob::execute()`) instead of `wget` and
  `curl`, and writes nothing to `/tmp`. New options: `--max-pages` (default 1000), `--max-depth`, `--images`,
  `--dry-run`, `--target`. The last three lines of its output say how the run ended, the pages warmed and the
  image aliases made.
- The address of a siteaccess is worked out from `MatchOrder` and every match setting, as a request is matched
  (`expPreloadAddress`): a `HostMatchMapItems[]` entry with a third field (`start`, `end`, `part`) and
  `HostMatchMethod` reach the same siteaccess as for a visitor, `URIMatchType=map` and `HostUriMatchMapItems[]` give
  their own prefixes, and an address the matching does not send to the siteaccess is warned about.
- `setup/preloadstream` no longer runs a crawl inside the request (it held a worker for minutes and started a load from
  a plain GET); it answers the events of the run going on or the last one.
- The preloader takes links and images from the page's own markup only: nothing inside `<script>`, `<template>`,
  `<style>` or comments, and no value that holds a quote, a JavaScript or template expression (`' +`, `${`,
  `{{ }}`) or whitespace. Inline scripts that build markup in strings were reported as broken links
  (`/' + esc(p.path) + '`). Velocity's own panel (`/Q/...`) is never requested.
- The preloader warmed only the front page of a site whose `SiteURL` already ends in its siteaccess prefix (uri
  matching, `latest.demo.exponential.earth/site`): the prefix was rightly not added twice, so the site was taken for
  one matched by its host, and every `/site/...` link was dropped as another siteaccess's. The site is now everything
  below the path of `SiteURL` plus the prefix, in every matching mode (host, uri, host_uri, an installation in a
  folder). Phase 1 also warms the section pages, the top level pages the starting pages link to (`/site/fitness`),
  when `URLTranslationKeyword` names none.

### Added

- What to warm: **Also check the images on each page** (`--images`) requests every image the warmed pages show, once,
  and lists the missing ones with the pages that show them. **Image aliases made** counts the alias files the
  rendered pages created during a run.
- **Dry run** on the page and `--dry-run` from the shell: the address, the prefix, the starting pages, the limits and
  the command, without a request.
- `GET setup/preloadjob/status` (the run going on and the last runs), `POST Action=dryrun`.
- `expPreloadAddress`, `expPreloadLock`, `expPreloadHistory`; guide [Preloading caches](../../eng-us/guides/preloading-caches.md).
  Tests: `expPreloadAddressTest`, `expPreloadLockAndHistoryTest` (no database).

## 6 October 2026: classic menus (Setup > Menus (classic))

### Updated

- **Setup > Menus (classic)** (`visual/menuconfig`, until now "Menu management") explains what it is for: the top
  and left menus of sites built with the classic designs (base, ezwebin, the demo and simple designs), chosen per
  siteaccess in `menu.ini [SelectedMenu]`. Sites built with Exponential Layouts set their menus in the layout editor,
  which the page links to.
- The page finds for every siteaccess of `RelatedSiteAccessList`, from the page layout its designs resolve to,
  whether it uses these settings, renders through Layouts, is an administration siteaccess or has a design that
  reads no classic menu. It opens on a siteaccess where they matter, folds the others away with the reason, says when
  none uses them, and asks for a tick before saving for a siteaccess they do nothing for.
- For the siteaccess shown: the current arrangement, the file each value comes from, a warning when
  `settings/override` decides it, the classes the menus list, and the file saving writes to. Each arrangement has a
  preview built from the installation's own pages, says what its menus list (parent page, classes, depth, hidden
  pages) and which template file draws it, and shows the lines saving writes.
- Five recipes with their exact lines and what a visitor sees (top menu only; top plus a left menu for the current
  section; menus limited to folders and landing pages; hiding a page; a different menu per siteaccess), the designs
  of the installation that draw classic menus, and "Use in your templates": copy-ready template examples for a page
  layout, a top menu and a section menu of your own, and where to put them.
- Saving writes only `[SelectedMenu] CurrentMenu`, `TopMenu` and `LeftMenu` into
  `settings/siteaccess/<siteaccess>/menu.ini.append.php`, through the INI editor of `exp:ini` (only the touched
  lines change, the PHP wrapper stays, a copy of the old file goes to `var/backup/ini`, the write is audited), reads
  the file back and puts the old one back when it does not hold what was written. Before, it saved every `menu.ini`
  setting the siteaccess read into that file, which froze the other menu settings for it. Only an arrangement of
  `AvailableMenuArray` is accepted, and only a known siteaccess whose directory lies in `settings/siteaccess`.
- After saving, the INI caches, the compiled page layouts and the template blocks are cleared and the page says that
  no restart of PHP-FPM or Velocity is needed.
- The form keeps `CurrentSiteAccess`, `SelectCurrentSiteAccessButton`, `MenuType` and `StoreButton` and every
  template variable, and works without javascript; new is `/visual/menuconfig/(siteaccess)/<name>`. Redesigned in
  `design/admin` and `design/admin4`, light and dark.
- The Setup and Design menus list the page as "Menus (classic)".

### Added

- `expClassicMenuSiteAccessInspector` and `expClassicMenuSettings` (`kernel/classes/classicmenu`): which siteaccesses
  and designs read the classic menu settings, and reading and writing them; the template examples in
  `kernel/classes/classicmenu/examples`. Tests: `expClassicMenuSettingsTest` (no database).
- Guide: [Classic menus](../../eng-us/guides/classic-menu-settings.md).

## 6 October 2026: publish without notification

### Added

- "Publish without notification" next to the publish button of `content/edit` and of the version preview, shown when
  the new setting `notification.ini [NotificationSettings] PublishWithoutNotification` is `enabled` (default
  `disabled`, the button hidden). The version is published as with the other button, but no notification event is
  made, so the subscribers of its location get no mail. The publish operation takes the new optional parameter
  `notify` (`true` by default), kept in the memento of a publication a workflow holds back, and
  `eZContentOperationCollection::createNotificationEvent()` gives it to the filter `content/notification/create`
  instead of `true`. Buttons in the admin, admin3, admin4 and standard designs, translated into German. Guide:
  [Publish without notification](../../eng-us/features/6.0/publish-without-notification.md), and section 8 of
  [the notifications administrator's guide](../../eng-us/guides/notifications-administrator.md#8-publishing-without-notification).
  Tests: `eZPublishWithoutNotificationTest` (no database), `eZPublishWithoutNotificationLiveTest` (live).
- Policy function `content/publish_without_notification` (no limitations; `content/*` includes it). The button is
  shown to, and counts for, users who have it; for anybody else, and with the setting disabled, a posted
  `PublishNotNotifyButton` or `PreviewPublishNotNotifyButton` publishes with notification and logs a warning.
  `eZContentOperationCollection::canPublishWithoutNotification( $user )` and `publishWithoutNotificationEnabled()`.

### Updated

- The conflict page of `content/edit` (`content/edit_conflict.tpl`, admin, admin4, standard) offers "Publish without
  notification" again when it was pressed before the conflict was found; its Publish data button notified.
- With `content.ini [PublishingSettings] AsynchronousPublishing=enabled`, a publication without notification is
  published at once instead of queued (`sendToPublishingQueue()` takes `notify`): the asynchronous publisher runs the
  operation with the object and the version only, so it notified.
- `createNotificationEvent()` takes `false`, `0` and `"0"` as no, and its debug line says when a version was published
  without notification rather than blaming a listener.

## 6 October 2026: Setup > Caches

### Updated

- **Setup > Caches** (`setup/cache`) is redesigned for the admin and admin4 designs. It lists every cache in groups
  (content and views, templates, INI and settings, images, pages, queries and the server, other) with what it
  holds, its id and tags, its directory relative to the installation, its size and number of files (measured on
  request, `setup/cache/(sizes)/1`, within three seconds), when it was last cleared where the kernel records it,
  and badges for a Velocity restart, Velocity's response cache and a disabled cache. Above it: figures (caches,
  enabled, groups, total size, last expiry), search and a group filter.
- Clear selected, clear a group (`ClearGroupButton`), clear by tag and clear all each name what goes in an in-place
  confirmation (details elements, so it works without javascript; "Clear selected" confirms with javascript), and
  end in a notice with the caches cleared, the time it took, the restart or response cache hint and the command.
- The HTTP cache, query cache, SQL profile, Velocity's response cache (`ClearVelocityCacheButton`, new) and PHP's
  OPcache and APCu are rows of the group "Pages, queries and the server" instead of separate boxes. The static
  cache and the commands that do the same from a shell are folded below.
- OPcache was reported as "not loaded" under PHP-FPM when `opcache_get_status` is in `disable_functions`; it is
  enabled, and Reset OPcache now works there too (`expCacheManager::phpCacheState()`, `resetOPcache()`).
- Every POST name and template variable of the earlier page is kept; the view is thin and its actions are
  methods of their own.

### Added

- `expCacheCatalogue`: the catalogue of the caches (groups, what each holds, masked directories, sizes within a
  time budget, last cleared, Velocity hints, commands); it only describes, clearing stays `expCacheManager`'s.
  Tests: `expCacheCatalogueTest` (no database).
- "Last cleared" on Setup > Caches also reads the audit trail's `system.cache.clear` records (one bounded query of
  the audit index and the end of the newest system file), with who cleared for users who may read the system
  channel: clears through the page, System information, `exp:cache` and, since the next entry, `bin/php/ezcache.php`
  (and so `exp:velocity deploy`) are recorded. Times read
  "just now", "12 minutes ago". Tests: `expCacheCatalogueTest` (CC-07, CC-08).
- `bin/php/ezcache.php`, and so `exp:velocity deploy`, writes one `system.cache.clear` audit record per run that
  clears something (`--clear-all`, `--clear-tag`, `--clear-id`, with `--purge` and `--expiry`), with the ids
  cleared and the operating system user of the shell, through `eZCacheHelper::auditCleared()` and the same
  `eZCache::auditCleared()` that `exp:cache` and the administration use. A failed audit write is a warning on stderr;
  the caches are cleared either way. Setup > Caches names a shell as "from a shell, os:root".
- `expAuditReader::linesBackwards()` lost records under Velocity: through a userland stream wrapper `fread()` returns
  8 KB at most, and the rest of each 64 KB chunk was skipped. It reads until the chunk is complete. Test:
  `expAuditReaderChunkTest`.
- Guide [Caches](../../eng-us/guides/caches.md).

## 6 October 2026: faster access arrays for users with many role assignments

### Updated

- The access array of a user (`eZRole::accessArrayByUserID()`) is built from the policies, limitations and values of
  all the user's roles loaded in three queries, the limitations of a policy are turned into their part once for all
  the assignments of its role, and the parts are merged in one call. A user with 600 subtree assignments (120
  teamrooms × 5 member roles of 20 policies) took 3,550 ms and about 48,600 queries, now 244 ms and 4 queries. The
  array is the same, in the same order (the values of a limitation by value, as read one by one). New setting
  `site.ini [RoleSettings] AccessArrayPrefetch` (`enabled`; `disabled` builds it role by role). Guide:
  [Faster access arrays](../../eng-us/features/6.0/access-array-prefetch.md). Tests: `eZPolicyPrefetchedAccessArrayTest` (no
  database), `eZRoleAccessArrayPrefetchLiveTest` (live).
- Fixed (in the same change, before release): the values of a limitation loaded ahead came in the order of their ids
  instead of by value; while the rows were loaded, a role, policy or limitation outside them was answered as having
  none (a policy then as unlimited) instead of asking the database; an IN () list could pass Oracle's 1000 (now at
  most 500 ids per query); a failed query left policies without their limitations, that is unlimited (the array is
  now built role by role then); a build inside a build dropped the outer one's rows. Compared on the demonstration server with `===`
  against the code before for every user, assignee and throwaway roles of every limitation kind: identical; 177,651
  queries and 14.3 s for 31 subjects before, 104 and 1.6 s now.

## 6 October 2026: the upgrade check

### Added

- `expFileConsistencyReport` (`kernel/private/classes/expfileconsistencyreport.php`): compares the files with
  `share/filelist.md5` and with the manifest an extension carries of its own, with result states (modified,
  missing, unreadable; unlisted, malformed and unordered as notes), counts, the time taken, a CSV and a text report.
  It needs no kernel; `bin/php/checkmanifest.php` and the upgrade check both use it, so they agree on the same tree.
- `expSchemaConsistencyReport` (`kernel/private/classes/expschemaconsistencyreport.php`): the database check by
  table (missing tables, tables not in the schema, changed fields and indexes with their shipped and current
  definition), the SQL per table, what removes something, and engine notes. Display only; nothing is run.
- `php bin/php/checkmanifest.php --all --extensions` also checks every extension's own manifest; `--csv` prints the
  findings as CSV. Guide: [The upgrade check](../../eng-us/guides/upgrade-check.md).
- Test: `expUpgradeCheckReportsTest` (no database): matching, modified, missing, malformed lines, CRLF, unreadable
  files, extension manifests, unlisted and unordered lines, the downloads, and the schema report.

### Updated

- **Setup > Upgrade check** (`setup/systemupgrade`) is redesigned for the admin and admin4 designs, light and dark,
  styled in `stylesheets/modules/setup.css`: the version, the manifest's entries and dates and how many
  active extensions carry a manifest; the two checks side by side; the file result grouped by state with a search,
  a filter by state and by extension and what to do about each kind of finding; the database result table by table
  with its SQL; the time each check took. **Download CSV**, **Download text** and **Download SQL**
  (`DownloadFileReportButton`, `DownloadSchemaReportButton`) send the report as a file. `MD5CheckButton`,
  `DBCheckButton` and the template variables `md5_result`, `failure_reason`, `upgrade_sql` and `mongo_*` are kept;
  new ones are `file_report`, `file_groups`, `schema_report`, `upgrade_info` and `upgrade_check`.
- The database check no longer calls a database consistent while hiding differences, nor inconsistent over noise:
  differences the engine's schema handler writes no SQL for (on SQLite, 19 tables whose `longtext` reads as `text`
  or whose `AUTOINCREMENT` key reads as `int`) are listed as engine notes and do not fail the check.
- A manifest with CRLF line ends is read like one with LF (the command line check reported every line as missing);
  a manifest line naming a path outside the installation is skipped and reported instead of being read; an empty
  or unreadable `share/filelist.md5` fails the check with its own message instead of passing.
- The audit event `system.upgrade.run` counts the findings of the new reports and names the format of a download.
- The SQLite schema reader reads a key declared `integer NOT NULL PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT` as `auto_increment`
  (it read `int NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`: expbookmark_folder and the five expmail_* tables), and the database check on
  SQLite compares only what SQLite can tell apart (`eZSQLiteSchema::normalizeForComparison()`: text and longtext,
  the width of an auto_increment key, a default left out or written as false), writing the SQL from the shipped
  definitions. The 19 engine notes on an SQLite installation are gone; a missing field, another type or length,
  NOT NULL and another default still show. Test: `expSQLiteSchemaReaderTest` (a throwaway SQLite file).

## 6 October 2026: the Extensions page

### Updated

- **Setup > Extensions** (`setup/extensions`) is one list instead of two: the separate Loading order card and the
  paged table with checkboxes are combined into one list of cards, redesigned for the admin and admin4 designs, light
  and dark, styled in `stylesheets/modules/setup.css`. Each card shows the position in `ActiveExtensions`,
  name, title, version, description, license and website, whether a siteaccess activates it as an access extension
  or uses one of its designs, its declared dependencies (requires, uses, extends) and the active extensions that need
  it, whether it is a git checkout, and its problems. Active extensions are moved with up and down buttons (without
  JavaScript too) or by drag and drop and the arrow keys on a grip; extensions are activated and deactivated in the
  same list, an activated one placed after what it requires and before what it extends, otherwise at the end. A
  search, a filter (all, active, inactive, problems) and an order (loading order or name) work in place and without
  JavaScript.
- Nothing is written until **Review changes** shows the extensions activated, deactivated and moved, the risks
  (deactivating an extension another one requires, the only provider of a design a siteaccess uses, `ezformtoken` or
  `ezjscore`; a move that lets another extension's full settings file win, as an installation settings extension
  moved after `ezoracle`; a reorder the declared dependencies undo) and the resulting `ActiveExtensions[]` lines;
  red risks need a confirmation. **Apply changes** writes the new set and order together, clears the INI, template
  override, design and active extension caches, regenerates the autoload arrays and says whether Velocity needs
  `exp:velocity restart`. A list changed elsewhere in the meantime is not written over.
- The page shows where each active extension really loads: with `ExtensionOrdering=enabled` the kernel sorts by the
  declared dependencies, and on a typical installation most extensions load at another position than written (51 of
  55 on the reference installation); the former Loading order card showed only the written order.
- Saving the list edits `settings/override/site.ini.append.php` as text: only the `ActiveExtensions[]` lines change,
  and the comments, the layout and the `<?php /*` wrapper of the file stay. `eZINI::save()` had rewritten the file and
  dropped every comment in it. A comment directly above an extension's line moves with it.
- The posts of the page before the redesign still work (`ActivateExtensionsButton` with `ActiveExtensionList[]`, and
  the JSON `ReorderExtensions`); the template variables of the earlier page are still set. The list is no longer paged,
  and `(sort)` takes `order` or `name` (other values fall back to the loading order).
- Guide: [The Extensions page](../../eng-us/guides/extensions-page.md).

### Added

- `expExtensionCatalogue` (`kernel/classes/expextensioncatalogue.php`): what is known about each extension (metadata,
  dependencies, designs, settings files, git checkout, access extensions and design users per siteaccess) and the
  rows of the list.
- `expExtensionChangePlan` (`kernel/classes/expextensionchangeplan.php`): moving, activating and deactivating, the
  diff, problems, risks and the order the kernel really loads in; `ezpActiveExtensions::replaceInText()` and
  `fromText()` for the file.
- Test: `ExtensionChangePlanTest` (no database): reorder, activate, deactivate, dependency warnings and risks, no
  change gives no write, and a round trip of the settings file that keeps every other byte.

## 6 October 2026: the package pages

### Updated

- **Setup > Packages** (`package/list`) shows every repository by default (it showed only `local`, so the setup
  wizard's own packages were out of sight): figures over all repositories, one tile per repository with its
  packages, size, install states, installer sources and the directories without a package definition it holds,
  and a warning naming every directory whose `package.xml` cannot be read (one such file ended the whole page with
  a fatal error). One card per package with its version, type, install state, summary, repository, maintainers,
  the packages it requires and those that require it, size, files and last change; a search, filters by
  repository, type and state, a sort, and pages whose size follows `[PaginationSettings]` and keep the filter (the
  pager lost the repository). Packages the installer takes as a source (the setup wizard's repository, site
  packages and what they require) are marked.
- The removal of packages asks on a page of its own (`package/confirmremove.tpl`) that says what goes: each
  directory with its files and size, whether it is installed, the packages that require it and why it is an
  installer source; an installer source needs its own tick. It checks `package/remove` for each package's type
  (it checked nothing), removes only what the confirmation offered, refuses a package whose directory contains a
  link (the recursive delete would follow it), and is recorded as `system.package.remove`. Its title read "Remove
  section?".
- A package's page (`package/view/full/<name>[/<repository>]`) leads with its actions (Compare, Install, Download,
  Uninstall, Remove, each by its own policy), then its details, what it carries (classes, content, extensions,
  settings files, files by kind) and its changelog, above the contents browser. A viewed file shows its first
  megabyte. The upload, install, uninstall and creation pages are redesigned the same way, in `design/admin` and
  `design/admin4` (the package section of `stylesheets/modules/structure.css`), light and dark.
- Downloads (`package/export`, `package/viewfile`) are sent in 256 KiB pieces after the page's output buffers are
  closed, answer `Range` with 206 or 416, quote the file name, and end outside any `try`/`catch` (Velocity).
  `package/export` and `package/viewfile` take the repository as a further parameter.
- Fixed: an upload extracted every entry where the archive said: `../` paths, absolute paths, symbolic and hard
  links and devices were written outside the package (zip-slip). `eZPackageUploadInspector` refuses such archives,
  wrong suffixes, files that are not gzip, archives over `package.ini [UploadSettings]` (new) and malformed
  definitions before anything is written, with the reason on the page; `eZPackage::import()` refuses them too, so
  the setup wizard and `ezpm import` are covered. A vendor that gives no directory name is refused (it put the
  package at the top of the storage).
- Fixed: a package name or repository in an address or a form is checked before it is joined to a path
  (`eZPackageRequestGuard`); `../7x/x` made `eZPackage::fetch()` read a `package.xml` outside the repository. An
  unknown view mode is "not found" (it drew an empty page). Install and uninstall check `package/install` for the
  package's type.
- Fixed: `eZPackage::parseDOMTree()` returns false for a definition without a name and reads a missing
  `<ezpublish>` or `<packaging>` as empty (both were fatal errors); `exportToArchive()` writes to a temporary file
  of its own, so a stale `archive.tmp` no longer makes every later export of the user fail.

### Added

- `eZPackageCatalog`, `eZPackageRemovalPlan`, `eZPackageUploadInspector`, `eZPackageDownload` and
  `eZPackageRequestGuard` (kernel classes), with tests that need no database: `eZPackageCatalogTest`,
  `eZPackageUploadInspectorTest` (malformed `package.xml`, path traversal and links in archives),
  `eZPackageRequestGuardTest`, `eZPackageDownloadTest`.
- Guide: [Packages](../../eng-us/guides/packages.md).

## 6 October 2026: roles, policies and unactivated users

### Updated

- The role list (`role/list`) shows each role as a card with its policies, the users and groups it is assigned to,
  and a *Full access* badge for a policy over every module or *Can change roles* for one over the role module. A
  search by name or id (`?q=`) and the orders name, id, number of policies and number of assignments, either way
  round (`(sort)`, `(dir)`); Remove selected asks first, in place.
- The role page (`role/view`) shows every policy as a sentence ("May read content, in section Standard, of the
  classes Article and Folder"), with its module, function and limitations under it, ordered by role order, module
  (grouped), function or limitation; the figures say how many users the role reaches in all, members of its groups
  counted once. Removing assignments asks first.
- The role editor (`role/edit`) lists the policies the same way, says when the draft differs from the saved role,
  asks before the page is left with unsaved changes, and asks before policies are removed. Policies are reordered
  by drag and drop on a grip, by the arrow keys on the grip, by the up and down buttons and by a position field
  (`MovePolicyTo`, `MovePolicyPosition[<id>]`), all as `eZRole::movePolicy()` steps in the draft: Save keeps the
  order, Cancel drops it. Dragging is offered in the role's own order.
- The policy wizard shows its three steps, explains each choice and warns before full access is added; the policy
  editor and the section step of "Assign with limitation" are redesigned the same way. Every value is escaped.
- `role/copy` shows what the copy will hold; the copy is made by the page's form (`CopyRoleButton`), no longer by
  opening the address.
- Unactivated users (`user/unactivated`): the count is of the users the list holds; a search by login, e-mail or name;
  the orders registration date, login, e-mail and name; the age of each registration, old ones marked; Activate,
  Send the activation mail again (new, `ResendButton`) and Remove, each confirmed in place; Remove all
  (`RemoveAllButton`) for every unactivated user or every one the search matches, in batches of 50, each checked
  again before removal, never the anonymous user, `admin` or the user removing, reported as "N removed, M skipped"
  and recorded as `access.user.remove` with the counts.
- All pages in `design/admin` and `design/admin4`, in the look of the redesigned pages (`stylesheets/modules/people.css`),
  light and dark. Every POST name and template variable of the pages is kept.

### Added

- `expRolePolicySentence`, `expRolePage` (kernel/classes/role) and `expUnactivatedUsers` (kernel/classes/user), with
  `expRolePagesTest` (no database). Guide: [Roles, policies and unactivated users](../../eng-us/guides/roles-and-policies.md).

### Fixed

- A policy id posted to the role editor of another role removed that policy from its role at once
  (`eZPolicy::removeByID()` removes any policy); the editor removes only policies of its draft. Likewise role/view
  removed any assignment by id; it removes only assignments of the role shown.
- "Assign with limitation" put the posted limitation type into the address unchecked; only subtree and section are
  taken, and `role/assign` checks the role, the limitation and its value, and returns from the content browser only
  to a page of this site.
- `role/policyedit/<id>` for a policy that does not exist was a fatal error; it is "not available".
- Activate on `user/unactivated` ran the whole register operation again, which for an account without a pending
  registration sent a new activation mail and disabled the account again; it now publishes the user object only.
  Remove on that page removes only users who are still unactivated.
- An unknown sort field on `user/unactivated` raised a notice; an `AssignRole` browse result returned to `role/list`
  was a fatal error.

## 6 October 2026: the account page

### Updated

- The account page (`user/edit`, and `user/edit/<id>` for another user) is redesigned in the look of the other
  redesigned administration pages, in `design/admin` and `design/admin4`, light and dark, with its own style
  (the user section of `stylesheets/modules/people.css`). Until now the administration drew the plain page of `design/standard` (or that of the
  two-factor extension, without Cancel), outside the admin's card.
- An overview card shows the name, login, e-mail, groups, roles, the last sign-in, the number of sign-ins, when the
  password was last changed (from the audit index) and the state of the account (active, disabled or locked).
- Security hints: disabled or locked, failed sign-ins, two-step sign-in off, a password stored with an old method or
  changed more than a year ago, never signed in.
- A card per page to go on to, shown only when the viewer may open it: profile, password, account settings,
  two-step sign-in (when its extension is active), API keys, bookmarks, notifications and e-mail preferences; on
  another user's page API keys and e-mail preferences open the administration pages of that user. Every POST name
  (`EditButton`, `ChangePasswordButton`, `ChangeSettingButton`, `CancelButton`) and template variable is kept;
  Cancel still goes back to the page the editor came from.

### Added

- `expUserAccountOverview` (kernel/classes/user), with `expUserAccountOverviewTest` (no database); the view sets
  `$account_overview`. Guide: [Security and audit](../../eng-us/guides/security-and-audit.md), section 6.

## 6 October 2026: no privilege escalation through roles; activation completes the registration

### Updated

- A user who may edit roles can only grant what their own access covers (`site.ini [RoleSettings]
  PreventPrivilegeEscalation=enabled`, new, default on). The policy wizard takes a policy they do not hold out again;
  Save of the role editor refuses a draft with a new or changed policy they do not hold; the policy editor refuses a
  broader change; `role/copy` refuses to copy, and `role/assign` and `role/view` to assign, a role with a policy beyond
  their access (an assignment's subtree or section limitation narrows the role first). Each refusal names the
  policies. Users with every function of every module may grant anything; `disabled` gives back the old behaviour.
- Activate on `user/unactivated` sends the activated user the "registration approved" mail again, as the original
  flow does (`site.ini [UserSettings] ActivationByAdministratorSendsApprovalMail=enabled`, new; `EmailRegistrationInfo`
  still switches that mail off everywhere).

### Added

- `expRoleGrantCheck` (kernel/classes/role): whether an access array covers a policy, with subtree containment, nodes
  inside subtrees, value limitations, the limitations of roles assigned for a subtree or section, and a policy covered
  by several policies together; `expRoleGrantCheckTest` (no database).
- `expUserActivation` (kernel/classes/user): completes an activation by resuming a pending registration, or by
  publishing and sending the approval mail when none is pending.

### Fixed

- `user/activate` (the link in the activation mail) ran user/register afresh for an account without a pending
  registration: the account was disabled again and mailed a new link. It now completes the registration as above;
  a pending registration resumes as before.
- The publish step of an activation (`eZUserOperationCollection::publishUserContentObject()`) compared the object's
  version with `!== '1'`; a database driver that types its results (SQLite) gives 1, so every activation was cancelled
  there: the user object stayed unpublished and no approval mail went out.

## 6 October 2026: permission reads fail closed, roles leave no policies behind

### Updated

- The access array fails closed. A read that fails while it is built (the roles of a user, the policies of a role,
  the limitations of a policy, the values of a limitation, the role ids of the user cache) denies what it could not
  read: the role grants nothing, the policy is left out. Before, a policy whose limitations could not be read became
  unlimited (`*`). Such an array is used for that request only and not stored in the user cache, and one error is
  logged per build (`eZRole::accessArrayByUserID`). When loading the rows ahead fails, the array is built role by
  role, as before. With a healthy database the array is the same: compared on the demonstration server with `===` for every user,
  assignee and the throwaway roles of every limitation kind. Guide: [Faster access arrays](../../eng-us/features/6.0/access-array-prefetch.md#when-a-read-fails).
  Behaviour change: [October 2026](../../eng-us/bc/6.0/behaviour-changes-2026-10.md). Test: `eZAccessArrayFailClosedTest`
  (no database).

### Fixed

- Removing a role left behind the copies role/policyedit makes of its policies while one is edited (`original_id`
  set); `eZRole::removeThis()` removes every policy of the role.
- Discard in role/edit for a role that was still new removed only its row and left its policies behind; they are
  removed with it.

## 6 October 2026: two-step sign-in

### Added

- A guide for two-step sign-in with sevenx_authentication_2fa, linked from the guides: setting it up as a user,
  turning it on or requiring it as the owner, a lost phone, social login, what protects it
  (`doc/eng-us/guides/two-factor-login.md`).

### Updated

- The admin4 login page shows the social login buttons of sevenx_authentication_2fa where that extension is active
  and enables a provider (`{if ezmodule( 'user2fa/oauth' )}`); nothing changes where it is not.
- sevenx_authentication_2fa (released on its own): the second step, its setup, the social login answers and the 2FA
