## 15 October 2026: publishing queues index_object again

### Fixed
- Publishing a version no longer queues a reindex of the whole subtree. The step `register-search-object` of the
  publish operation had no parameters of its own, so it got all of the operation's parameters by position; since the
  publish operation takes `notify` ([6 October](#6-october-2026-publish-without-notification)), `notify` (true by
  default) landed in `registerSearchObject()`'s `$isMoved`. With `site.ini [SearchSettings] DelayedIndexing=enabled`
  (or `classbased` for a listed class) every publication was queued as `index_moved_node` instead of `index_object`,
  and the indexing cronjob (`indexcontent`) reindexed the object's entire subtree each time. Moving an object to the
  trash removes only its `index_object` entries, so these entries stayed until the cronjob had run them. The step now
  takes `object_id` and `version`, like its neighbours. Without delayed indexing nothing changed: the object is
  indexed at once, and `$isMoved` is read only to choose the pending action. Test: `eZPublishRegisterSearchObjectTest`
  (the step's parameters, the pending action through the operation's parameter mapping with `notify` true, false and
  missing, no other publish step taking more than object and version without parameters of its own, a move still
  telling the search engine it is one).

### Updated
- A publication that changes the object's parent (a location moved in the edit form) is a move for the search
  engine, as content/move is: an engine that implements `addNodeAssignment()` (ezfind) hears of it with `$isMoved`
  true, so with delayed indexing it queues one `index_moved_node` entry and the paths of the moved subtree are
  reindexed; before, only the object was. With the kernel's `ezsearch`, whose index holds no paths, nothing changes
  (one `index_object` entry). `publishNode()` marks the version when it moves the node, and the step
  `register-search-object` uses the mark once.
- The entries `registerSearchObject()` queues in `ezpending_actions` carry the time they were queued (`created`),
  with SQL and MongoDB, so the cronjob `indexcontent` takes the oldest first and a row tells when it came.
- An object that goes to the trash loses its queued `index_moved_node` entries as well as its `index_object` ones.
- What each operation queues with delayed indexing, for both kinds of engine, is a table in
  [After installing, Search](../../eng-us/install/10-after-installing.md#search).
- Test: `eZPendingIndexActionsTest` (without the database): the queued row and its time (SQL and MongoDB), the moved
  publication with both kinds of engine and its mark per version, a real move, a new location, the trash, the
  cronjob, and every method step of every kernel operation that inherits the operation's parameters filling only
  the optional method parameters it is meant to (delete, swap, translationavailable, sort, updatepriority, user
  activation; a loop's children take the loop's `child_parameters`).

### Upgrading
- An installation with delayed indexing that ran the code of 6 to 15 October 2026 may hold `index_moved_node`
  entries made by publications. List them with
  `SELECT id, param FROM ezpending_actions WHERE action = 'index_moved_node' ORDER BY id;`. Those entries carry no
  time (`created` stayed empty until this fix), so the row alone does not tell a publication from a move. Where the
  search engine does not implement `addNodeAssignment()` with `$isMoved` (the kernel's `ezsearch` does not), no
  move queues
  `index_moved_node`: every such entry comes from a publication, and
  `UPDATE ezpending_actions SET action = 'index_object' WHERE action = 'index_moved_node';` turns them into the
  intended reindex of the object alone. Otherwise leave them: the cronjob processes them correctly, at the cost of the
  subtree.

## 15 October 2026: the segmented switch is one pill

### Changed
- admin4's segmented switch (`.a4-tabs.is-segmented`, `components.css`) is one pill instead of three square boxes
  with lines between them: one track (a 1px edge in `--a4-line-strong`, `--a4-soft` inside, `border-radius: 999px`)
  around every choice, each choice a pill inside it. The current choice is tinted in the accent (`--a4-accent-soft`
  over `--a4-card`), with an edge and text in `--a4-accent-text` (4.8:1 light, 6.1:1 dark) instead of the solid
  accent fill. The switch stays as high as the other controls (`--a4-control-h`, 32 px); each choice is 26 px high, round
  icon buttons (`.is-icons`) 34 px wide. Every choice has its own focus ring (`--a4-ring`, 3 px), drawn over its
  neighbours. Under forced colors the track is drawn in `CanvasText`, the current choice in `Highlight`, and the
  focus ring stays outside the choice in `CanvasText`.
- The current choice is found by `aria-pressed="true"` (buttons) or `span.current`, `a.current`, `.is-current`
  (links, with `aria-current`), so a switch of links shows its current state the same way.
- It is seen at the bottom of every left menu (the width of the left column: narrow, medium, wide,
  `parts/left_width_control.tpl`), in the version preview's device switch and in the online editor's engine switch
  (below). The user preferences of the right column are the design's tabs (`ul.tabs`) since the same day. The markup
  of the width switch is unchanged.
- Every segmented switch answers the arrow keys (`admin4.js`): Left and Right move the focus to the previous and
  next choice (round, a disabled one skipped), Home and End to the first and last; each choice keeps its Tab stop
  and is pressed with Enter or Space, Up and Down keep scrolling the page.
- ezoe's engine switch below a rich text field is the same pill: the engines' span carries `a4-tabs is-segmented`,
  so `components.css` draws it in every administration design (track, the engine in use tinted with an accent edge,
  focus ring, forced colors) instead of the joined segments with the engine in use filled in `--a4-accent-strong`;
  `modules/content.css` only places it and keeps the pill's hover. Tests: `AdminContentEditFormsTest`,
  `AdminLegacyControlsTest::testTheSegmentedSwitchesAnswerTheArrowKeys` (a node harness,
  `tests/tests/design/js/segmented_switch.js`).
- `design/admin/stylesheets/components.css` stays a copy of admin4's.
- Test: `AdminLegacyControlsTest::testTheSegmentedSwitchIsOnePill` (track, round choices, height, tint, focus ring,
  forced colors, both copies identical).

### Upgrade notes
- CSS of your own that relied on the joined boxes (`border-radius` of `:first-child` / `:last-child`, the `-1px`
  margin) no longer has anything to adjust. A site design that wants the old filled current choice sets
  `background-color: var(--a4-accent-strong); color: var(--a4-on-accent)` on
  `.a4-tabs.is-segmented > [aria-pressed="true"]` in its own stylesheet. Clear the stylesheet cache after the
  update (`var/<vardir>/cache/public/stylesheets`).

## 15 October 2026: the version preview in the width of a device

### Added
- admin4's version preview (content/versionview: "Preview" from the edit form, the node view and the version history)
  shows the version in the width of a device: a segmented pill iPhone (390 × 844 px) | iPad (820 × 1180) | Desktop
  (1280 × 800) | Large Desktop (1920 × 1080) above the preview, each a button with its icon (`phone`, `tablet`,
  `laptop`, `display`), its name and `aria-pressed`, and a landscape switch (`phone-flip`) for phone and tablet,
  disabled for the desktops. The iframe is drawn at the device's size and scaled down when the content column is
  narrower (never up); beside the switches the size and the scale are shown ("820 × 1180 px, shown at 74 %"), and a
  change of the column's width redraws it. It still asks `content/versionview/.../site_access/<siteaccess>`, so it
  shows the site's own rendering for the siteaccess chosen in the left column.
- The choice is the signed-in user's: the eZPreferences `admin_preview_device` (`phone`, `tablet`, `desktop`,
  `large`) and `admin_preview_landscape` (`1`, `0`), saved through `window.a4Pref` and written on `<html>` by
  `parts/a4_preference_attributes.tpl` like admin4's other remembered choices, so it follows the user to another
  browser; an anonymous page keeps it in the browser (`exp-admin4-preview-device`, `exp-admin4-preview-landscape`),
  and a choice stored there is taken along once. Without one the preview opens as Desktop, scaled to the column
  (before, the site was drawn in the column's own width). The script is part of `admin4.js` (packed with the other
  admin4 scripts); the template holds no script. The arrow keys move between the devices. A stored landscape choice is kept while a desktop is chosen. In a column narrower than 640 px
  the switches show their icons only, the names stay for screen readers and in the titles. Light and dark mode come
  from the tokens; under forced colors the frame has a `CanvasText` edge.
- Without JavaScript the switches stay hidden (`hidden`) and the iframe fills the column at 800 px, as before.
- New Bootstrap Icons 1.13.1 files `tablet.svg`, `laptop.svg`, `display.svg` and `phone-flip.svg` (unchanged), with
  their `.a4-bi-*` mask rules in `components.css` (both copies) and `IconList[]` entries in `design.ini`. The styles
  are in `modules/content.css` (`.a4-content-preview-*`, both copies). New texts are translated to German.
- Test: `AdminPreviewDevicesTest` (the pill and its sizes, the iframe's address and fallback, the script run in node:
  the saved and restored preferences, landscape, the scale, the anonymous fallback; the stylesheet in both copies,
  the icons, the translations); `AdminPreferencesTest` knows the two preferences.

### Upgrade notes
- A site design that overrides `content/view/versionview.tpl` keeps its own preview. The classic `design/admin`
  template is not changed; only its copy of `modules/content.css` carries the rules. Clear the template, stylesheet
  and translation caches after the update.

- Test: `AdminIconsTest::testEveryLinkToTheSiteShowsTheGlobe` (the three templates, the mask rule in both copies,
  the icon list, the unchanged SVG).

### Upgrade notes
- A site design that overrides `page_topmenu.tpl`, admin4l's `admin_theme_switch.tpl` or `node/view/a4_full_body.tpl`
  keeps the arrow until its copy takes `a4-bi-globe2`. Clear the template and stylesheet caches after the update.

## 15 October 2026: SVG images, sanitized on upload

### Added
- The image datatype accepts SVG images (`.svg` and gzip-compressed `.svgz`): `image/svg+xml` is on
  `image.ini [ImageUploadSettings] AllowedMIMETypes[]`, and the new setting `SanitizeSVG=enabled` (the default)
  stores every SVG as the output of the kernel's SVG sanitizer, `expSvgSanitizer` (`lib/ezimage/classes/`). It is
  allow-list based and parses with `DOMDocument` without network, entity substitution or DTD loading: only SVG
  elements and attributes known to be safe are kept; `<script>`, `<foreignObject>`, event attributes, references
  other than internal `#fragments` (except `http(s)`/`mailto` links and embedded PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP images),
  `javascript:` and `data:` URLs, external `url()`, `@import` and `expression()` in CSS, and animations of `href`,
  `style` or event attributes are removed. A DOCTYPE with entities (XXE, billion laughs), UTF-16 input, a file over
  5 MB (also after decompression), 50 000 nodes or 128 levels, and a file in which nothing drawable remains are
  refused. `SanitizeSVG=disabled` refuses SVG, so an SVG is never stored unsanitized. The edit form, the upload
  module and multi-upload, the rich text editor's upload, the AJAX uploader, WebDAV and imports all go through it;
  the package thumbnails stay raster images. Page: [SVG images](../../eng-us/bc/6.0/svg-images.md).
- Every alias of an SVG is the original file, with the width and height from its `width`/`height` attributes or its
  `viewBox`; an SVG is never handed to GD or ImageMagick.
- SVG files are served with `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox; default-src 'none'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'` and
  `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`: by `.htaccess_root`, `content/download`, the cluster gateway and Exponential
  Velocity (0.0.4.53). A virtual host configuration needs the lines of the page above.
- Tests: `expSvgSanitizerTest` (86 harmful SVGs, 15 real icons and drawings that render the same) and
  `expImageSvgTest`.

### Fixed
- `expSvgSanitizer` checks every piece of a `<style>` element a browser can read as a declaration, not only the
  innermost rule blocks: a declaration next to a nested rule, in a last block left open or before the first rule
  could keep an external `url()`. CSS functions that load an image from a plain string (`image-set()`,
  `-webkit-image-set()`, `image()`, `cross-fade()`, `element()`, `src()`) and `attr()` are removed like an external `url()`, and a
  `url()` is a fragment only when it starts with `#` as written, not after decoding character references. An `<a>`
  link must start with `http(s)://` or `mailto:` as written.
- `expSvgSanitizer::isSVGFile()` recognises an SVG behind a DOCTYPE with `>` in its system identifier, a processing
  instruction with `>` in its data, and a prolog longer than 64 kB; the prolog is matched without backtracking, and a prolog the pattern cannot finish counts as SVG, so the sanitizer decides.
- `eZImageAliasHandler` does not store a file as SVG (by its name or the type the browser sent) that the content
  check does not recognise as SVG, so it is never stored unsanitized; this closes the package import's path, which
  has no check before storing. New: `eZImageAliasHandler::isUnsanitizedSVG()`.
- The serving headers match `.svg` and `.svgz` in any case, in `.htaccess_root` and the cluster gateway.
- Labels and titles of an SVG (`aria-*`, `title`, `xlink:title`) keep ordinary text such as "Image (2)" or
  "metadata:": only a script or data URL at the start of a word removes them, since such text is never loaded. The
  test corpus covers further style sheet nesting, escaped and upper case image functions and character references.

### Upgrade notes
- SVG uploads are accepted after the upgrade. To keep refusing them, set `SanitizeSVG=disabled` (or remove
  `image/svg+xml` from `AllowedMIMETypes[]`) in an override. Behind a virtual host instead of `.htaccess`, add the
  serving headers of the page above.

## 15 October 2026: the order of the Dashboard menu

- menu.ini `TrailLinks[]`: links of a left menu drawn after all its other links, in the order named, also after the

  profile (`TrailLinks[]=change_password`, `TrailLinks[]=edit_profile`); Audit trail is the last link of the kernel,

## 15 October 2026: the user menu links to the user's own object

### Added
- The name at the top of admin4's user menu (display name and login) is a link to the full view of the signed-in
  user's own object, its main node's URL alias (`content/view/full/<main node id>` for a node without one), as the right column's "Current user" box links the name.
  It is drawn only when the user's object has a main node and the user may read it (`main_node.can_read`); without
  either, and on edit and browse pages, where the menu's links lead nowhere, the name stays text as before. The link
  is a plain link of the disclosure (no menu role) and the first stop of its arrow keys; it is named by the name and
  login it shows, with "Open your user object" as its tooltip (`title`) and description (`aria-describedby`). Hover
  and focus ring as the menu's other links, light and dark; in forced colors the menu's links are underlined on
  hover. No new icon. The markup is in `parts/a4_header_user.tpl`, so admin4l (`admin_theme_switch.tpl`) has it too.
- The header's cache blocks keep their keys: they are keyed by the page context, the role hash (`$user_hash`) and
  the user's object id, so one user's link is never served to another.
- New string: "Open your user object" (design/admin/pagelayout), ger-DE "Eigenes Benutzerobjekt öffnen" and eng-US.
- Test: `AdminHeaderTest::testTheNameLinksToTheUsersOwnObject` renders the template with the real template engine
  (no database): the link and its target, the text without read access, without a main node and on edit and browse
  pages, two users with their own nodes, the cache keys of both designs, the CSS and the translations.

### Upgrade notes
- A site design that overrides `parts/a4_header_user.tpl` keeps its own copy. Clear the template cache after the
  update. Moving a user's object to another main node, or a change of its section or state that changes who may
  read it, shows in the header once its cache block expires; a change of roles or their limitations shows at once.

## 15 October 2026: visibility and account status at a glance

### Added
- admin4 marks a node's visibility with a small status mark whose shape tells the state as well as its color: a
  green filled disc for visible, a red ring for hidden, a red ring with a bar for hidden by an item above it
  (`is_invisible` without `is_hidden`); a user account that is disabled gets a gray crossed ring and "Disabled", one
  locked after too many failed logins an amber square and "Locked". The marks are one component, `.a4-mark` in
  both copies of `components.css`, at 3:1 or more in light and dark mode, with their shapes in forced colors. They
  show in the node view's header (a badge, replacing the "Hidden" and "Hidden by a parent" badges), before every
  name of the sub-items table (and beside the text of its Visibility column), after hidden nodes and disabled
  accounts in the content tree, and before hidden nodes of the browse list. Page:
  [admin4, Visibility and account status](../../eng-us/features/6.0/admin4-design.md#visibility-and-account-status).
- `expUserAccountStatus` reads the account status of a page of objects with at most two queries; the rows of
  `ezjscnode::subtree` and `expsubitems::rows` carry `is_hidden`, `is_invisible` and, for user accounts,
  `user_status`; the tree menu's answer carries `user_status` for a disabled account; templates have
  `fetch( 'user', 'account_status', hash( 'user_id', ... ) )`. The status is shown to users with `role/read`, like
  the sub-items columns "Account enabled" and "Locked out".
- Tests: `AdminVisibilityStatusTest`, `expUserAccountStatusTest`.

### Updated
- Enabling or disabling a user account (user/setting, activation) marks the user's locations as modified, so the
  content tree, which the browser keeps by `modified_subnode`, shows the change.
- admin4's content tree keeps the "(Hidden)" and "(Hidden by superior)" text after a node's name for screen readers
  (visually hidden instead of `display: none`); the status mark before it is decorative.
- German: every "hidden by a parent" text reads "Versteckt durch übergeordneten Eintrag", next to "Versteckt": the
  node view's badge ("Hidden by a parent", was "Durch übergeordneten Knoten verborgen"), the sub-items table's
  Visibility column and the tree ("Hidden by superior", was "Vom übergeordneten Element versteckt"), the tree's
  "(Hidden by parent)", the bookmarks and "Hidden by its parent". Only the translations changed, not the sources.
- The classic admin design shares the sub-items script, `children_detailed.tpl`, the tree script and template and
  `components.css` with admin4, so it shows the marks too.
- The mark is a class of its own, `.a4-mark`: the pull request named it `.a4-status`, the class of the status with a
  dot and its text (Setup > Cronjobs, Preload, the workflow processes), which would have drawn those as 10 px rings.
  `AdminVisibilityStatusTest` keeps the two apart.

### Upgrade notes
- A design of your own that overrides `node/view/a4_full_body.tpl`, `children_detailed.tpl`,
  `contentstructuremenu/show_content_structure.tpl` or `content/browse_mode_list.tpl` keeps its markup; take the
  include `design:parts/visibility_status.tpl` or the `STATUS` labels to follow. CSS of your own for `.a4-badge-warn`
  (admin4.css) no longer matches anything, and `.a4-subitem-name > a:first-child` is now `a:first-of-type`. Clear the
  template cache after the update.

## 15 October 2026: the new Exponential logo in admin4

### Updated
- admin4 and admin4l show the new logo artwork of the project in the header and on the sign-in page, drawn inline from
  one template, `parts/a4_logo.tpl`: the letters and outlines take the text color of the theme (dark on light,
  light on dark, CanvasText under forced colors), the orange square keeps the brand color `#f15a28` (the `--a4-brand` token, light and dark), and the inner
  square of the back mark takes the surface color. Until now the header painted `images/admin4/logo-light.png` in
  light mode and admin3's white sprite in dark mode, and the sign-in page did the same with its own rules.
- The header logo is 24px high and 152px wide in the 32px box the old image had; the header keeps its heights (56px,
  88px from 576px, 56px from 1280px). Below 576px the header shows the mark alone, in a 36px link like the tools:
  with the word the phone row left its tools 8px apart and narrowed the menu button. From 576px the search field gives the logo its 44px
  (`clamp(6rem, calc(100vw - 454px), 230px)`, was 410px), so the tools keep one row at 576px.
- The logo links, their targets and names are unchanged; the drawings are `aria-hidden`.
- New files: `design/admin4/images/admin4/exponential-logo.svg` (word and mark, cut to the drawing, no background)
  and `exponential-mark.svg` (the mark, square), the same drawing as the template. The `--a4-logo` token is gone.
  `logo-light.png` stays: the old admin design and the kernel's error page still use it.
- The word is drawn as paths, as high as the mark: the E and the two squares share top and baseline (viewBox
  `6 6 380 60`). The raster logos (`images/admin4/logo-light.png`, `images/setup/2/Exponential-logo-title.png`,
  `images/Exponential-logo.gif` in the standard design) are re-rendered from the SVG, sizes adjusted to the word.
  Each is twice the size it is drawn at, so it stays sharp at 200% zoom and on high density screens:
  `logo-light.png` and the setup wizard's `Exponential-logo-title.png` (the same file) 400 x 100 with the drawing at
  its own scale in the middle (7 KB, was 4 KB with the old, smaller word), `Exponential-logo.gif` 420 x 120 for its
  210 x 60 on the standard login page (was 210 x 60). AdminUI's white logo (exp_adminui 1.0.0.26) comes from the same
  drawing. `AdminLogoTest` LG-07 (the E and the l share the squares' top and baseline) and LG-08 (the sizes, half
  the size where they are drawn, the setup copy identical).
- Required packages: exp_adminui ~1.0.0.26 (the white logo at 520 x 82 for its 260 x 41, and the colors of
  xrowextract's pages in AdminUI from the `--a4-muted` token).
- Tests: `AdminLogoTest` (new: one source, the names, plain SVG without script, style or references, the files equal
  to the template, the colors, the header heights); `AdminPhoneMenuTest` PM-03 takes the new search field width;
  `AdminIconsTest` lets the logo be the one inline SVG of admin4's templates.

### Upgrade notes
- A site design that overrides `page_topmenu.tpl`, `loginpagelayout.tpl` or admin4l's `admin_logo.tpl` keeps an empty
  logo link until its copy includes `design:parts/a4_logo.tpl` inside the link. A stylesheet that set `--a4-logo`
  has no effect any more. Clear the template and stylesheet caches after the update.

## 15 October 2026: the environment badge of the administration

admin4 and admin4l can name the installation's environment (development, test, staging, production) in the header,
on the sign-in page and before the page title, so a live site is not taken for a test copy. Off by default.
[Feature](../../eng-us/features/6.0/admin4-design.md#what-you-get) ("Environment badge").

### Added
- `site.ini [AdminEnvironment]`: `Level` (`development`, `test`, `staging`, `production`, or `auto` for the level of
  `EXP_ENV`; empty, the default, shows nothing), `Name`, `ShortName`, `Release`, `ShowVersion` (`disabled`) and
  `ShowInTitle` (`enabled`).
- The template operator `exp_environment_badge()` (`expEnvironmentOperator`, beside `exp_environment()`) and the
  include `parts/a4_environment_badge.tpl`: a pill in the level's color beside the logo (admin4's
  `page_topmenu.tpl`, admin4l's logo block, `loginpagelayout.tpl`). From 768px the name, and for signed-in users the
  release and version; up to 767px, phones included, the short code (never the color alone), the name read out. The header keeps its
  heights (56, 88, 56px). Color tokens `--a4-env-<level>`, `-ink` and `-line` in `admin4.css`, light and dark;
  forced colors keep the text. German and American English for the nine new strings. Test:
  `AdminEnvironmentBadgeTest`.

### Updated
- `design/standard/templates/page_head.tpl` takes an optional `title_prefix` (empty by default); admin4, admin4l and
  admin4's sign-in page pass "[NAME] " when `ShowInTitle` is on.
- The cache-blocks of admin4's and admin4l's head and header and of the sign-in page's head are keyed by the badge,
  so a changed setting shows on the next request. The key list changes, so these blocks are rendered anew once after
  the upgrade.

### Upgrade notes
- Nothing changes without `Level`. To use it, set `[AdminEnvironment] Level` per environment, for example in
  `settings/override/site.ini.prod.append.php` (`EXP_ENV=prod`) or with `Level=auto`. No template cache needs
  clearing; where `EZP_INI_FILEMTIME_CHECK` is off, clear the INI cache.
- A site design that overrides `page_head.tpl` gets no prefix unless it prints `{$title_prefix|wash}` itself.

## 15 October 2026: the environment badge shows on hover

The environment badge of admin4 and admin4l no longer stands in the header at all times: by default it shows only
while a mouse or trackpad pointer rests on the logo, or the logo has the keyboard focus. Touch screens, which cannot
hover, keep it beside the logo. The page title keeps its prefix. [Feature](../../eng-us/features/6.0/admin4-design.md#what-you-get) ("Environment badge").

### Added
- `site.ini [AdminEnvironment] BadgeDisplay`: `hover` (the default) or `always`, the badge beside the logo as before.
  `exp_environment_badge()` returns it as `display`, and it is part of the badge's cache key.

### Updated
- `parts/a4_environment_badge.tpl`: the badge carries `id="a4-env-badge"` and the class `a4-env-hover` or
  `a4-env-always`; the title attribute only with `always`. The logo links of admin4's `page_topmenu.tpl`, admin4l's
  logo block and `loginpagelayout.tpl` name it in `aria-describedby`, so screen readers read the environment with the
  logo.
- `admin4.css`: with `hover` the badge is out of the flow and clipped to nothing; inside
  `@media (hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)` it appears on `:hover` and `:focus-visible` of the logo link, beside the
  logo from 576 to 1279px and on the sign-in page, under it from 1280px and below 576px, and stays while the pointer
  is on it. A 120ms fade, none with reduced motion. The rules that made room for the badge (the logo mark from 576
  to 767px, the search field's width, the short code) apply to `always` only. The header keeps 56, 88 and 56px.
  Tests: `AdminEnvironmentBadgeTest`, `AdminLogoTest`.
- The hover badge meets WCAG 1.4.13: Escape hides it until the pointer or the focus comes back (`admin4.js`, the
  class `a4-env-dismissed`; an open menu's Escape comes first), and the pointer can move onto it. Everything that hides
  it sits inside `@media (hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)`: on a touch screen, a stylus or any pointer that cannot
  hover finely, the badge stands beside the logo as with `always`, with its room and the short code up to 767px, so
  the information never depends on hovering. `AdminEnvironmentBadgeTest` checks that the touch rules equal those of
  `always` and that nothing outside the two pointer media queries names the hover badge.

### Upgrade notes
- To keep the badge in view, set `[AdminEnvironment] BadgeDisplay=always`. A site design that overrides
  `page_topmenu.tpl`, `loginpagelayout.tpl` or admin4l's `admin_logo.tpl` keeps working; add
  `aria-describedby="a4-env-badge"` to its logo link so screen readers keep the environment.

## 15 October 2026: a mega menu for the Setup tab (prototype, opt-in)

### Added
- admin4 (prototype, opt-in): a mega menu for the Setup tab. A small button beside the tab (the tab stays a link)
  opens a card under the header with a short choice of the Setup menu's links in their groups, each with its icon,
  name and description, up to three groups side by side, and "All Setup functions" for the rest. The choice is
  `menu.ini [Leftmenu_setup] MegaMenuLinks[]` (link keys, in order, at most 12 shown; the default names twelve
  frequent ones, among them the AI and Updates links, shown when their extensions are active), with the menu's
  groups and access checks. The links a moved tab brings (`SetupTabs[]`, `GroupSections[]`) are chosen as
  `<group>-<key>` (`audit-console`); a chosen Setup link hidden because its group has the same address takes that
  link (`audit` → the Audit group's Settings). The WAI-ARIA disclosure navigation
  pattern: `aria-expanded`/`aria-controls`, Enter, Space and ArrowDown open it, Tab goes through it, Escape closes
  it and gives the focus back, a click outside closes it. A mouse resting on the tab for 300 ms opens a preview that
  closes 400 ms after leaving; never on touch. From 1280px only; the two-row header and the phone menu are
  unchanged. Switched on with `menu.ini [TopAdminMenu] MegaMenuTabs[]=setup`; empty by default, and then nothing
  changes. [admin4 design, "Mega menu"](../../eng-us/features/6.0/admin4-design.md#mega-menu-prototype-opt-in).
- The view `setup/megamenu`: the Setup menu's groups and links as an HTML fragment without page layout, drawn from
  `menu.ini [Leftmenu_setup]` by `parts/ini_menu.tpl` (new parameter `tiles_layout='mega'`, template
  `parts/a4_mega_menu.tpl`), with the access checks of the menu and the functions of `setup/menu`. The header loads
  it once, when the panel is first opened (`design/admin4/javascript/a4_mega_menu.js`, loaded only while
  `MegaMenuTabs[]` names a tab), so the page grows by the button only.
- Test: `AdminMegaMenuTest` (with a node harness for the script, `tests/tests/design/js/mega_menu.js`).

### Upgrade notes
- Nothing to do while `MegaMenuTabs[]` stays empty. To try it, set `MegaMenuTabs[]=setup` and clear the INI and
  template caches. A site design that overrides `page_topmenu.tpl` or `page_topmenuitem.tpl` for admin4 draws no
  button until it takes the new markup. Opening the panel requests a setup view, which the audit trail records as
  `access.view.sensitive`.

## 15 October 2026: the mega menu for the Users tab, one view for every tab (prototype, opt-in)

### Added
- admin4 (prototype, opt-in): a mega menu for the Users tab, switched on with `menu.ini [TopAdminMenu]
  MegaMenuTabs[]=users`: Users and user groups, Sessions | Roles and policies, Unactivated users | Edit profile,
  Change password, My notification settings, My e-mail preferences, in three groups, and "Go to Users". Its links
  come from `menu.ini [Leftmenu_user_mega]`, a section that gathers the tab's functions without restating a link:
  `GroupSections[]` adds `[Leftmenu_user]` (the tab's left menu, unchanged) and `[Leftmenu_my]` (the user's own
  settings) with their addresses and policies; its own links are the user tree and the sessions. The choice is its
  `MegaMenuLinks[]`. Icons `shield-lock` and `person-slash` (Bootstrap Icons 1.13.1) added to `IconList[]` and both
  `components.css`; `LinkIcons[]`/`LinkDescriptions[]` for the four links of `[Leftmenu_my]` and the two of
  `[Leftmenu_user]` (read only where a menu is drawn as tiles or as a mega menu).
- Any tab can have a mega menu: `[Topmenu_<tab>] MegaMenuTemplate` names the template that draws its menu
  (`design:parts/setup/mega_menu.tpl`, `design:parts/user/mega_menu.tpl`), and `MegaMenuTabs[]` switches it on.
  [admin4 design, "Which tabs take part, and how to add one"](../../eng-us/features/6.0/admin4-design.md#which-tabs-take-part-and-how-to-add-one).
- Tests: `AdminMegaMenuTest` checks the Users section (its added menus, existing links, icons, names and
  descriptions in ger-DE and eng-US), which tabs the view serves and that it serves only a tab the user has (hidden,
  disabled, not shown, not listed), and runs two tabs in the node harness (one panel open at a time).

### Changed
- The fragment view is now `user/megamenu/<tab>` for every tab, in place of `setup/megamenu`: policy `user/login`,
  since the tabs belong to different modules. It answers only for a tab of `MegaMenuTabs[]` with a
  `MegaMenuTemplate` (else not available) that the topmenu operator shows enabled and open to the user, never for
  the anonymous user (else denied); each link is checked as in the left menu.
- The panel's strings name the tab: "Open the %tab menu", "Loading the %tab menu", "The %tab menu could not be
  loaded.", and "Go to %tab" (the tab's own page) for "All Setup functions". One panel is open at a time; a panel
  opened by a click or a key is not replaced by the hover preview of another tab.
- ger-DE: "My notification settings" reads "Meine Benachrichtigungseinstellungen" (was "Meine Benachrichtigungs-
  Einstellungen").

### Changed after the review
- The mega menu shows from 576px, wherever the main menu is one line (the two-row header from 576 to 1279px too,
  which is what a browser at 200% zoom shows on a 1920px screen); it was drawn from 1280px only. Below 576px, in the
  phone menu, the tab stays a plain link.
- admin4l has the mega menus too: the tabs of both designs are drawn by one template, `parts/a4_topmenu_items.tpl`,
  which admin4's `page_topmenu.tpl` and admin4l's tab menu block include; admin4l's block drew no button before.
- Settings that cannot be used are named in the debug report (`parts/a4_mega_menu_check.tpl`): a `MegaMenuTabs[]`
  value without `MegaMenuTemplate`, a `MegaMenuLinks[]` key that is no link of the section (`[Topmenu_<tab>]
  MegaMenuSection`, new: `Leftmenu_setup`, `Leftmenu_user_mega`) or of a section it adds, and more than 12 keys.
  They were left out silently.
- The button's name is "%tab menu" ("Setup menu", ger-DE "Menü „Setup“") instead of "Open the %tab menu": a screen
  reader adds "collapsed" or "expanded", which contradicted "open" while the panel was shown.
- Tests: `AdminMegaMenuTest` checks the width, the one template of both designs, and the debug messages of the check.

### Upgrade notes
- Nothing to do while `MegaMenuTabs[]` stays empty. A site that named `setup` already needs nothing either; to add
  the Users tab, set `MegaMenuTabs[]=users` and clear the INI and template caches. A site that gives another tab a
  mega menu sets `MegaMenuSection` beside its `MegaMenuTemplate` to have its `MegaMenuLinks[]` checked.

## 15 October 2026: the upload page (ezmultiupload 6.1.0)

`ezmultiupload/upload/<node>` is rebuilt in admin4, admin4l and adminui, in the frame of the content action pages
(box header, box content, control bar with the main action first) with the shared controls; the classic
`design/admin` keeps its lighter page. Root `composer.json` (and `composer.ci-phpunit.json`) require
`se7enxweb/ezmultiupload ~6.1.0` and `se7enxweb/exp_adminui ~1.0.0.20`. Guide: the extension's
`doc/upload-page.md`.

### Added

- Added: The upload page's target location shows its path and whether it takes files; *Change location...* opens
  a location picker (breadcrumbs, a filter, the containers with the reason where one cannot take files), a row of
  the queue can have a location of its own, and the last location is the eZPreference `exp_multiupload_location`.
- Added: Folders (where the browser supports it), drag and drop and pasted images; per file a preview, the class
  it becomes (upload.ini), an editable title, the alternative text of an image (required where the class asks,
  L16), a progress bar and the status with the reason; cancel, retry and remove per row and for all; three uploads
  at a time; a summary toast.
- Added: Checks before anything is sent (`ezmultiupload/service`, Action=check): the create permission for the
  class in the location, the type list, the size limit (`MaxFileSize=64M` and PHP's limits) and the alternative
  text; a file with the same name and size already there offers Skip, Rename or Upload anyway.
- Added: SVG images on the upload page: an SVG becomes an Image (sanitized by the kernel), galleries take `*.svg` and
  `*.svgz`, and the preview is an `<img>` of the stored file after the upload.
- Added: `expMultiuploadService::canUploadAt()`, one check whether the current user can upload at a node, for
  links and buttons to the upload page.
- Added: *Keep as drafts* (the kernel's upload without publishing).
- Added: After the upload, the list of what was made with View and Edit, and *Move to...*, *Add location...* and
  *Remove...* (to the trash, after a confirmation) through the kernel's move, addlocation and delete operations and
  their permission checks.
- Added: *Import compressed zip file(s) contents*, off by default (eZPreference `exp_multiupload_zip_import`; off,
  a ZIP file is one File item): the archive is read on the server by the new ZIP import backend
  (`expMultiuploadZipImporter`) and its entries listed, also those of ZIP files inside it, with the folders kept as folder items or flattened (eZPreference `exp_multiupload_zip_mode`). Limits
  in `[ZipSettings]`: depth 3, 1000 entries, 500 MB uncompressed, a compression ratio of 100 (ZIP bombs); unsafe
  paths, links and devices are skipped, encrypted archives refused, and the archive is unpacked in a private
  directory of the request that is removed afterwards.

### Updated

- Updated: The content tree's "Upload multiple files" works on every container: the page used to answer with the
  kernel's "object is not available" error on any location whose class was not `folder` or `gallery`
  (`AvailableClasses[]`), which on the media site's installations is nearly every node (Fit & Healthy is an
  `ng_frontpage`). New `[MultiUploadSettings] AnyContainer=enabled`: every container the user may create content
  in takes uploads; a location that cannot shows the page with the reason and offers the nearest one above.
  Upgrade note: `AnyContainer=disabled` keeps the 6.0 rule.
- Updated: Every request of the page that changes something is a POST with the session's form token, checked again
  by the extension (constant-time) and never accepted from another site; node ids are positive integers only.
- Updated: The Admin UI header's search button and user menu have names (exp_adminui 1.0.0.20).
- Tests: `tests/tests/extension/ezmultiupload/` (testsuite `ezmultiupload`, no database): the input checks, the
  token, the permission pre-check, the duplicate check (`expMultiuploadServiceTest`) and ZIP files made by the test:
  nesting and its limit, traversal, links, a ZIP bomb, the entry and size limits, encryption, names that are not
  UTF-8, mixed types and both folder modes (`expMultiuploadZipTest`).

## 15 October 2026: sub items reordered by drag and drop

Where a node sorts its children by priority and the user may change the priorities, admin4's sub items table can be
reordered by dragging a row, with the keyboard as well, and the new order is saved as the children's priorities.
[Feature](../../eng-us/features/6.0/admin4-design.md#reordering-sub-items-by-drag-and-drop).

### Added
- admin4: a column of grips (`grip-vertical`) in the sub items table (`javascript/subitems_priority_drag.js`, loaded
  by `children.tpl`), only where the node is sorted by priority and `$node.can_edit` (the condition of the classic
  priority fields, `confObj.priorityDrag` in `children_detailed.tpl`), while the table is sorted as the node sorts and
  not filtered. Pointer events for the mouse, touch and pen (no HTML5 drag and drop, no library): the row is its own
  placeholder, a ghost with the name follows the pointer, the window scrolls near its top and bottom edge, Escape
  cancels. Keyboard: Space or Enter picks a row up, the arrow keys (Home, End) move it, Space or Enter drops it,
  Escape or leaving the grip cancels; Alt+Up and Alt+Down move it by one place at once. Every step is announced
  ("Position 3 of 23", "Moved to position 4 of 23"), translated in ger-DE and eng-US.
- Saving right after the drop through `ezjscnode::updatepriority` (the inline priority editor's server function,
  with the page's form token): the page's own priorities are given out again in the new order, in the node's sort
  direction, so no row moves to another page; equal priorities on the page, or one shared with the neighbouring row
  of the page before or after, become distinct values between those neighbours (in steps of 10 where there is room);
  without room, all children are numbered again in steps of 10. A toast says "Order saved" with Undo (it stays 10
  seconds), which puts the previous priorities back. A failed save puts the rows back and says why.
- `ezjscnode::updatepriority` takes the compact lists `PriorityList` and `ExpectedPriorityList`
  ("node_id:priority,...", one field for a page of any size, far below `max_input_vars`) and `Renumber=1` with
  `PageOrder` and `PageOffset`. With `ExpectedPriorityList` nothing is written when one of those children has another
  priority now or is no longer a child (`array( 'conflict' => true, 'nodes' => ... )`); the table then loads again
  and says that someone else changed the order. Otherwise the answer lists the changed priorities
  (`'changed' => array( node_id => array( old, new ) )`). `ExpectedPriorityList` is required with the new fields
  and must name every node they write (a node of `PriorityList` or `PageOrder` without its shown priority is
  refused); they are refused for a node that does not sort its children by priority. Permission as the classic
  form: the parent's `canEdit()`; `ContentNodeID` must be a positive integer. The check and the write run in one
  transaction, rolled back together on an error, the rows read with `FOR UPDATE` on MySQL, PostgreSQL and Oracle; the
  write is the kernel's (the `content/updatepriority` operation with its triggers, else
  `eZContentOperationCollection::updatePriority()`), and a workflow that stops or defers it is answered as an error,
  not as saved. Posted `Priority[]`/`PriorityID[]` work as before.
- `window.a4Toast( message, kind, opener, delay )`: the time a toast stays, for a toast with an action button
  (`.a4-toast-action`).
- Tests: `AdminSubitemsPriorityDragTest` (its behaviour runs in node, `tests/tests/design/js/priority_drag.js`) and
  `ezjscServerFunctionsNodePriorityTest`.

### Updated
- The sub items lists sorted by priority (`ezjscnode::subtree`, `expsubitems::rows` through
  `expSubitemsColumnRegistry::sortFor()`) order equal priorities by node id, in the same direction, so a page shows the
  same rows each time and the renumbering keeps the order the list showed.
- The priority cells stay editable. A drag does not start while a priority is being edited with a changed value
  (a toast asks to save it with Enter or cancel it with Escape first, and the editor keeps the focus) or while such a
  change is being saved; no cell can be edited while a new order is being saved.

## 15 October 2026: language badges show the state of a translation

### Added
- admin4's language badges (DE, EN) show the state of each language for the object by their shape, not by a color
  per language and without the status colors: the main language filled, a translation outlined, a language of the
  site the object does not have dashed with pale text, a translation last edited before the main language with a
  small mark. The code stays the text; the state is in the title and the accessible name ("German – main language",
  "French – missing", "Spanish – outdated, older than German"), in ger-DE and eng-US. Under forced colors the shapes
  stay. Shown in the node view's meta line (every language of the site, the missing ones as a "Translate into ..."
  link, `content/edit/<object>/f/<language>`, for a user who may create that translation) and in the Translations
  tab (the state of each translation, the missing ones under the table). The sub-items table's Translations column
  shows main, present and, on a site with up to six languages, the missing ones (as text); the outdated mark stays
  on the node. Include `design:parts/language_state_badge.tpl`, the fetch function
  `fetch( 'content', 'language_states', hash( 'object', $object ) )` and the class `expContentLanguageStates`.
- A translation is outdated when the newest published or archived version edited in it is older than the newest
  edited in the main language; both must exist, so an object whose languages came in one version (an import, a copy)
  or whose older versions were removed is never marked. One grouped query reads all objects asked for.
- The node JSON of the sub-items table (`ezjscAjaxContent::nodeEncode()`) carries `initial_language`, the locale of
  the object's main language.
- Tests: `AdminLanguageStatesTest` (states, outdated, the list limit, one query for a page of objects, the texts, no
  color per language, forced colors, the sub-items formatter run in node), a contrast pair in
  `AdminTokenContrastTest`.

### Updated
- A plain language badge is outlined (`--a4-field-line`, 3:1) on no fill, so it reads as "this language exists"
  next to the filled main language; the badge of the language shown keeps its accent edge, tint and underline.
- A badge in front of a language's name in a table cell (the Translations tab) keeps a gap to the name.
- The node view's copy buttons (Node, Object, Remote ID) are 24px targets at every pointer, not only on touch
  screens: the language badges of the meta line are 24px high and wrap beside them on a phone, where the shorter
  buttons failed axe's target-size check.
- A `fetch()` in a compiled template checks that the function's class and method can be loaded before it calls them,
  as the interpreted `fetch()` (`eZModuleFunctionInfo::executeClassMethod()`) always did. A template compiled by a
  process that knew a new kernel class could run in a PHP-FPM or Velocity worker whose autoload did not know it yet,
  and the page ended in a fatal "Class not found" (it happened once on alpha with `expContentLanguageStates`, until
  the workers were reloaded). Such a worker now renders no result and writes an error to the debug output. A new
  kernel class is still deployed with its autoload (`exp:velocity deploy --kernel`) before a template uses it.

## 15 October 2026: an Upload button in the node view

### Added
- The node view's actions have an Upload button after Edit (design/admin and admin4, so also admin4l; first in the
  "..." menu of exp_adminui). It opens ezmultiupload's upload page for the node (`ezmultiupload/upload/<node id>`)
  and shows only where the current user can upload there: the `ezmultiupload/upload` module is enabled and the new
  fetch function `fetch( 'ezmultiupload', 'can_upload', hash( 'node_id', ... ) )` says yes (the module policy, read
  and create access at the node, a container or a configured upload class or node; ezmultiupload 6.1). Drafts and
  the edit pages never show it. An `.a4-btn` with the Bootstrap Icon `.a4-bi-upload` (new in `components.css`),
  "Upload" / "Hochladen". Guide: doc/eng-us/bc/6.0/admin4-styling.md, "The node view's Upload button".

## 15 October 2026: unreferenced objects and broken relations

### Added
- The kernel module `contentcheck` with two content checks for the administration. Page:
  [The content checks](../../eng-us/features/6.0/content-checks.md).
- **Unreferenced objects** (`contentcheck/unreferenced/<node id>`): the published objects below a node that no other
  object refers to, filtered by class, sorted by name, class, date or id, and paged, with name, class, file size,
  last modified and location. A reference is a row of `ezcontentobject_link` of any type (object relation, relation
  field, rich text embed and link) from a published, draft, pending or queued version of an object that is not in
  the trash; relations from trashed objects, old versions and the object itself do not count. One `COUNT` and one
  page query with `NOT EXISTS` and the `content/read` condition in the SQL; 1 to 1.7 s for 100,000 hits on MariaDB.
- Ticked unreferenced objects go to the trash after a confirmation that lists what happens to each (POST, the form
  tokens and a confirmation token bound to the objects shown). Each object is checked again (still unreferenced,
  `content/remove` on every location, no sub-items, the class allows the trash, no pending version, no content job)
  and moved by the kernel's removal, recorded as `content.node.remove.trash`; at most `MaxTrashBatch` (50) at a time.
- **Broken relations** (`contentcheck/brokenrelations/<node id>`): visible, published content whose published version
  relates to, links to or embeds an object or node that is deleted, in the trash, hidden, never published or without
  location: the relations in `ezcontentobject_link` and the `object_id` and `node_id` of links and embeds in the text
  of `ezxmltext` fields (`XmlDatatypes[]`), which still name a deleted target. Source, field and language, target and
  its state, and an Edit button where the user may edit; filters for the subtree, excluded subtrees, the states and
  the kinds of reference. Read only. The sources are checked by object id in chunks, a page goes on after the last
  object checked (20 to 30 ms for a page of 50 findings).
- Policies `contentcheck/unreferenced`, `contentcheck/trash` and `contentcheck/brokenrelations` (Administrator only in
  a new installation); the checks answer on administration siteaccesses only (`contentcheck.ini`). What a user may
  not read is left out of every list, count and page in the SQL; a target the user may not read is "not available",
  without id or state, and a deleted, trashed or hidden target the user may read is shown by its id and state only.
- Setup menu and overview: **Unreferenced objects** (icon `file-earmark-break`) and **Broken relations** (icon
  `exclamation-diamond`) in the group Content model, after the content finder. Context menu of the content tree and
  the sub-items: **Advanced > Unreferenced objects in subtree** and **Broken relations in subtree**, for users with
  the policy. Translations eng-US and ger-DE.
- Tests: `expContentCheckQueryTest` (no database) and `expContentCheckSandboxTest` (a throwaway installation only),
  suite `kernel-contentcheck`.

### Updated (review before the merge)
- **Unreferenced objects lists assets only, judged against everything that can use them.** A published page, a
  folder, a user or a settings object is reached through the tree whether or not anything links to it; the first
  version listed every published object without a relation row, which on a demo installation was 647 of about 800,
  including landing pages, users and the site's settings object, all offered to the trash. The list now covers the
  classes of `contentcheck.ini [UnreferencedSettings] AssetClassList[]` (image, file and the media classes), only
  when every location's parent is a storage folder (`StorageParentClassList[]`, `folder`): below a gallery, an
  article or an event the parent's template shows the asset.
- **The reference index** (`expContentCheckReferences`) adds every source a relation row misses: relation field data
  (`ezobjectrelation`, `ezobjectrelationlist`, `ngenhancedlink`, which never writes a relation row); ids, schemes,
  storage paths and internal paths in the text of any field (54 images of the demo installation were embedded in
  rich text with no relation row, and the first version would have offered them for the trash); the `ezurl` table,
  custom URL aliases and wildcards; the layouts (collection items by node or object, dynamic collections by parent
  node and class, block parameters including `exp-remote-location://` links, rule targets); node ids in every
  settings file; literal ids in every design's templates; role and policy limitations by node or subtree; the shop,
  collected information, pending actions, bookmarks, subtree notifications, draft placements and the newsletter
  (configurable table columns, a missing table skipped) and the newsletter's article pools. The index is kept for
  `IndexTTL` seconds; the move to the trash always builds it again; a source that cannot be read lists nothing.
  Published objects without any location are counted.
- **Broken relations** also read the data of relation fields (a target named where no relation row was written),
  show what to do about each finding, and list on the first page the layouts whose collection items, dynamic
  collection parents, block links or rule targets name a node or object that is deleted, in the trash or hidden.
- **On the command line**, report only: `./console exp:content-check --build-index | --unreferenced | --broken |
  --why=<ids> [--node=<id>] [--json]` (`bin/php/contentcheck.php`), and the cronjob part `contentcheck` for
  installations that set `BuildOnRequest=false`.
- **Ids of imported content** are resolved as the site resolves them: `contentcheck.ini [ReferenceSettings]
  ImportedIdRemotePatterns[]` and `ImportedIdObjectPatterns[]` (the media site demo resolves enhanced link and layout
  component ids as remote id `media-o-<id+776>`, `media-o-<id>` or object `<id+776>`; on the demo installation all
  23 enhanced links reported as broken resolve that way).
- A dynamic collection of the **current location** counts below the nodes its layout's rules target (children of a
  node target, everything below a subtree target, anywhere below a parent of a class condition's classes for other
  rules and for shared layouts), not anywhere: one gallery block had kept every image from ever being listed.
- Tests: `expContentCheckReferencesTest` (no database): every reference source's extractor, the judging, the asset
  classes in the SQL, relation fields and hints.

### Upgrade notes
- Nothing to do: no database change. Give the three policies to the roles of the people who tidy up content.

## 15 October 2026: removing and hiding check the reverse relations

### Added
- `content.ini [RemoveSettings] ReverseRelationCheck=disabled|warn|block` (default `warn`): the confirmation page of
  content/removeobject lists, per selected item, the objects that point at what the removal takes away: the
  referring object with a link, its class, the kind of reference (object relation, embed or link in rich text,
  relation field with the field's name) and its target. It replaces the general hint to look at Reverse related for
  subtree. With `block` the OK button is disabled while references exist, and the view refuses a posted
  confirmation, `HideRemoveConfirmation=true` and a removal as a content job on its own as well.
- `content.ini [HideSettings] ReverseRelationCheck=disabled|warn` (default `warn`): with `warn`, hiding a node
  that other objects point at, or whose object has further locations, takes a confirmation page with the list
  first: from the context menu, the Locations tab, a link to content/hide and "Hide selected" of the sub items list.
- Only objects the current user may read are named. References from other objects, and further locations the user
  may not read, are counted per item and never named ("2 references you cannot see"). `block` counts them all the
  same.
- `content.ini [ReverseRelationSettings] ProviderClassList[]`: classes that report references kept outside
  `ezcontentobject_link`. explayouts adds `expLayoutsReverseRelationProvider`: blocks of published layouts whose
  collection names a node inside the removed or hidden subtrees, listed as "Layouts block" with a link to the
  layout.
- What counts: rows of `ezcontentobject_link` from the current version of a published object, of any kind, to an
  object whose every location lies inside the selected subtrees, from an object that stays. Page:
  [Removing and hiding: the check of reverse relations](../../eng-us/features/6.0/reverse-relation-check.md).
- Tests: `expReverseRelationCheckTest`, `AdminReverseRelationCheckTest`. German and American English for the new
  strings.

### Updated
- content/hide draws both of its confirmation pages with one method, `confirmationPage()`; `expContentHideReturnTest`
  counts them there.
- With `block`, removing the last locations of an object on the Locations tab goes through content/removeobject
  (listed and refused there), and a WebDAV delete is refused. The standard design's removal page shows the list.
- The selected nodes are fetched in one query; class and field names once per request.

### Upgrade notes
- Hiding content that others point at, or an object with further locations, shows the list first from now on
  (`[HideSettings] ReverseRelationCheck=warn`); set `disabled` for the hide of earlier releases.
- The removal page shows the list from now on (`warn`); selecting every location of an object that others point at
  now shows the confirmation page instead of removing it at once. Set `ReverseRelationCheck=disabled` under
  `[RemoveSettings]` for the page of earlier releases, or `block` to refuse removals while references exist.

## 15 October 2026: language badges show the language, not the region

### Updated
- A language badge (`design:parts/language_badge.tpl`, admin4 and the classic admin design) always shows the
  language part of the locale's language tag: `eng-GB` and `eng-US` are both `EN`, also when the installation has
  both. Before, two content languages of the same language showed the region as well (`EN-GB`, `EN-US`), so a row
  of badges mixed `DE` with `EN-GB`. The full name (English (United Kingdom), English (American)) stays the badge's
  title and accessible name, or is written next to it, so a tooltip and a screen reader still tell the two apart.
  The include no longer fetches `content/translation_list` to count the languages: one query less per badge. The
  sub-items table's Translations column gets its codes from the same include and follows. Guide:
  [the admin4 design: languages](../../eng-us/features/6.0/admin4-design.md#languages).
- Test: `AdminLanguageBadgeTest` (the badge is the language part of the tag, the include fetches no translation
  list; every locale file of `share/locale/` gives a two or three letter code, every `esl-*` locale `ES`, so a
  locale added later such as `esl-AR` follows).
- Two regions of one language on one object keep `EN` on both badges: the name tells them apart, written next to
  the badge in the Translations tab and the edit page's language choice, as each badge's tooltip elsewhere, and in
  the accessible name together with the state ("English (United Kingdom) – outdated, older than English (American)").
  [The admin4 design: languages](../../eng-us/features/6.0/admin4-design.md#languages).

## 15 October 2026: checking user accounts

### Added
- **Users > Check user accounts** (`user/hygiene`, also a tile of the Setup overview under *Users and messages*):
  every user account, or those below a user group, filtered by never signed in, not seen for N days, disabled,
  locked (more failed sign-ins than `MaxNumberOfFailedLogin`), orphaned (no user object, never published, no
  location) or a shared e-mail address, each filter with its count; a search, the order of the columns and the page
  size. Each row shows the states, the last visit, the number of sign-ins, the groups and the roles (also those of
  the groups above). Disable, enable and unlock the ticked accounts after a confirmation with the counts; every
  account is decided again when it is changed, never the anonymous user, the user creator, the administrator
  account (id 14, login `admin`) or the user who acts, and only with `content/edit` on the account's object. The
  changes go through the kernel's paths and their audit events (`access.user.disable`, `access.user.enable`,
  `access.user.unlock`). Export the filtered list as CSV (UTF-8 with a byte order mark, formula cells made text,
  recorded as `data.export.csv`). Nothing here removes an account: a user object never goes to the trash.
  New: the function `user/hygiene`, `expUserHygiene`, menu.ini `user_hygiene` in `[Leftmenu_user]` and
  `[Leftmenu_setup]` (icon `person-badge`, new in `IconList[]`), offered in the Users tab's mega menu
  (`MegaMenuLinks[]=access-user_hygiene`), [Check user accounts](../../eng-us/features/6.0/user-hygiene.md). Test:
  `expUserHygieneTest`.

### Updated
- **Check user accounts** has the filter **Shared login** (any case, not counting accounts in the trash; the
  duplicates LoveStack L04's `exp:user-login-check` reports, to resolve before `--fix-index`), a badge on a shared
  login, and one line under the tabs that says exactly what the current filter holds. The anonymous user is no
  longer counted as *Never signed in* (it cannot sign in). The page counts the published user objects that have no
  account row (the other side of *Orphaned*, which no action can touch) and says how many there are. Test:
  `expUserHygieneTest` UH-09.

### Upgrading
- The page is only for roles with `user/hygiene` (*Administrator* has it through `*/*`); e-mail addresses on it and
  the filter of shared addresses need `role/assign`, as the E-mail column of the sub-items table.

## 15 October 2026: an Export menu in the content tree

### Added
- The Advanced submenu of the content tree's menu and of the sub-items "…" menu has an **Export** submenu. Each
  entry opens a form filled in for the item; the export is started there, never from the menu: the item, or its
  subtree, as a content package (with or without class definitions, with all versions, or the package wizard
  itself), and, with xrowextract 2.5.11, the children or the subtree as CSV, the subtree of one class (a list of the
  classes below the item, read when it opens), every attribute or the migration columns. Export is shown only to a
  user with package/create and package/export (the content object wizard) or xrowextract/csv; an Editor sees no
  change. Guide: [The Export menu of the content tree](../../eng-us/bc/6.0/content-export-menu.md).
- Entry parameters of `package/create`: `(creator)/ezcontentobject/(node)/<id>[/(subtree)/1][/(classes)/0|1]`
  `[/(versions)/current|all]` start the content object wizard with the node or subtree selected and the second step
  preset; only a node the user can read and a user who may use the wizard and download its package.
- `fetch( 'package', 'content_export_access' )`, and admininterface.ini `[AdditionalMenuSettings]`
  `ExportMenuTemplateArray[]` and `ExportSubMenuTemplateArray[]` for an extension's export entries.
- ezpopupmenu.js: `menuArray[id]['onshow']`, for a submenu whose items are filled in when it opens.

### Updated
- ezpopupmenu.js places a nested submenu inside the window: beside its parent on the side with room, moved inside
  the window when neither side has room (960px at 200 % zoom, phones), up from the bottom edge, scrolling when
  taller than the window. adminui's copy (exp_adminui 1.0.0.22) is now admin4's, with the keyboard pattern of #165.
- Required packages: xrowextract ~2.5.11 (the CSV form's entry parameters and the class list of the Export menu); the
  context menu script of exp_adminui 1.0.0.22 is in the required ~1.0.0.23.

## 15 October 2026: deactivate and move user accounts instead of removing them

### Added
- **Deactivate and move** and **Reactivate and move back** (opt-in): instead of removing a user account (users
  never go to the trash, so removing one is final), it is disabled and all its locations are brought together in
  one hidden user group without roles; the locations it had are remembered, and reactivating it enables it and
  moves it back (a former location that is gone is left out and counted; a gone main location falls back to
  `[UserSettings] DefaultUserPlacement`). On a user's page, in the context menu (*Advanced*) and as bulk actions on
  Users > Check user accounts, each after a confirmation that lists the accounts, their groups and where they go.
  The group is `[UserSettings] DeactivatedUserGroupNodeID` or the user group with the remote id
  `exp_deactivated_users_group`, created on first use by a user who may, or beforehand by
  `bin/php/ezcreatedeactivatedusergroup.php -s <siteaccess> [--commit]`. Every step goes through the kernel's
  operations (`user/setsettings`, `content/move`, `content/removelocation`, `content/addlocation`) in one
  transaction; recorded as `access.user.deactivate_move`, `access.user.reactivate_move` and
  `access.user.deactivated_group.create` (the audit catalogue has 148 names). The remembered locations are one
  `ezsite_data` row per account (`exp_user_deactivation_<id>`), so no database update is needed.
  New: the function and view `user/deactivate`, `fetch( 'user', 'deactivation_offers', ... )`, `expUserDeactivation`,
  [Deactivate and move](../../eng-us/features/6.0/user-hygiene.md#deactivate-and-move). Tests: `expUserDeactivationTest`,
  `expUserDeactivationLiveTest`.

### Updated
- The group of deactivated users keeps no roles: **Assign** on a role (role/view and role/assign) leaves it out of
  the chosen users and groups and says so on the role's page, and Deactivate and move refuses while a role is
  assigned to the group some other way. Check user accounts no longer depends on the new class being loaded (a
  process started before the deploy). Tests UD-07 and UL-08; texts in eng-US and ger-DE.

### Upgrading
- Nothing changes until you switch it on: `site.ini [UserSettings] DeactivateAndMove=disabled` is the default, and
  the installer neither switches it on nor creates the group. To use it:
  1. optionally create the group beforehand: `php bin/php/ezcreatedeactivatedusergroup.php -s <admin siteaccess>`
     (dry run), then the same with `--commit`; it prints the node id;
  2. set `[UserSettings] DeactivateAndMove=enabled` (and, only to use a group of your own,
     `DeactivatedUserGroupNodeID=<node id>`) in `settings/override/site.ini.append.php` or the admin siteaccess,
     then clear the caches (`php bin/php/ezcache.php --clear-all`, and `var/cache/ini/`);
  3. give the function `user/deactivate` to the roles that retire accounts (*Administrator* has it through `*/*`);
     they also need `content/edit`, `content/move` and `content/remove` on the accounts, and `content/create` in
     the group (see the feature page).

## 15 October 2026: the left column's size is a width only

### Fixed
- admin4: the left column's sizes (the small, medium and large switch, and a width dragged by hand) change its width
  only. The medium and large sizes also made its text larger (16px and 20.9px instead of 13.2px), a rule taken over
  from admin3, where the sizes were font sizes. The column's text now keeps the body's size (`--a4-text-body`, 13px
  up to 14px on very wide windows) at every width, as the right column; headings, the content tree and the group
  titles keep their sizes relative to it. `--left-sidebar-font-size` is gone from admin4's `pagelayout.css`;
  `--left-sidebar-width` is unchanged. admin3 keeps its own rule.
- Test: `AdminLeftColumnFontTest` (testsuite `design`): no `html[data-a4-left-size]` rule of admin4 sets a font size,
  and `.sidebar.left` inherits the body's.

### Upgrade notes
- A stylesheet of your own that set `--left-sidebar-font-size` for admin4 has no effect any more; set `font-size` on
  `.sidebar.left` instead.

## 15 October 2026: an Import/Export button in the node view

### Added
- The node view's actions have an **Import/Export** button after Upload and before Move (admin4, admin4l, admin;
  exp_adminui 1.0.0.24: an entry of the toolbar's "…" menu). Its menu offers the export entries of the context
  menus' Advanced > Export and, with xrowextract 2.5.12, Import into this item (only on a container below which the
  user may create content), the export and import jobs, the export history and the schedules, each for its policy.
  It is shown only for a published object with an entry for the user: an Editor sees no change, a user with
  xrowextract/csv only sees the CSV entries. A menu button (Enter or Space open it, Escape returns to it) with the
  Bootstrap icon arrow-down-up, which is now bundled (`.a4-bi-arrow-down-up`, design.ini `IconList[]`).
  Guide: [The Export menu of the content tree](../../eng-us/bc/6.0/content-export-menu.md).
- `popupmenu/popup_export_items.tpl`, one definition of the export entries for both menus, and admininterface.ini
  `ImportExportMenuTemplateArray[]` for an extension's import and job entries; ezpopupmenu.js's element option
  `shown_if` leaves out an entry that does not apply to the item.

### Updated
- Required packages: xrowextract ~2.5.12 (`xrowextract/import/(parent)/<node id>`, the import and job entries),
  exp_adminui ~1.0.0.24.

## 15 October 2026: the phone menu fits the screen and is a grid of tiles

### Fixed
- admin4: the open phone menu (below 576px) was cut off at the bottom in Safari and Chrome on iOS. The panel was at
  most `100vh` high and started at the window's top, with the header's height as padding inside it. On iOS `100vh`
  is the height with the browser's toolbars hidden; with them shown (as when the menu is opened) the panel's end lay
  under the bottom toolbar, and scrolling the panel could not bring its last items up: with the search scope popup
  open at a visible height of 664px the panel ended 60px below the window (measured in an emulated 390 x 664
  viewport with `100vh` resolved to 750px, as iOS does). The panel now starts under the header's row and is at most
  `100dvh` (the visible height, which follows the toolbars; `100vh` before it for browsers without dynamic viewport
  units) less the header. It is the only scroller (admin3 let the list inside scroll as well), its scrolling does not
  chain to the page (`overscroll-behavior: contain`), the page does not scroll while it is open, and the safe area
  insets keep its ends clear of the home indicator where a page covers it (`viewport-fit=cover`).
- The closed phone menu was still in the Tab order (`max-height: 0` hides, it does not remove): it is `visibility:
  hidden` after its closing slide now. A menu left open while the window grows past 575px (a phone turned to
  landscape) is closed, which also gives the page its scrolling back.

### Updated
- The phone menu is laid out for thumbs: the search first and on a row of its own, then the tabs as a grid of tiles
  (three to a row at 390px, two at 320px, one at 200% text) instead of a column of small centred links, the current
  tab filled with the accent; the user's actions (the user's name, leading to their object, Change information,
  Change password, My e-mail preferences, Logout) as a row of their own; a Close button at the end. Every target is
  at least 44 x 44px (Apple's Human Interface Guidelines; the search's scope button 40px inside the 44px field), with
  16px at the panel's top and 24px at its sides and end. On a phone the header's row keeps the logo, the
  environment badge, "Open the site", light/dark and the menu button, which shows a cross while the menu is open; the
  user menu and logout move into the menu (admin4l's header, which has no such row, keeps them).
- Keyboard: while the phone menu is open, Tab and Shift+Tab stay in the header (its row, the menu button and the
  panel); Escape and the Close button close it and give the focus back to the button; a tap outside the header
  closes it. No slide with reduced motion; in forced colors the tiles keep an edge and the current tab is underlined
  with a heavier edge.
- New template `parts/a4_phone_user.tpl` (the user's actions; the same links and conditions as
  `parts/a4_header_user.tpl`). In `page_topmenu.tpl` and admin4l's tab menu block the phone search moved to the
  menu's start; admin4.js's one-line menu leaves the phone's own items (`.a4-phone-only`) out of its fitting. From
  576px nothing changes.
- Test `AdminPhoneMenuTest` (testsuite `design`): the panel's height (`100dvh` after a `100vh` fallback, no `100vh`
  left in the phone rules), its single scroller and `overscroll-behavior`, the grid, the 44px targets, the order of
  the menu's items, the user row's links against the header's, the hidden closed panel, and in node the focus trap,
  the Close button, the tap outside (`pointerdown`: iOS sends no click for a tap on the page's background) and the close on a wider window. `AdminHeaderTest` follows the new order.
- admin4l's tab menu block (`explayouts/block/admin_tab_menu.tpl`) ends with the same Close button as admin4's top
  menu, wired by the same script; `AdminPhoneMenuTest` checks its order and button too.

## 15 October 2026: the site menu of the administration

The globe in the admin4 and admin4l header, which opened the site, now also lists the installation's other sites
that the signed-in user may log in to, with their addresses, so an administrator can see which end points there are without reading the INI
settings. One button in the place of the link: the header takes no more room. On by default.
[Feature](../../eng-us/features/6.0/admin4-design.md#what-you-get) ("Site menu").

### Added
- `site.ini [AdminSiteAccessMenu]`: `Show` (`enabled`; `disabled` leaves the globe a plain link), `List` (`related`:
  `RelatedSiteAccessList[]` of the admin siteaccess, else `AvailableSiteAccessList[]`; `available`: all of
  `AvailableSiteAccessList[]`; an installation with separate databases per site sets `RelatedSiteAccessList[]` for
  each administration), `HiddenSiteAccessList[]`, `AdminSiteAccessList[]` (which siteaccesses count as
  administrations; empty: those whose `SiteDesign` begins with `admin` or that set `FrontSiteAccess`) and
  `ListAdministrations` (`disabled`: sites only; `enabled`: the administrations too, as a group of their own; part of
  the cache key).
- `ezpSiteAccessMenu` and the template operators `siteaccess_menu( $current_user )` and `siteaccess_menu_key()`
  (`ezpSiteAccessMenuOperator`), and `ezpSiteAccessURL::frontSiteAccess()`, the name of the siteaccess `front()` links
  to. The addresses are `ezpSiteAccessURL::root()`'s, as `siteaccess_url()` has them (MatchOrder, URIMatchType,
  HostMatchMapItems, SiteURL, the scheme, host and port of the request).
- `parts/a4_header_site.tpl`, the site link and the menu for admin4's `page_topmenu.tpl` and admin4l's tools block.
  For signed-in users, the globe is a disclosure button (aria-expanded, aria-controls) whose list opens the site
  first, highlighted, then the other sites (with `ListAdministrations=enabled` the administrations as a second
  group), each with its SiteName (the identifier without one), identifier, locale and address, in the same window; the siteaccess in use carries
  `aria-current`. Keys as the user menu: ArrowDown opens it, ArrowUp/ArrowDown/Home/End move, Escape closes it and
  returns the focus, a click outside closes it. Without JavaScript, or with the menu off, or with no other
  siteaccess to list, the globe stays the link it was. On a phone the list opens under the header. Light, dark and
  forced colors. German and American English for the four new strings. Test: `AdminSiteAccessMenuTest` (with the
  node harness `tests/tests/design/js/site_menu.js`).
- Besides the site the globe opens, a siteaccess is listed only where the signed-in user may log in: `user/login`
  with its SiteAccess limitation, checked by `eZUser::canLoginToSiteAccess()` as the login checks it. The template
  passes `$current_user` to `siteaccess_menu()`; the header's cache blocks are keyed by the user's role hash already.
  The entries take `--a4-control-h` and a spacing token.

### Updated
- The header's cache blocks (admin4 and admin4l `pagelayout.tpl`) are keyed by `siteaccess_menu_key()` too: the
  list, its groups, the siteaccess in use and the scheme, host and port of the request. A changed `SiteName`,
  `Locale` or `SiteDesign` of a listed siteaccess shows once the template caches of the administration siteaccess
  are cleared.
- A mega menu does not open on hover while the site menu is open, as for the user menu.

## 15 October 2026: admin4's typeface is the Helvetica Neue stack

### Updated
- admin4: `--a4-font` (`components.css`, also the copy in `design/admin`) is now `"Helvetica Neue", "Noto Sans",
  "Liberation Sans", Arial, sans-serif, "Apple Color Emoji", "Segoe UI Emoji", "Segoe UI Symbol", "Noto Color Emoji"`
  instead of the operating system's interface font (`system-ui, -apple-system, "Segoe UI", Roboto, ...`). The text
  looks the same on macOS, Windows and Linux, and emoji in content names and text are drawn in color everywhere.
  System fonts only; no font file is added.
- admin4: the stylesheets that still named a font list of their own use the tokens: `core.css` (body, headings, form
  fields, buttons; `pre` and `code` take `--a4-mono` instead of "Courier New"), the pager of
  `theme/admin_datatable.css`, `content.css`, `packagecompare.css`, `ezinfo.css`, `visual-templateview.css`.
  `core.css` keeps Arial (and `monospace`) as the fallback of `var()`, for the standard popup, print and redirect
  layouts that load it without `components.css`. The text and the table cells of the rich text editor
  (`editor_content.css`) follow `--a4-font` instead of the editor skin's Arial, its literal blocks `--a4-mono`.
- Test: `AdminFontStackTest` (testsuite `design`): `--a4-font` is exactly this stack in both copies of
  `components.css`, and every `font-family` of admin4's stylesheets is `var(--a4-font)` or `var(--a4-mono)` (with an
  optional fallback) or `inherit` (icon fonts aside); the emoji faces follow the text faces (the generic family
  last of these, Windows' color emoji before its monochrome symbols); no font is downloaded (the one `@font-face` is
  the IcoMoon icon font from the design's folder, no template or stylesheet of admin4 or admin4l loads a web font).
  admin4l has no stylesheets of its own and takes all of this from admin4.

### Upgrade notes
- The administration's text changes from the system interface font (San Francisco on macOS, Segoe UI on Windows) to
  Helvetica Neue, Noto Sans, Liberation Sans or Arial, whichever is installed first, with color emoji fallbacks. To
  keep the old look, redefine `--a4-font` in a stylesheet of your own (`design.ini [StylesheetSettings]`
  `BackendCSSFileList[]`), e.g. `:root { --a4-font: system-ui, -apple-system, "Segoe UI", Roboto, "Helvetica Neue",
  Arial, sans-serif; }`.

## LoveStack features (from 2026-10-12)

Features of LoveStack, Mugo Web's distribution of the same kernel, rebuilt in Exponential with thanks to Mugo Web;
each line names the LoveStack commit it comes from.

Overview, feature by feature, with who each one is for and how to switch it on:
[LoveStack features in Exponential 6](../../eng-us/bc/6.0/lovestack.md).

- Moving a site from LoveStack: `./console exp:lovestack check` lists what a LoveStack installation brings along
  and what to do with each item: the database version row `lovestack`, LoveStack's `db-schema-version` row, a unique
  index on `ezuser.login`, a `data_text NOT NULL DEFAULT ''` column, the `legacy_2018` extension, LoveStack-only
  settings in `settings/override` and the siteaccesses (with the Exponential name where there is one) and the
  `mugoweb/ezpublish-legacy` package in `composer.json` (`expLoveStack`). It reads only and ends with exit code 0
  when nothing needs to be done, 1 otherwise; `--json` for a machine-readable report. Tested on a LoveStack-shaped
