## 17 October 2026: map addresses escaped for HTML before JavaScript

### Updated
- A map location's address is stored as typed and escaped where it is printed, in the same order everywhere: `|wash`
  in HTML, `|wash|wash( 'javascript' )` where a map script hands it to `setContent()`, which reads HTML. Markup in
  an address (or in the title of cjw_themes_jumper's popup) ran in the map popup before.
  Required versions: ezgmaplocation ~6.0.9, ezflow ~6.1.6, cjw_themes_jumper ~0.1.4.
- ezgmaplocation 6.0.8 stores the address without HTML entities. **Upgrade step, once per database, before editors
  work with it:** `php extension/ezgmaplocation/bin/php/decodeaddressentities.php -s <siteaccess>` (dry run), then
  again with `--apply`; rows saved by 6.0.7 or earlier would show their entities otherwise. Its `fromString()` keeps
  an address with `|#` whole and stores only numeric coordinates, and its settings file no longer prints itself.
- ezgmaplocation 6.0.9: the edit form opens on a database that returns the coordinates as numbers (SQLite, PDO),
  and its address, latitude and longitude fields have accessible names.
- ezflow 6.1.6: the map block leaves out a location without a map value instead of stopping the whole map.
- Tests: `expMapAddressEscapingTest` (suite `maps`, no database): the escaping order in every map template, the
  rendered pipeline, the coordinates made strings before `explode()`, and `fromString()`.

## 17 October 2026: job queue, autosave and star rating without inline script or GET writes

### Updated
- expqueue 0.1.3: a job queue control that asks before it is sent declares its question in `data-a4-confirm` instead
  of an inline `onsubmit` handler. admin4 and admin4l ask it with `a4_actions.js`, the other administration designs
  with the extension's `expqueue_confirm.js` (`design.ini [JavaScriptSettings] BackendJavaScriptList`); it is asked
  once in every design.
- ezautosave 6.0.10: the admin edit form reads the autosave settings from a JSON block (`<` and `>` escaped) and starts
  the autosave from the deferred `javascript/ezautosave/autosave_admin.js`, so the edit page carries no inline script.

### Fixed
- ezstarrating 6.0.11: `ezstarrating::rate` answers POST requests only, so an image or link to its GET address on
  another page can no longer rate in the visitor's name. The shipped script already posts with the form token. A theme
  of its own that still calls the GET address can be allowed with the new `site.ini [eZStarRating] AllowGetRating`
  (`disabled` by default), at the cost of that protection.
- Required versions: expqueue ~0.1.3, ezautosave ~6.0.10, ezstarrating ~6.0.11. Tests: each extension's DB-less
  `tests/unit` (run `phpunit` in the extension).

## 17 October 2026: the icon theme follows the design

### Updated
- The class, file type, class group and other icons of admin4 and admin4l (`class_icon`, `mimetype_icon`,
  `classgroup_icon`, `icon`, the content tree's `icon_info`) are Bootstrap Icons without a setting of the siteaccess.
  New `icon.ini [IconSettings] DesignTheme[<design>]`, set to `bootstrap-icons` for `admin4` and `admin4l`: while that
  design is the `SiteDesign`, its theme replaces `Theme`, and `Theme` is searched right after it. Before, the theme
  was opt-in per admin siteaccess, and an admin siteaccess whose `icon.ini.append.php` says `Theme=crystal-admin` (as
  the setup writes it) drew the crystal PNGs in the content tree, the node header and the sub items. A user who
  switches to admin3 gets the siteaccess's `Theme` again; a type's own `Theme` still wins.
- The Settings extension wizard and the RAD health check know the themes of `DesignTheme[]` as well.

### Fixed
- `class_icon` and the other icon operators no longer pass null to `strlen()` when `eZSys::wwwDir()` is not set
  (command line).
- `icon_info` keeps its answer per type and theme list instead of per type only, so a persistent worker or a switch of
  the design in the same session never hands one design the icons of another.
- `icon_info` leaves out a `ClassMap[]` (or other map) entry whose file the theme does not have: an extension that
  maps its classes to crystal paths (cjw_newsletter) no longer gives the admin4 content tree addresses under
  `bootstrap-icons/svg/` that answer 404; the theme's own entry or its `Default` is used.
- The two tests of `eZSiteAccess::getIni()` and `eZSiteAccess::findPathToSiteAccess()` live once, in
  `eZSiteAccessGetIniAndFindPathTest` (kernel-classes, no database), instead of twice under file names that differ
  only in case.

### Upgrade notes
- Nothing to do. To keep the crystal PNGs in admin4, set `DesignTheme[admin4]=` (empty) in the admin siteaccess's
  `icon.ini.append.php`. A design of your own built on admin4 needs its own `DesignTheme[]` entry. The opt-in setting
  (`Theme=bootstrap-icons`) still works and can stay.

Tests: `eZWordToImageOperatorDesignThemeTest` (kernel-classes), without a database.

## 17 October 2026: safer defaults in owsimpleoperator, no inline tracker in bcwebsitestatistics, full sitemap URLs in bcgooglesitemaps

### Updated
- owsimpleoperator 1.2.7 no longer permits `file_get_contents` and `ereg_replace` as template operators by default:
  the first let every template read any file the web server can read, the second no longer exists since PHP 7. Its
  `DebugOutput` is disabled by default. A site whose templates use one of them lists it again in its own
  `owsimpleoperator.ini.append.php` (`[PHPFunctions] PermittedFunctionList[]`); no template shipped with or installed
  on this installation uses either.
- bcwebsitestatistics 1.0.11 drops the inline `urchinTracker()` onClick handler from the download links of its
  binary file view: the retired Urchin tracker no longer exists, and the handler was blocked under a Content Security
  Policy without 'unsafe-inline'.
- bcgooglesitemaps 1.1.6.5 builds each `<loc>` of the dynamic sitemap with `ezurl( 'no', 'full' )`: the addresses carry
  the scheme (https too), host, port and siteaccess path the site is served on, also on servers that do not pass
  `HTTP_HOST` in the environment (they were `http:///` there).
- Required versions: owsimpleoperator ~1.2.7, bcgooglesitemaps ~1.1.6.5, bcwebsitestatistics ~1.0.11.

## 17 October 2026: formulas, tables and embeds survive the assistant and the Tiptap editor

### Fixed
- expai 0.0.1.8: formulas, tables and embeds are no longer lost when Vector or a tool writes a rich text field. What
  the model cannot express in an ezxmltext field is sent as a placeholder (`⟦C1⟧` and the like) and restored from the
  stored value; a write that drops, repeats or invents one is refused, or staged for review in a Vector thread. New
  `expai.ini [RichTextSettings]` (`Placeholders=enabled`). The bars of field actions are drawn with the edit form, so
  its fields no longer move down; above a rich text field whose editor has an assistant of its own (Tiptap with its
  AI on) the field actions are hidden (`[EditViewSettings] HideActionsWithEditorAI=false` shows them), and the
  length of a field the model reads is `[EditViewSettings] MaxFieldLength` (default 20000).
- exp_oe_tiptap 0.1.10 (not a requirement of this repository; sites that install it get it through their own
  constraint): formulas (`exp_oe_tiptap.ini [EditorSettings] AtomicInlineCustomTags[]`, default `math_latex` and
  `math_mathml`) are inline atoms, so typing, deleting or formatting around a formula no longer changes it; their text
  is edited in the custom tag dialog's new field Content. Unedited content is stored byte for byte as before.

## 17 October 2026: configuration managed by the deployment

### Added
- `site.ini [ConfigurationSettings] ManagedByDeployment` (default `disabled`). Enabled for a siteaccess, the admin
  interface does not change its configuration on the server: the views that write settings, templates, generated
  code or packages (`settings/edit`, the toolbar and menu editors, the template editor, the setup wizard, extension
  activation, `setup/rad` and the code generators, `package/upload|install|uninstall|create|export`) answer like a
  disabled view and are hidden from the menus, and neither `eZINI::save()` nor `expIniEditor::save()` writes a
  settings file in a web request, under Velocity too. Read-only views and command line scripts are not affected;
  generated audit and mail preference keys are still written. See `doc/eng-us/features/6.0/managed-by-deployment.md`.

## 17 October 2026: log management on the command line and as a cronjob part

Page: [Log rotation and pruning](../../eng-us/features/6.0/log-rotation.md).

### Added
- **`expLogManager`** (`kernel/classes/logviewer/`), the kernel's implementation of `expLogManagerInterface`, which
  the log viewer's management pages take through `expLogManagerFactory`:
  - every log with its archives, sizes, growth, limits (with where they come from) and owner;
  - plans (dry runs) for rotate, prune, compress and empty, carried out step by step, each step resolved through the
    allow-list again under the rotation lock.
- **On the command line**: `./console exp:logs --archives`, `--rotate=<keys>`, `--rotate-all [--force]`,
  `--prune[=<keys>] [--days=N]`, `--compress[=<keys>]`, `--truncate=<keys> [--in-place]`, each with `--dry-run` and
  `--json`; changes are audited as `system.log.rotate`, `system.log.delete` and `system.log.truncate`.
- **The cronjob part `logs`** (`cronjobs/explogrotate.php`, `[CronjobPart-logs]`, in no default part): rotates the
  logs over their `MaxSize`, prunes and compresses.
- `logviewer.ini [RotationSettings]`: `MaxSize[]`, `Keep[]`, `Compress[]` and `MaxAgeDays[]` per log key (empty by
  default; a log without an entry takes `site.ini [FileSettings]`).
- Tests (no database): `expLogManagerTest`.

## 17 October 2026: the expai test suite runs on any site

### Updated
- expai 0.0.1.9 (tests only, no change to what the extension does): its unit suite no longer depends on the language,
  region rule and operating system of the site it runs on. A German siteaccess with `[DataPolicy]
  DefaultRegions[]=eu;local` gives the same result as an English one, and a run without a database skips the tests
  that need it instead of ending in errors.

## 17 October 2026: authorizing an OAuth client needs the policy oauth/authorize

Page: [Upgrade note: authorizing an OAuth client needs the policy oauth/authorize](../../eng-us/bc/6.0/oauth-authorize-policy.md).

### Fixed
- `oauth/authorize` never denied a signed in user: its check `!$access['accessWord'] == 'yes'` reads as
  `(!$access['accessWord']) == 'yes'`, which is always false, and the `oauth` module had no policy function to grant.
  The check is now `expOAuthAuthorizePolicy::userMayAuthorize()`; a user without the policy is sent to the client's
  end point with `error=access_denied` (RFC 6749, section 4.1.2.1). A search of the kernel and every extension for the
  same `!$x == ...` pattern found no other access check with it.

### Added
- The policy function **oauth/authorize**, with an optional SiteAccess limitation.
- `rest.ini [OAuthSettings] AuthorizeRequiresPolicy` (default `enabled`; `disabled` gives back the old behavior and
  is meant only for the time of an upgrade).
- `bin/php/addoauthauthorizepolicy.php` (`--dry-run`, `--role=<id or name>`): grants `oauth/authorize` to every role
  with `user/login`, for the same siteaccesses; roles that have it already and roles of only the anonymous user are
  skipped.
- New installations give the Editor role `oauth/authorize`.
- Tests (no database): `expOAuthAuthorizePolicyTest`, `expOAuthAuthorizePolicyGrantTest`, and the view's
  `access_denied` redirect in `ezpOauthAuthorizeRedirectTest`.

## 17 October 2026: a toolbar theme for the debug bar

### Added
- `debugbar.ini [DebugBarSettings] Theme=toolbar` draws the pinned bar of the Exp Debug bar as a dark, compact
  toolbar in the manner of the Symfony web debug toolbar: an icon in each block, the request's HTTP status and
  module/view in the first block, amber and red blocks filled with their colour, and the details of a block (page and
  query time, queries, memory and its limit, the message counts, the request) in a popup above it on hover or focus.
  The panel follows the Symfony profiler: the tabs a dark menu down the left side with icons, a large title, and
  metrics at the head of Messages, Timing, SQL, Templates, Memory and Server. New stylesheet
  `design/standard/stylesheets/expdebugbar-toolbar.css`, linked only in this theme. The setting is also in the bar's
  Settings tab ("Bar theme").

### Upgrade notes
- Nothing to do: `Theme=default` keeps the bar as it was. Guide: `doc/eng-us/bc/6.0/debug-bar.md`, "Themes".

Tests: `expDebugBarReportToolbarThemeTest` (lib), without a database.

### Fixed
- The debug bar no longer grows or moves after the page has loaded (#571): the minimise symbol, "Keep open on reload"
  and the minimised tab are in the report's markup instead of being added by its scripts, the bar keeps at least the
  height of one line, and its sides are logical, so its link stays at the end of the line in a right-to-left
  interface. Both themes. The report has no inline script any more: what must run before the first paint is
  `design/standard/javascript/expdebugbar-head.js`, linked without `defer`, so a Content-Security-Policy with
  `script-src 'self'` holds.


## 17 October 2026: Tiptap in the layout editor, and Tiptap's admin4 look without a setting

### Updated
- explayouts-ui-api ~1.3.19: the layout editor's rich text and code editors are Tiptap instead of CKEditor 4 and Ace.
  CKEditor 4 is end of life without free security updates; the two editors were 23 MB of `design/standard/vendor/`,
  which is removed. The editor bundle is unchanged: an adapter provides the calls it makes. Rich text keeps CKEditor's
  toolbar and writes its HTML as CKEditor did, and unchanged content comes back byte for byte. HTML snippet and Markdown
  blocks keep their text byte for byte. No longer available: anchors, bidi buttons and paste-from-Word cleanup;
  syntax checking, themes, search and code folding. Nothing has to change in an installation.
- exp_oe_tiptap 0.1.11 (not required by this package): `exp_oe_tiptap.ini [EditorSettings] DesignSkin[]` names the
  Tiptap skin per admin design, used when `Skin` is not set. admin4 and admin4l get admin4's look (`a4`) without a
  setting of their own; `Skin=o2k7` keeps ezoe's look. The edit template no longer writes "Operator 'has_attribute' is
  not registered" to warning.log.

## 17 October 2026: the admin4 mega menu loads reliably

Page: [admin4 design, "Mega menu"](../../eng-us/features/6.0/admin4-design.md#mega-menu-prototype-opt-in).

### Fixed
- The mega menu of a top tab no longer says "The <tab> menu could not be loaded." when nothing failed: a load the
  browser stops because another page is being opened (Firefox reports it as a network error) shows no error, and the
  next opening loads again.
- A passing failure (no connection, 5xx, 408, 429, no answer after 10 s, an answer without the menu) is tried once
  more after 600 ms. A failure for good offers "Try again" and keeps the link to the tab's page; a signed-out user is
  offered a link to sign in instead of the error.
- One request per tab and page, shared by every opening while it runs; the script binds each tab once.
- `user/megamenu` answers its script with a bare status and an `X-Exp-Mega` header (`ok`, `unknown`, `signed-out`,
  `denied`), never the sign-in page or an error page in the page layout; it releases the session as soon as the user
  is known, so the page's other requests do not wait for it, and sends the fragment without the templates'
  indentation (Setup about 5 KB instead of 75 KB).

### Updated
- `site.ini [SiteAccessSettings] AnonymousAccessList[]` and `[RoleSettings] PolicyOmitList[]` name `user/megamenu`:
  the view checks the signed-in user, `user/login` for the siteaccess and the tab itself. A site that removes either
  entry gets the sign-in or error page back, which the script also handles.
- The fragment's root carries `data-a4-mega-fragment="<tab>"`; the script takes the element with that marker (or a
  design's own `data-a4-mega-groups`).
- New strings (eng-US, ger-DE): "Try again", "You are signed out. Sign in again to see the %tab menu.", "Sign in".
- Tests (no database): `AdminMegaMenuTest` MM-09, the load scenarios run in node against `mini_dom.js`.

## 17 October 2026: git_manager, ezupdate and the database editor follow the admin design

### Updated
- The pages of git_manager (dashboard, commit details, Backup), ezupdate (`update/dashboard` and the header, footer,
  status and method parts every update view shares) and sevenx_dse (`dse/dashboard`, its gate page and the embedded
  database editor) are drawn with the shared admin components (`.a4-card`, `.a4-table`, `.a4-btn`, `.a4-toolbar`,
  `.a4-tag`/`.a4-badge`, the notices and empty states) and the `--a4-*` tokens, so they follow admin4's light and dark
  modes and admin4l, and the classic admin gets the shared classes it has. No template carries a `<style>` block, a
  `style=""` attribute or an inline script any more: each extension's own rules are in a stylesheet loaded through its
  `design.ini` `[StylesheetSettings]` (bundled by the packer), and the scripts are files. Every button, form,
  confirmation and status poll works as before. Required versions: git_manager ~2.0.20, ezupdate ~1.1.20,
  sevenx_dse ~1.1.10.
- git_manager's dashboard and commit details get their own left menu (Git Manager, Backup) that ends with admin4's
  width switch; the Backup page and the update and database editor pages keep the Setup menu, which has it already.
  Sites that override git_manager's templates with the old `.gm-*` classes lose that styling.
- The database editor's stylesheets are scoped to the editor, so they no longer restyle the admin frame and its left
  column, and its login fields, logo, menu button and language select carry accessible names.
- New DB-less tests under `tests/tests/extension/{git_manager,ezupdate,sevenx_dse}` check that these extensions'
  admin templates carry no inline CSS or scripts, that their stylesheets use only the tokens, and that their admin4
  menus include the width switch. Each skips when its extension is not installed.

## 17 October 2026: copying a layout makes each zone once

### Fixed
- explayouts-core ~1.0.8: copying a layout gave every zone of the copy two rows, the layout type's and the source's.
  The layout editor added new blocks to one row and showed the other, so a block added to a copy disappeared from the
  editor and the page. A copy now has each zone once, with the source zone's position, its link to a shared layout
  zone and its blocks, collections and parameters. Draft and publish are unchanged. Layouts copied before can carry
  duplicate zone rows; an installation that copied layouts can list them by grouping `explayouts_zone` on
  `layout_id, status, identifier`.

## 17 October 2026: 404.log and security.log, error.log keeps the real faults

Page: [Request logs: 404.log and security.log](../../eng-us/features/6.0/request-logs.md).

### Added
- **`var/log/404.log`** (`expRequestLog`, `kernel/classes/requestlog/`): one line per 404 or 410 answer with its
  reason (`module:`, `view:`, `disabled:`, `node:`, `object:`, `rule:`, `file:`, `kernel:`), the siteaccess, the node when
  known, the referrer and a short user agent; client addresses masked, every query value `***`. `site.ini
  [RequestLogSettings]`: `NotFoundLog=enabled`, `NotFoundLogFile=404.log`, `MaskIP=enabled`, `MaxURILength=300`.
- **The request classifier and `var/log/security.log`** (`expRequestClassifier`, `kernel/classes/requestclassifier/`,
  `settings/requestclassifier.ini`): a request that is a probe, a path traversal, an injection, comes from a known
  attack tool or is one of too many not-found answers from one client is written as one line
  `<category>/<rule> confidence=... <status> ...`, whatever its status, instead of its 404, notice or error line.
  Classification only, nothing is blocked. Rule sets by INI (`RuleSets[]`, `[RuleSet-<id>]` with `Patterns[]`,
  `Disabled[]`, `Excludes[]`), extensions add their own (`expRequestRuleSetInterface`, `expRequestPatternRuleSet`),
  and `Listeners[]` (`expRequestVerdictListenerInterface`) is the hook for a later ban list or rate limit.
- The log viewer: `404.log` and `security.log` are kernel logs (`/setup/logs/view/404`, `/setup/logs/view/security`)
  with the labels "Not found requests (404)" and "Security (request classifier)", the levels notice and warning, and
  rotation defaults (`[RotationSettings]`: 10 MB, 5 archives, compressed, pruned after 30 and 90 days).
- The error digest groups per log (`logviewer.ini [DigestSettings] GroupBy[<key>]`: `message`, `category-rule`,
  `reason`, `reason-kind`, `uri`) and lists other levels per log (`LogLevels[<key>]`): security.log by
  `<category>/<rule>`, 404.log by the reason of the answer, with its notices.
- `LogLabel[<key>]` names any log of the log directories by its key, translated in the context `kernel/logviewer`.
- Tests (no database): `expRequestLogTest`, the classifier's tests in `tests/tests/kernel/requestclassifier/` and
  `expLogRequestLogsTest`.

### Updated
- `error.log` keeps the real faults: "Undefined module", "Module/View ... is disabled", "Undefined view", "Error
  ocurred using URI ... HTTP 404" and node not found go to `404.log` (or to `security.log` for a classified probe)
  while `NotFoundLog=enabled`. With `NotFoundLog=disabled` they are written to `error.log` as before. 401 and 403
  stay notices in `notice.log`.
- The log parser reads the lines of both logs: the request, the siteaccess and the status become fields, the source
  is the status or `<category>/<rule>`.
- `logviewer.ini [DigestSettings]` and `[HealthSettings]` are read from the settings: before, the defaults were used
  whatever the file said.

### Upgrade notes
- Nothing to do. To keep the old behavior, set `site.ini [RequestLogSettings] NotFoundLog=disabled` and
  `requestclassifier.ini [ClassifierSettings] Classification=disabled`.

## 17 October 2026: the file manifest check is a warning on pull requests

Every Quality failure of a pull request since 15 October stopped at "File manifest (share/filelist.md5)". The check
runs on the merge of the branch into the current `main`, so when `main` changed the same files a contributor could
not produce the merged checksums, and the person merging refreshes the manifest anyway.

- On a `pull_request` event a mismatch is a `::warning::` annotation plus the diff and a note in the job summary;
  the job does not fail. On a push to `main` the check stays blocking, exactly as before.
- Test: `CiWorkflowPullRequestTest::testManifestWarnsOnPullRequestsAndBlocksOnPush` (suite `quality`).
- Guide: [Pull requests](../../eng-us/features/6.0/quality-checks.md#pull-requests), [CI](../../eng-us/bc/6.0/ci.md).

## 17 October 2026: a merged contributor commit no longer fails the commit message check

Every pull request is merged and its commits are kept as written, so a contributor's subject such as `Changed: ...`
reached `main` and the Quality step "Commit messages" failed the push (and the pull request) for a commit nobody
could reword any more.

- The check is `.githooks/check-commit-range <base> <head>`, used by CI and by the local gates alike. A commit by a
  project author, an address listed in `.githooks/commit-msg-authors`, must still start with `Added: `, `Updated: `,
  `Fixed: `, `Removed: ` or `Renamed: ` and fails otherwise. A commit by anyone else gets a warning annotation and
  never fails. Merge commits are not checked. `EXP_COMMIT_MSG_STRICT_ALL=1` makes every author strict.
- The `commit-msg` hook on a developer machine is unchanged.
- Tests: `CheckCommitRangeTest` (suite `quality`) builds throwaway repositories and covers project and contributor
  commits, a merged contributor branch, the strict switch and the annotations.
- Guide: [The commit messages](../../eng-us/features/6.0/quality-checks.md#pull-requests).

## 17 October 2026: publishing under Velocity after a registration

### Fixed
- content/edit and user/register each give the edit form an action check of their own name
  (`expContentEditCheckContentActions`, `expUserRegisterCheckContentActions`) instead of sharing
  `checkContentActions`. Velocity's persistent workers keep every function a worker defined, so after one
  user/register in a worker, Publish in content/edit there ran the registration operation on the edited object:
  the publish answered 500 and the draft stayed unpublished. Apache and PHP-FPM were not affected. A custom
  content/edit or user/register view that hooks `checkContentActions` keeps working, but under Velocity it should
  give its function a name of its own too.
- The text part of the mail layout (`design:mail/layout_text.tpl`) writes a row value that is not a string (a number,
  or nothing) as text instead of stopping the mail with an error.

## 17 October 2026: module views define global functions of their own name

### Fixed
- Under Velocity's persistent workers a worker keeps every global function it ever defined, so two views that
  defined the same name behind `function_exists()` served each other's function. shop/productcategories and
  shop/vattype both defined `applyChanges` (with different bodies: saving the categories of one view could run the
  VAT type code of the other), content/copy and content/copysubtree both defined `browse`, content/search and
  content/advancedsearch both defined `pageLimit`. Apache and PHP-FPM were not affected.
- Every global function of a kernel module view now has a name of its own, `expModuleView<Module><View><Name>`
  (for example `expModuleViewShopVatTypeApplyChanges`, `expModuleViewContentCopyBrowse`). oauth/authorize no longer
  defines the generic `error()`, `response()` and `getHTTPVariable()` (now `expModuleViewOauthAuthorizeError`,
  `expModuleViewOauthAuthorizeResponse`, `expModuleViewOauthAuthorizeGetHTTPVariable`), which an extension defining
  the same name would have replaced. `kernel/settings/validation.php` defines `expSettingsValidate()` instead of
  `validate()`, and the unused `testPHPIni()` of the setup tests is `expSetupTestPHPIni()`.
- The bundled ezjscore (1.8.3) and ezoe views do the same: `expModuleViewEzjscoreRunExitWithInternalError`,
  `expModuleViewEzjscoreRunHasAccessToBySetting`, `expModuleViewEzjscoreCallMultipleServerCalls`,
  `expModuleViewEzoeSpellcheckRpcFormatParam`, `expModuleViewEzoeSpellcheckRpcGetRequestParam`.
- The helpers of `kernel/content/node_edit.php`, `relation_edit.php`, `section_edit.php` and `state_edit.php`
  (`initializeNodeEdit()` and the rest) keep their names: custom content/edit views call them.

### Tests
- `expGlobalFunctionCollisionTest` (no database) scans kernel/, lib/, design/ and the bundled extensions by token
  and fails when two request-time files define the same global function, or when a module view defines a generic
  or unprefixed one.

### Upgrade notes
- Code that called one of the old names of a view's function (only possible after that view had run in the same
  request) calls the new name. A custom view should give its own global functions a prefixed name too.

## 17 October 2026: extensions without inline scripts in their templates

### Updated
- The installed extensions move their inline scripts, event handler attributes and `javascript:` links into script
  files with `data-*` attributes, so the administration's Content Security Policy (`script-src 'self'`) no longer
  reports them, and they keep working once it is enforced. composer.json requires these releases:
  cjw_newsletter 4.2.12, eztags 2.4.17, xrowextract 2.5.17, ezownerchange 1.4.2, ezgmaplocation 6.0.10, ezwt 6.0.11,
  ezie 6.0.13, recaptcha 1.4.8, hcaptcha 1.4, syndication 1.3.8, bccie 1.1.16, ezautosave 6.0.12, ezmbpaex 6.0.7,
  explayouts 1.4.37, explayouts-ui 1.3.20, explayouts-ui-api 1.3.24 (the layout editor), explayouts-content-browser-ui
  1.0.8, xrowmetadata 1.4.13, ezstarrating 6.0.12, bcwebsitestatistics 1.0.12, enhancedezbinaryfile 4.4.8,
  ezmultiupload 6.1.7, expui 1.0.0.10, expai 0.0.1.12, expchangeclass 1.0.9 and sevenx_authentication_2fa 1.0.9
  (and AdminAid 1.1.4, which is installed from its repository).
- Their context menu entries are added to `menuArray` by the extension's script from data attributes, and the

- The expservices portal designs (expportal_jquery, expportal_reactive) set their saved color mode from
  `javascript/portal/theme_head.js` instead of an inline script.

## 17 October 2026: the update Overview no longer starts Composer on every visit

### Fixed
- ezupdate's Overview (`update/dashboard`) ran `composer --version` each time it was opened, and each run wrote a
  notice to `var/log/notice.log`. ezupdate 1.1.21 keeps the answer in `<cache dir>/ezupdate/composer-version.json`
  for `ezupdate.ini [ComposerSettings] VersionCacheTime` seconds (default `86400`, `0` asks every time), keyed on the
  binary's path, size and time, so a new `composer.phar` is asked again at once; a failure is never kept. The cache is
  a cache item of its own (`ezupdate`, "Update extension cache"), cleared by "Clear all" and
  `bin/php/ezcache.php --clear-id=ezupdate`. A Composer run that succeeds is logged at debug level, one that fails
  still writes a notice. Required version: ezupdate ~1.1.21. Tests: `ezUpdateComposerVersionCacheTest` (DB-less, skips
  when ezupdate 1.1.21 is not installed).

  `--exp-le-on-dark-muted`). The editor's CSS moved from an inline style block in `app.tpl` into
  `stylesheets/explayouts-editor.css`, and the app and mobile scripts are linked without a version query. The new

  `EditorAccessibilityTest` (no database).

## 17 October 2026: every request is classified once, also one that ends through a clean exit

Page: [Request logs: 404.log and security.log](../../eng-us/features/6.0/request-logs.md).

### Fixed
- A request that ended through `eZExecution::cleanExit()` (a file download, an ezjscore call, a redirect, a view that
  sends its own output) was not classified, so a probe such as `/content/download/../../etc/passwd` or an ezjscore
  call with an injection in its query had no line in `security.log` unless it failed through the error module.
  `ezpKernelWeb::run()` now starts the request log with `expRequestLog::begin()`, which registers
  `expRequestLog::finish()` as a clean-up handler of `eZExecution`; the clean-up runs on a clean exit, at the normal
  end and after a fatal error. `finish()` runs once per request, however many of these reach it, so a request has
  at most one line, in `security.log` or `404.log`.
- Under Velocity's persistent workers the handler belongs to its request (`eZExecution` keys its handlers on
  `REQUEST_TIME_FLOAT`) and is added once: nothing is carried into the next request and nothing accumulates.
- `expRequestClassifier::setInstance()`: a test hook for the classifier `instance()` returns.

### Tests
- `expRequestLogRequestEndTest` (no database): a download probe and an ezjscore injection that end through the
  clean-up have one line each in `security.log`, a normal download and ezjscore call none; the normal end, the error
  module and a clean exit together still write one line; a real `cleanExit()` in a process of its own; eight
  requests in a row in one process as a persistent worker serves them, each probe once, one handler per request.
- `phpunit.xml` has the testsuite `kernel-requestlog` (`tests/tests/kernel/requestlog`,
  `tests/tests/kernel/requestclassifier`): the request log and classifier tests were in no suite, so CI did not run
  them.

## 17 October 2026: a repeated probe reaches PHP under Velocity too

Page: [Request logs: 404.log and security.log](../../eng-us/features/6.0/request-logs.md).

### Fixed
- Under Velocity the response cache answered a repeated identical anonymous request itself (`X-Cache: HIT`), also
  when the request sent `Cache-Control: no-cache`, so a probe sent five times reached the request classifier once and
  `security.log` had one line where Apache writes five. A request the classifier flags is now answered
  `Cache-Control: private, no-store` (`expRequestLog`), whatever its status, so no shared cache keeps it.
- `[CacheSettings] NotFoundSeconds` ships as `0`: every 404 reaches PHP, as under Apache, so `404.log` and
  `security.log` see each one. A 404 without `Cache-Control` (a REST answer) was answered from the cache for a minute.
- `[CacheSettings] HonorRequestNoCache=enabled`: a request with `Cache-Control: no-cache` (or `Pragma: no-cache`)
  reaches PHP and its answer is stored again; a browser's ordinary reload (`max-age=0`) is still answered from the
  cache. Needs exponential-velocity with `Q.web.cache.honorRequestNoCache` (main 8a1321e); older versions ignore it.
- Normal anonymous pages are cached as before: measured on alpha's :8080, 12 of 15 requests for three cacheable
  pages answered from the cache before and after. A probe sent five times now writes five lines in `security.log`
  (one before, four answered from the cache).

### Tests
- `expRequestLogTest::testVerdictKeepsTheAnswerOutOfSharedCaches` (no database).

## 17 October 2026: a restart or deploy that is cut off no longer leaves Velocity down

### Fixed
- `exp:velocity restart`, and the restart step of `exp:velocity deploy`, run the stop and the start as one detached
  unit (own session, and a transient systemd scope `vc-restart-<date>-<token>` as root). On 17 October a deploy's
  caller was killed between the stop and the start and :8080 refused connections for 28 minutes; now the restart
  finishes on its own. The caller still waits and prints the outcome with the same exit code.
  `[ControlSettings] DetachedRestart=disabled` restores the old behavior.
- The server runs at `[ServerSettings] Nice` (default 5) whatever the caller's priority; a deploy run under
  `nice -n 19` left every worker at 19. `exp:velocity status` reports a parent at another value. `Nice=inherit`
  keeps the caller's.

### Added
- `restart-in-progress.json` and `stopped.json` next to the pid file (`expVelocityMarkers`): an explicit stop or kill
  marks the server as stopped on purpose, start, restart and graceful lift it. `deploy` starts a server that is down
  without that mark and leaves one that was stopped on purpose alone.
- `exp:velocity watchdog install|status|run|remove`: an optional systemd timer (`vc-watchdog`, every 45 s) that starts
  the default engine when no process of it runs, unless `stopped.json` says it was stopped on purpose or a restart is
  in progress (`restart-in-progress.json` younger than `[WatchdogSettings] RestartTimeout` with its process alive). It
  never touches a running server, backs off after three failed starts, logs to `var/log/velocity-watchdog.log` and is
  paused by `exp:velocity conf disable watchdog`. Off by default.

### Tests
- `expVelocityMarkersTest`, `expVelocityNiceTest` and `expVelocityWatchdogTest` (no database): markers, the start decision, the detached command,
  the priority prefix and its status check, the hand-over of the
  result.

## 17 October 2026: git_manager's commit log has pages and filters

### Added
- The commit log of `git_manager/dashboard` browses the whole history one page at a time: first, previous, the shared
  admin pager, next and last, "Showing x–y of z commits", and 10, 25, 50 or 100 commits per page (kept as the user
  preference `admin_git_manager_log_limit`, 50 until one is chosen). A page is `git log --skip --max-count` and the
  total `git rev-list --count`, so a long history is never read as a whole.
- The log filters by branch or tag, author and message text (fixed text, any case) besides the dates. The page, its
  size and the filters are view parameters (`/(offset)/50/(limit)/25/(ref)/main/(author)/jane/(grep)/fix`), so a link
  reproduces the view. Every value reaches git as an argument of its own, without a shell, and a branch or tag must be
  one of the repository's refs. The filter no longer lives in the session. Required version: git_manager ~2.0.21.
- Tests (no database): `tests/tests/extension/git_manager/GitManagerLogPagingTest.php` in the `admin-extensions`
  suite (the paging maths, the view parameters and the git arguments, hostile values included).

## 17 October 2026: export and import of layouts

### Added
- Layouts can be exported and imported between installations (explayouts_core 1.0.9, explayouts_ui 1.3.22). The
  admin page Layouts > Export chooses layouts (one, several, all), shared layouts, the mappings and drafts, or
  everything, and downloads a versioned ZIP file (JSON documents and a `manifest.json` with the format version, the
  source site, the counts and a sha256 per file). The shared layouts a chosen layout links a zone to always come along.
- Layouts > Import validates such a file and shows a dry run first: each layout and mapping is new, identical, changed
  or conflicting, with the differences. A strategy can be chosen for all or per item: skip, overwrite (a new draft,
  published only when asked) or create a copy. Node and object ids are mapped by remote id, and references without a
  match are listed. The import writes in one transaction (rolled back on any error), makes shared-layout links by
  identifier, writes audit events (`data.explayouts_core.import`) and removes only the resolver cache entries of the
  layouts and mappings it touched. The format is described in `extension/explayouts_core/doc/TRANSFER.md`.
- `./console exp:layouts list|export|import` does the same from the console, with `--dry-run`, `--json`,
  `--strategy`, `--item`, `--only`/`--all`, `--publish` and `--unmapped`.

### Updated
- The console resolves the `@alias` tags of extension commands as well (`exp:layouts` runs
  `ext:explayouts_ui:layouts`). A full name is used as written; a bare alias stays in the extension's namespace
  (`ext:<extension>:<alias>`). The aliases the extensions already declared now work too (`ConsoleExtensionAliasTest`).
- Export and import have policy functions of their own, `explayouts_ui/transfer_export` and
  `explayouts_ui/transfer_import`. The import page asked for `explayouts_ui/edit` before: a role that should keep
  importing needs `explayouts_ui/transfer_import` (roles with `explayouts_ui/*` have it).
- Required versions: explayouts_core ~1.0.9, explayouts_ui ~1.3.23 (1.3.22 sent no valid ZIP file: under the persistent
  server no file arrived, under Apache a stray newline came first; 1.3.23 fixes the download).

## 17 October 2026: the Velocity watchdog records an audit event only when it acts

### Updated
- `exp:velocity watchdog run` (the `vc-watchdog` timer, every 45 s) no longer records `system.command.run` for every
  run, about 1,900 events a day. It records one specific event when it does something: `system.velocity.watchdog.start`
  (it started the engine, or a start failed), `system.velocity.watchdog.backoff` (it waited after three failed starts)
  or `system.velocity.watchdog.skip` (it left a down engine alone: stopped on purpose, a restart in progress, the lock
  held, an overlapping run, an error). A run that finds the engine running, or a paused watchdog, records nothing.
  Every other command still records `system.command.run`.

### Added
- `eZScript::setAuditCommandRun( false )`: a command that records its own, more specific events can leave out the
  generic `system.command.run` of its run (only that run; there is no setting). `eZScript::auditCommandRun()` records
  it, and `shutdown()` calls it as before. The three new names are on by default (`settings/audit.ini` can switch them
  off like any other name).

### Tests
- `expVelocityWatchdogTest` (no database): a run that finds the engine running writes no audit event, a start, a failed
  start, a backoff and a skip write exactly one specific event each, and another command still writes
  `system.command.run`. `expAuditRecordTest`: the catalogue has 170 names.

## 17 October 2026: the Velocity watchdog records a skip only when its reason changes

### Fixed
- While the server stayed down on purpose (or a restart was in progress, or the lock was held), every 45-second check
  still wrote `system.velocity.watchdog.skip`, some 1,900 events a day. A skip is now recorded only for the first skip
  of a period or a new reason. The last reason is kept in `watchdog-last.json` beside the markers and removed when a
  check finds the engine running or starts it. `start` and `backoff` events are unchanged; `velocity-watchdog.log`
  still has one line per skip.

### Tests
- `expVelocityWatchdogTest` (no database): three skips with the same reason write one event, a new reason writes a new
  one, the engine running again resets it, and a start ends the period.

## 17 October 2026: enforcing the Content Security Policy

### Added
- **`site.ini [ContentSecurityPolicy]`** gains what an enforced policy needs; the shipped policy and `Mode=auto` stay
  as they were:
  - `AdditionalSources[]=<directive> <sources>` adds hosts to a directive, as a list that extensions and siteaccesses
    can each extend.
  - `ReportOnlyDirectives[]` sends a stricter `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` beside an enforced policy, for
    example `style-src 'self'` while `'unsafe-inline'` styles are still allowed.
  - `ReportOnlyDesigns[]` (shipped: `admin`, `admin2`, `admin_classic`) keeps requests in the classic administration
    report-only, including when a user picks it with the UI switch.
  - `ReportOnlyViews[]` (module/view, empty by default) keeps a view that sends a policy of its own report-only
    under the kernel's policy, for a server that does not let the view's header replace the kernel's (Velocity).
  - `Nonce=enabled` and `NonceDirectives[]=script-src` add a nonce per request. The template operator `csp_nonce()`
    prints it. It is never added next to `'unsafe-inline'`, and it is renewed per request in Velocity's persistent
    workers.
  - `ReportEndpoint` sends the reports to the siteaccess's own `csp/report`. An absolute report address is also
    announced with `Reporting-Endpoints` and `report-to`.
- `expContentSecurityPolicy::allowsInlineScripts()` tells code whether inline scripts run under the current policy.
  The online editor uses it: it does not offer TinyMCE 3 where the policy is enforced without `'unsafe-inline'`
  and falls back to TinyMCE 8.
- The shipped policy names `worker-src 'self'` (the same as the fallback before), so `AdditionalSources[]` can extend it.

### Updated
- `Mode=auto` also counts a siteaccess whose `AdditionalSiteDesignList[]` has an administration design (an editor
  siteaccess on admin4l). It is report-only there, as on the other administration siteaccesses.
- admin3 runs its pre-paint column width from `admin3_head.js`; no administration pagelayout has an inline script.
- Rolling back is one setting: `Mode=report-only`, then clear the INI cache. See
  [Content Security Policy](../../eng-us/features/6.0/content-security-policy.md), "Enforcing: nonce, more sources, a
  stricter companion, rollback".
  Tests: `expContentSecurityPolicyTest`, `AdminPrePaintGuardsTest`, `expOEContentSecurityPolicyEngineTest`.

## 17 October 2026: the reports of the Content Security Policy in a log of their own

### Added
- The kernel view `csp/report` takes the reports of the Content Security Policy, in both formats
  (`application/csp-report` of `report-uri`, `application/reports+json` of the Reporting API), and writes one line per
  violation to `var/log/csp.log`: directive, what was blocked, enforce or report, page, file, line, column and the
  first characters of the code. It is open to every visitor (`AnonymousAccessList[]`, `PolicyOmitList[]`), answers a
  status without a body, and protects itself: content types, size (`MaxReportBytes`), rate limits per client and in
  total (`ReportsPerMinute`, `ReportsPerMinuteTotal`), masked and bounded lines (`MaxViolationsPerReport`). A flood is
  written to `security.log` once a minute as `abuse/csp-report-flood`. Settings: `site.ini [ContentSecurityPolicy]`;
  guide: [Content Security Policy](../../eng-us/features/6.0/content-security-policy.md), "Reports".
- `csp.log` is a kernel log of the log viewer ("Content Security Policy reports", key `csp`), rotated like the request
  logs (5 archives, compressed, 10 MB, pruned after 30 days). The error digest groups it by directive and blocked
  origin (the new grouping `directive-blocked`), and the parser reads its fields.

### Updated
- ezformtoken leaves a POST to `csp/report` with a report content type alone: a signed-in browser sends its session
  cookie with a report, but no form token. Every other POST is checked as before.

### Tests
- `expContentSecurityPolicyReportTest` and `expLogCspReportsTest` (no database): both formats, masked and bounded lines,
  every status, the rate limits and the one flood line, the settings and registrations, the form token exception and
  the log viewer's key, levels, rotation, parser and digest.

## 17 October 2026: the setup wizard no longer takes an installed site offline

### Fixed
- A GET of `setup/init` (a crawler of a signed-in administrator) started the web setup wizard on an installed site,
  and its maintenance mode answered every page with 503 "The site is being set up". Opening the wizard now changes
  nothing: maintenance starts only with the first posted step of a fresh installation.
- On an installed site `setup/init` shows "This site is already installed" with the way to reinstall (the kickstarter
  or a fresh installation) and writes nothing: no maintenance marker, no setup log run. Installed means
  `var/site_installed.json` exists (the wizard's last page writes it), the site is served normally, or the database
  holds content.
- The warning "Undefined variable $partName" of the setup view.

### Added
- `settings/site.ini [SetupSettings] AllowWizardOnInstalledSite` (default `disabled`, development only): when enabled,
  the wizard runs on an installed site after a confirm page posted back with a one-time token.
- `expSetupWizardGuard` (`kernel/classes/expsetupwizardguard.php`) decides what a wizard request may do.

### Updated
- The wizard's lease is 15 minutes (`expMaintenance::WIZARD_LEASE` = 900, was 1,800), renewed by posted steps only and
  at the end of a long step; the browser's cookie is renewed with it.

### Tests
- `SetupWizardGuardTest` (no database): an installed site refuses the wizard whatever is posted, a GET never starts
  maintenance, it starts only from a posted step or a confirmed POST, the confirm token is used once and only with
  POST, the switch is off by default, and an abandoned wizard's lease runs out.

## 18 October 2026: the help system in the administration

### Added
- The kernel module **exphelp** (`kernel/exphelp/`): the documentation of the repository inside the administration.
  `exphelp/dashboard` (search, the help per audience, categories, featured pages, recent changes, how to report an
  error), `exphelp/viewer/<page>` (a page of `doc/`, rendered with its navigation, contents, previous and next page,
  and an "Edit on GitHub" link), `exphelp/search` (results page, and JSON for the instant search), `exphelp/asset`
  (images of `doc/`) and `exphelp/report` (which tracker, a prefilled issue, safe diagnostics, the private address
  for security issues). Guide: `doc/eng-us/features/6.0/help-system.md`.
- The policy function `exphelp/read`. Administrators have it; Anonymous does not; give it to the Editor role to let
  editors read the help.
- The help shows only git-tracked files below `doc/` (`exphelp.ini [IndexSettings]`); everything else, untracked
  files, `..`, symbolic links, generated HTML and SVG, is a 404. Markdown is rendered safely by
  `expHelpMarkdownRenderer` (raw HTML escaped, unsafe links dropped, images only from `doc/` or `https://`) with
  server-side highlighting (`expHelpHighlighter`) and the code colour tokens `--a4-code-*` (light and dark).
- Context help: a **Help** link in the header of admin4, admin4l and the classic admin opens the page of
  `exphelp.ini [ContextHelp] Map[]` for the current module and view (`expHelpContextRegistry`, fetch
  `exphelp/context`; extensions add entries in their own `exphelp.ini.append.php` or with an
  `expHelpContextProviderInterface`). The link checks its page in a small cached list of served pages, never in
  the help index, so it adds no measurable time to an administration page.
- `./console exp:help index|search|check`: rebuild the index and the search index, search from the command line, and
  report broken links and images in `doc/`.
- New settings file `settings/exphelp.ini`; module stylesheet `stylesheets/modules/help.css` (design/admin and
  design/admin4) and script `javascript/exphelp.js` (instant search, `/` to focus, contents that follow the reading
  position, copy buttons, collapsible sections; no inline script, CSP-clean).
- Translations of every new string (context `kernel/exphelp`) in the complete catalogues.
- Tests: `tests/tests/kernel/classes/help/*` (rendering and XSS, served paths, index, search, context, error report
  without secrets, module policy), `AdminHelpScriptTest` (the script under the mini DOM), no database.
- `exphelp/contents`: every help page as one tree with a filter field; the viewer's side navigation lists the
  complete tree too (`expHelpNavigation`, cached per index, siteaccess and language; folder names in
  `exphelp.ini [NavigationSettings] FolderNames[]`).
- Fixed: the help pages styled `.a4-help` as their page frame, which is also the shared help text under form fields;
  as help.css loads on every admin page, field help texts across the administration were squeezed to one word per
  line. The help frame is `.a4-help-frame` now, and a test keeps help.css off `.a4-help`.
- `exphelp/contents` opens with every folder closed (title and page count); **Expand all** and **Collapse all** in a
  panel fixed near the bottom of the window, above the debug bar or its minimised tab (`--exp-debug-clearance`, set by
  `expdebugbar.css`), mirrored in right-to-left languages; the filter opens the folders of its matches and puts them
  back when emptied; `#exphelp-folder-<path>` opens a folder. Strings in the 11 complete catalogues.
- The help dashboard shows the Exponential logo above "Help center" (the admin4 header's drawing, following the dark
  mode; the same drawing's file in the classic design), named "Exponential", at a fixed size.
- The help navigation (at the side of every help page and on `exphelp/contents`) keeps the filter text and the
  folders the reader opened or closed (by their path; Expand all / Collapse all too) from page to page in the
  browser's localStorage (`exphelp-nav`); the folders of the page in view are always open; a **Clear filter** button
  and Esc empty the filter; without storage the page still works. Expand all / Collapse all now sit sticky at the end
  of the help column, so the Tools sidebar can no longer cover them.
- The help navigation's stored folders and filter are applied before the page is first drawn (the script opens each
  stored folder as the page is read; a stored filter keeps the tree hidden, with its room, until it is applied), and
  help page images carry their width and height, so neither moves the page (layout shift). In the classic design the
  page frame clips with `overflow: clip` instead of `hidden`, so sticky controls such as Expand all / Collapse all
  stay in the window.
- Search and browse addresses with view parameters (`/exphelp/search/(q)/<words>/(category)/<id>/(audience)/<id>`,
  `(type)`, `(offset)`, `(limit)`); without `(q)` the page lists every page the filters allow. Old `?q=` addresses
  and the search form without scripts are redirected once to the canonical address; the JSON of the instant search
  accepts both forms.
- The header's "?" opens a Help menu in admin4, admin4l, the classic admin and AdminUI (search, the Help center right
  below it, help for this page, related topics, quick links; `exphelp.ini [ContextHelp] MenuTopics`, `MenuLinks[]`, fetch `exphelp/context_menu`);
  the Help center is linked from the user menu, the footer, the dashboard and the Setup overview. In the classic admin
  and AdminUI the menu is a native disclosure; Escape closes it and gives the focus back to its button. The root requires
  exp_adminui 1.0.0.48 and ezwt 6.0.12, whose website toolbar "?" opens the Help center for a signed-in editor.
- Print and PDF: a help page prints without the administration around it, and "Download as PDF"
  (`exphelp/pdf/<page>`, `./console exp:help pdf`) draws it with the kernel's own PDF library
  (`exphelp.ini [PdfSettings]`).
- The help states its license, the GNU Free Documentation License 1.3 or later: `doc/eng-us/LICENSE-GFDL-1.3.txt`,
  `doc/eng-us/README-LICENSE.md`, a notice on every help page, its print view and PDF, and a Free documentation block on the
  dashboard (`exphelp.ini [License]`).
- The release history (3.x to 6.x) and the branch history are shown as tables per series with a filter
  and sortable columns (`expHelpRecordRenderer`).
- Help pages: developing with Git and growing from user to developer (with a dashboard band), changing the
  administrator password, the default administrator password per install method, and security hardening.
- Help pages on the supported databases (SQLite 3, the default, then PostgreSQL, MongoDB, MariaDB, Oracle and MySQL;
  Microsoft SQL Server is possible with additional work) and on the supported operating systems; the installation
  guides state the same order. `composer.json` suggests `ext-sqlite3` as the default database and lists the others
  in that order.
- `php bin/php/addexphelpreadpolicy.php` (`./console exp:help-grant`) gives the Editor role `exphelp/read` on an
  existing installation.
- **Examples** (`doc/eng-us/examples/`, category "Examples", featured on the dashboard): web server configurations for
  Apache (`.htaccess`, virtual host), nginx, Caddy, FrankenPHP, LiteSpeed, IIS, PHP-FPM and Velocity, each with the
  routing of `.htaccess_root` and checked (syntax, and test servers for Apache and nginx); a cookbook of fourteen
  worked tasks; and about 2 400 reference pages generated from the code by `./console exp:help:examples`
  (template operators and functions, fetch functions, module views, policies, INI settings per block, datatypes,
  workflow events and triggers, cronjobs, console commands, extension points). `--check` reports pages that are not
  current. `doc/eng-us/examples/htaccess` and `doc/eng-us/examples/ezpublish.conf` were rewritten and are free documentation now.
- `exphelp.ini`: the category `examples`, the page type `generated` (`TypeBoost[generated]=0.5`, left off the
  audience cards) and `[NavigationSettings] FoldedFolders[]`: a folded folder shows its overview and page count and
  lists its pages only while a page inside is open, so the generated pages add no links to the other pages.
- Two more audiences of the help, **Project managers** (`project-manager`) and **Sales & marketing**
  (`sales-marketing`): their dashboard cards link a start page, the search filters, the page pills and the
  categories **Project leadership** (`doc/eng-us/help/leadership/`) and **Sales and marketing** (`doc/eng-us/help/business/`)
  know them. Help pages for both: planning, scoping, hosting choices, checklists, quality gates, releases, risks,
  Git teams, templates and measures for project managers; positioning, feature highlights, factual comparisons,
  demo scripts, objections, licensing, brand rules and the community for sales and marketing; and shared pages on
  working together, becoming a community leader and a reading path. `exphelp.ini [AudienceSettings]` and
  `[CategorySettings]` list them; installations with their own audience list in an override add the two ids there.
- Eight community audiences of the help: **Partners** (`partner`), **Advocates** (`advocate`), **Designers**
  (`designer`), **Hosting companies** (`hosting`), **Application creators** (`app-creator`), **Website creators**
  (`website-creator`), **Supporters** (`supporter`) and **Sponsors** (`sponsor`), each with a dashboard card, a
  search filter and a page pill, and their folders under `doc/eng-us/help/` (`partners/`, `advocates/`, `designers/`,
  `hosting/`, `creators/app-creators/`, `creators/website-creators/`, `supporters/`, `sponsors/`; pages in
  `extension-development/` are for developers and designers). New categories with an introduction each:
  Partnership, Advocacy and community, Theme design, Templates, Extension development, Theme extensions,
  Publishing and distribution, Selling themes and services, Maintenance life cycle, Hosting, Building applications,
  Building websites, Supporting Exponential and Sponsorship. Installations with their own audience list in an
  override add the new ids there.
- Landing pages for every audience of the help, `exphelp/audience/<audience>`, opened from the dashboard's audience
  cards. They are built from blocks set in `exphelp.ini [LandingSettings]` and `[Landing_<audience>]`: a hero with a
  summary, a search box and the start page, the reading path, info boxes, a search button per category, key pages,
  recently updated pages and related audiences. The developers' page has a paged log of the latest commits to
  Exponential, read by the server from the GitHub feed with a timeout and a 10-minute cache and kept when GitHub
  cannot be reached (`[CommitFeedSettings]`). A **Support Exponential** block at the bottom of the dashboard and on
  the supporters' and sponsors' pages asks for monthly sponsorships of 7x and of the Exponential Foundation through
  GitHub Sponsors (`[SupportSettings]`).
- Two more audiences of the help, each with a dashboard card, a search filter, a page pill and a landing page:
  **Trainers** (`trainer`, category Training, `doc/eng-us/help/trainers/`) and **Press, media and influencers** (`press`,
  category Press and media, `doc/eng-us/help/press/`). The dashboard's "Grow from user to developer" band has a fifth step,
  **Contribute extensions back**, shown once its page `doc/eng-us/help/contribute-extensions-back.md` is in the help.
- The help cache no longer grows with every change of the documentation: rebuilding the index removes the older
  indexes and the navigation trees and rendered pages that belonged to them (one stale index is about 5 MB). A new
  category, **The Exponential project** (`project`, `doc/eng-us/help/project/`), holds the pages about the project itself.
- Two more audiences of the help with dashboard cards, search filters, pills and landing pages: **Businesses**
  (`businesses`, category Exponential for businesses `business-solutions`, `doc/eng-us/help/businesses/`) and
  **Governments** (`government`, category Public sector `public-sector`, `doc/eng-us/help/government/`); and the category
  **Two ways to build** (`build-two-ways`, `doc/eng-us/help/build-two-ways/`). The help search weighs the trainers' lessons
  below the editor and administrator pages they teach (`[SearchSettings] AudienceBoost[trainer]=0.6`), except while
  the search is filtered to trainers.
- One more audience of the help with a dashboard card, a search filter, a pill and a landing page: **Customers**
  (`customer`, category Customer success `customer-success`, `doc/eng-us/help/customers/`), for the organizations and
  people who run their own site on Exponential and their teams; the aliases `customers`, `site-owner` and
  `site-owners` select it too.
- The help search keeps to a time budget: the words of each title are stored in the search index (format 4) instead
  of being split and stemmed again for every hit of every query, and a test (`expHelpSearchBudgetTest`, no database)
  fails when a query over 7 000 generated pages takes more than 300 ms of CPU time at the 95th percentile.

### Fixed
- The search form sent without scripts redirected to itself until the browser gave up; the view now sends its
  redirect without the query string.
- `exp:help check` reported a backslash-escaped `\[x\](y)` as a broken link; it reads it as text, as the renderer does.

## 18 October 2026: quick location buttons on the upload page

  place in the content tree. It finds the well-known places from a short key and content.ini (Websites, Media, Users,
  a node id, `remote:<node remote id>`, a URL alias path), never from fixed node ids, and builds a row of the places
  right below one of them with the current one and the one it lies in marked, filtered by a check the page gives.

  buttons or links, the current one filled (`aria-pressed`, `aria-current` or `.a4-btn-primary`), the one the current
  place lies in outlined (`.is-within`), in light, dark and forced colors.

  then the sites and the other folders of Media, only where the user may create the classes uploaded files become;
  one click makes a place the target. Required version: ezmultiupload ~6.1.8.

- The dashboard's **Upload files** button (admin4 and admin4l) opens the upload page at Media > Images where the user
  can upload there, else at Media (ezmultiupload 6.1.8, `ezmultiupload.ini [DashboardSettings] UploadTargetList[]`,
  fetch function `dashboard_target`), so uploads land in the Images folder first. The Admin UI's dashboard does the

  (`expContentCheckScope::presetSource()`, `presetNodeID()`, `presetLabel()` and `siteRow()` keep their names and
  answers, and a preset can now also be `remote:<node remote id>`). Its styles moved from `.a4-setup-scope` in
  `stylesheets/modules/setup.css` to the shared `.a4-quickpick`; a site that styled `.a4-setup-scope` renames it.
- Required versions: ezmultiupload ~6.1.8 (exp_adminui ~1.0.0.44 includes 1.0.0.43). The quick location buttons' scripts are tested
  under the mini DOM (`expMultiuploadQuickLocationsScriptTest`, QJ-01 to QJ-04, node, no browser).

## 17 October 2026: Manage logs names every log again, 404.log included

Setup > Manage logs (`/setup/logsmanage`) showed every log as a link without a name, marked "Web server, read only",
without its actions: the kernel's `expLogManager::logs()` left out the fields the page reads (`label`, `lines`,
`managed`), which only `expLogManagerFallback` gave.

### Fixed
- `expLogManager::logs()` gives each log its `label` (the `LogLabel[<key>]` of `settings/logviewer.ini`, else the file
  name), its `lines` (about) and `managed`, as `expLogManagerInterface` now documents for every implementation.
- A key of digits only (`404` of `var/log/404.log`) is an integer as soon as PHP uses it as an array key. The log
  viewer keeps it the string it is everywhere: `logs()` of both managers, `select()`, `plan()` (also for every log)
  and `apply()` name it `"404"`, `expLogFileLocator::resolveKey()` and `labelOf()` also take the integer, and Log health
  asks for its settings and builds its link with the string. The key is not renamed: `/setup/logs/view/404` stays.
- Log health showed "Array" for the largest size and the archives kept of every log: `expLogHealth::settings()` now
  reads the `{value, source}` entries of `expLogManager::effectiveSettings()` as well as the flat limits.

### Tests
- `expLogNumericKeyTest` (no database): the key `404` through the settings, the locator (also a rotation), both
  managers (fields, label, plan and apply), the permalink, Log health and the digest settings.

## 18 October 2026: every country of a translated language gets its translation; fallback chains

Merged pull request #283 (Germany's, France's, Spain's and the Arab world's other countries; 48 new locales from
CLDR 48) and built on it. Docs: [Translation fallbacks](../../eng-us/languages.md#13-translation-fallbacks),
[Writing a fallback rule](../../eng-us/features/6.0/ui-switch-languages.md#writing-a-fallback-rule).

### Added
- `i18n.ini [TranslationSettings] FallbackLanguages[]` for every country of a language with a translation: the
  German-, French- and Spanish-speaking countries fall back to `ger-DE`, `fre-FR` and `esl-ES`, the Arabic ones to
  `ara-SA` once it exists, and `chi-SG`, `dut-BE`, `ita-CH`, `por-AO`, `por-CV` to theirs. With `esl-AR` the
  administration was English, it is Spanish now.
- 48 locales: Spanish of Paraguay, Costa Rica, Panama, Honduras, El Salvador, Nicaragua, Puerto Rico and Equatorial
  Guinea; French of Luxembourg, Monaco, Haiti, Vanuatu, Mauritius and 20 African countries; Arabic of Bahrain, Oman,
  Libya, Palestine, Yemen, Sudan, Mauritania, Djibouti, Comoros, Somalia, Chad and Eritrea (right to left); German of
  Luxembourg, Liechtenstein and Belgium.
- Fallback chains: a fallback can have a fallback of its own (`esl-AR -> esl-MX -> esl-ES`), tried in order for each
  translation root, with cycles stopped and `FallbackMaxDepth` (default 4).
- `expI18nFallbackResolver`, the one source of truth for the translator, the interface language choice,
  `exp:i18n` and Setup > Interface languages, with rules: `FallbackLanguages[]` first, then the classes of
  `FallbackRules[]` (`expI18nFallbackRuleInterface`, for extensions), then the automatic same-language fallback.
- `AutomaticLanguageFallback=enabled`: a locale with no translation of its own and no configured fallback takes the
  most complete translation of its language (`fre-NC` to `fre-FR`); `AutomaticFallbackExclude[]=eng`. It changes
  nothing for the shipped locales, which all have their line.
- `FallbackMode=merge` (opt-in; default `first`, as before): every translation of the chain is loaded, the most
  specific winning, so `esl-MX` takes the messages it lacks from `esl-ES`.
- `./console exp:i18n fallbacks [--locale=] [--only=] [--format=table|json]`: per locale its chain and the kernel's
  and every extension's coverage; `./console exp:i18n check [--strict]` exits 1 on a cycle, a chain too long, a
  missing or invalid target or a rule that is none (for CI).
- Setup > Interface languages (`setup/languages`, policy `setup/system_info`): the same list in the administration,
  with one locale's chain step by step and every extension; read only.

### Updated
- `expUISwitchLanguageCatalogue` counts a locale without a translation with its whole chain (cache format 5); its
  entries carry `chain`.
- The translation cache's directory includes the fallback settings, and with `TranslationCheckMTime` a changed file of
  a fallback renews the cache of the locales that fall back to it.

### Tests
- `expI18nFallbackResolverTest`, `eZTSTranslatorFallbackChainTest`, `expI18nFallbackReportTest` (no database):
  chains, cycles, depth, the automatic fallback and its exclusions, rule registration and order, problems, first and
  merge in the translator per root, the report, the command's output and exit codes, the page's rows.
  `expUISwitchLanguageProviderTest` and `expLocaleFilesTest` from the pull request.

## 18 October 2026: "New draft" on the edit-conflict page opens the new draft

### Updated
- The edit-conflict page (content/edit of an object that already has a draft in the language) of the admin designs
  admin, admin3 and admin4 (and admin4l, which uses admin4's): "New draft" creates the draft and opens its editor. The
  page posted the hidden field `DoNotEditAfterNewDraft` (since 2004), so content/edit created the draft and redirected
  back to the same page, which looked as if the button did nothing. Not caused by the Content Security Policy: the

  the kernel still honors it.

## 18 October 2026: expui's test page under an enforced Content Security Policy

### Updated
- Required version: expui ~1.0.0.12. exp::sticky's `scrollToStart` focuses the first text field without scrolling
  again (`preventScroll`): on admin4 and admin4l, whose fixed header sets `scroll-padding-top`, the focus scrolled
  the field to the middle of the window and undid the scroll to the start (its expui/test case failed there, with
  or without a policy).
- Its test page (`/expui/test`) reports the three tests that need inline scripts
  (a dialog with `scripts: true`, the classic `onclick` calendar icon by click and by keyboard) as "N/A under
  enforced CSP" with the reason, instead of failing, where the policy's `script-src` has no `'unsafe-inline'`
  (admin4 and admin4l on the admin siteaccesses); they still run where the policy allows inline scripts (the classic
  design's report-only policy). The test view asks `expContentSecurityPolicy::allowsInlineScripts()`.

  kernel's admin designs do (see above).

## 18 October 2026: AdminNeo keeps its page and its policy in a persistent Velocity worker

On Exponential Velocity with persistent workers, `/dse/adminneo` lost AdminNeo's document head and its own
Content-Security-Policy whenever the worker had served `/dse/dashboard` before, so the enforced administration policy
blocked AdminNeo's inline scripts. The dashboard defined `NEO_BASE_URL` (AdminNeo embedded in the dashboard) and ended
on its warning gate or a redirect before it asked for the worker to be replaced. PHP-FPM was not affected.

### Fixed
- Required version: sevenx_dse ~1.1.13. The dashboard asks for the worker to be replaced first, on every path;
  `dse/adminneo` does too, and a worker that already ran AdminNeo answers 503 with a page that reloads itself (no
  script). `dse/adminneo` removes the kernel's report-only policy header, so the page carries AdminNeo's policy only.
  The extension ships `[ContentSecurityPolicy] ReportOnlyViews[]=dse/adminneo`, so the kernel's policy cannot block
  AdminNeo on a server that keeps both headers.

### Tests
- `SevenxDseAdminNeoWorkerStateTest` (no database): a process prepared by the dashboard or one that ran AdminNeo is
  recognised, both views ask for the worker to be replaced before they define a constant or end the request, and the
  reload page has no script.

## 18 October 2026: help maintained as content (phase 1 of the help system plan)

Help topics, manuals, figures and the glossary as content of the site, after the plan
[help-system.md](../../eng-us/specifications/6.0/help-system.md) and the [help style guide](../../eng-us/specifications/6.0/help-style-guide.md).
Guide: [help maintained as content](../../eng-us/features/6.0/help-content.md).

### Added
- `./console exp:help:content` (`bin/php/exphelpcontent.php`) with the actions `install` (`classes`, `tree`,
  `roles`), `check [--fix]`, `seed-glossary`, `seed-topics [--release]`, `report` and `uninstall
  [--with-content]`. `-s <siteaccess>` is required, `--dry-run` changes nothing, every step is idempotent (a second
  run prints "No change."). Nothing runs on its own: an installation creates the model by running `install` once.
- The classes `help_manual`, `help_section`, `help_concept`, `help_task`, `help_reference`, `help_troubleshooting`,
  `help_screenshot`, `help_glossary_entry` (class group Help; text fields are Markdown in `eztext`), the sections
  `help_public` and `help_internal`, the object state group `help_status` (Draft, Review, Released), the folder tree
  below the Media root (`exphelp.ini [ContentSettings] ParentNode`), the roles "Help editor" and "Help release", and
  one `content/read` policy of Anonymous limited to `help_public` and Released.
- The validator of the style guide (`expHelpContentValidator`): required fields per topic type, help_id form and
  uniqueness, one sentence of short description, exactly one numbered list of steps, alt text, glossary words,
  relations of manuals and chapters, review dates, and the section that the audience requires (`--fix` sets it on
  the object only).
- `share/help/seed-topics.json` (the five templates of the style guide and a manual for editors) and
  `share/help/seed-glossary.json`.
- `exphelp/topic/<help_id>`, `exphelp/manual[/<remote_id>]` (policy `exphelp/read`) and the context help JSON
  `exphelp/context/<help_id>`, `exphelp/context/manual/<remote_id>` with ETag (new policy function
  `exphelp/context`). Released topics for who may read them; Draft and Review only for who may edit them, as a
  preview. Anonymous gets `exphelp/context` only with `[ContentSettings] AnonymousContextHelp=enabled` (default
  disabled).
- `expHelpContextPageInterface`: a context help provider implementing it may answer `content/<help_id>`, and the
  Help control of a page opens that topic (`expHelpContentProvider` with `[ContentSettings] ContextHelpIds[]`).

### Tests
- `expHelpContentTest` (no database, 17 cases): the model and roles, help_id parsing, the style guide's templates
  pass, each validator rule, sections from audiences, maps, the topic's Markdown and document, the registry accepts
  `content/...` only from a page provider and only with an address below `/exphelp/`, one Anonymous policy with both
  limitations, ETag handling, policy signatures, and the shipped seed files.

## 18 October 2026: well-known passwords refused everywhere, and forgot-password links expire

### Added
- `expWeakPasswordList` (`kernel/classes/expweakpasswordlist.php`): one list of well-known and common passwords and
  the check, with simple variants (case, digits and symbols at the ends, separators, letter swaps, common endings, a
  word typed twice) and patterns (repeats, keyboard, alphabet and digit runs). More words with
  `site.ini [UserSettings] WeakPasswordList[]` and `WeakPasswordFile`; `WeakPasswordCheck=disabled` switches it off for
  the forms (default `enabled`). See [the upgrade note](../../eng-us/bc/6.0/weak-passwords-and-reset-key-lifetime.md).
- `site.ini [UserSettings] ForgotPasswordKeyLifetime` (default `3600`; `0`: a link works until it is used, as before).

### Updated
- The setup wizard refuses a well-known or common administrator password, also when it is long enough.
- The kickstarter replaces a `kickstart.ini` administrator password that is shorter than `MinPasswordLength` (as it
  already did for an empty or well-known one) with a random one, printed once and written to
  `var/log/initial-admin-password` (mode `0600`); `exp:install` uses the same list.
- `user/password`, registration, the user edit form, `exp:resetuserpassword` and the web services (`expaccount`,
  `expuser`) refuse a well-known or common new password (the rule `not_common` of `expPasswordPolicy`). Existing
  passwords keep working; nobody is refused at sign-in.
- "Forgot your password?" links expire after `ForgotPasswordKeyLifetime`: an expired link is refused with "This link
  has expired." and removed, the mail says how long the link works, and expired keys are removed on every visit of
  the page. The web services use the same lifetime (they had a fixed 24 hours). No database change: the table
  `ezforgot_password` already stores the time.

### Tests
- `expWeakPasswordListTest` and `eZForgotPasswordExpiryTest` (no database); `eZSetupStepsTest`, `InstallPasswordTest`
  and `expPasswordPolicyTest` cover the wizard, the installers, the account form and the console.

## 18 October 2026: clear help titles, a Standards section and the design pattern catalogue

  (placeholders `{title}`, `{name}`, `{fileversion}`, `{dirversion}`) give a clear title and summary to help pages
  whose own is unclear; a front matter title or summary always wins. 47 inherited pages are retitled
  (`doc/eng-us/standards/header.txt` showed as "//", two README pages as "5.90", six changelogs as "CHANGELOG"), and the
  help has no duplicate titles any more. [Help system](../../eng-us/features/6.0/help-system.md#titles-of-unclear-pages).

  standards Exponential follows and its compliance with each, with evidence and known gaps (accessibility, security,
  privacy, the web platform, internationalization, PHP and Composer, coding standards, testing and CI, source control
  and releases, documentation and publishing, APIs and protocols, databases and SQL), and its architectural rules
  (kernel architecture, layering and naming, compatibility, releases and deployment). [Standards](../../eng-us/standards/README.md).
- `php bin/php/exppatterncatalogue.php [--write|--check]`: generates the design pattern catalogue
  (`doc/eng-us/standards/patterns/`) from the source code with PHP's tokenizer, no database: every class, interface, module
  view, policy function, fetch function, template operator, cronjob part, event hook and INI plugin point of the
  kernel, lib/ and the se7enxweb extensions, in 29 pattern categories, with file, description and extension point,
  one page per pattern and per area. The output is reproducible (sorted, no dates). The pages have the new help page
  type `catalogue` (`[SearchSettings] TypeBoost[catalogue]=0.5`, left out of the dashboard's audience cards), so the
  written pages rank first. [Design pattern catalogue](../../eng-us/standards/patterns/README.md).

  wins, pages without an entry keep their title, and every `Titles[]` entry of the shipped settings names a file.
