## 19 October 2026: package fixes: the index address, escaped index data, version comparison, the wizard's tiles

### Updated
- The setup wizard's fallback for an empty `[RepositorySettings] RemotePackagesIndexURL` (package.ini) builds
  `RemotePackagesIndexURLBase` plus `6.0/6.0.15/`, the folder the package server has, through the new
  `ExponentialSDK::packageServerPath()`. Before, it appended the release state (`6.0/6.0.15stable/index.xml`) and got
  `404`. A release that is not stable keeps its state (`6.0/6.0.16alpha1`).
  [Package server settings](../../eng-us/help/site-packages/package-server/package-server-settings.md).
- Security: the Site package step (`setup/init/site_types.tpl`) escapes every field of a remote `index.xml` entry it
  prints: `description` (plain text, line breaks kept; before printed as HTML), `summary`, `version`, `url`, the
  requirement names and versions and the repository address. `eZStepSiteTypes::sanitizeRemotePackageAttributes()`
  keeps a `url` or `thumbnail_url` only when it is an `http://` or `https://` address (no `javascript:`, `data:` or
  `file://`, so neither an image nor a download can use one) and skips an entry whose `name` is not letters, digits,
  `.`, `-` and `_`. The standard package view escapes the maintainer and changelog e-mail addresses in its `mailto:`
  links. [The index.xml format](../../eng-us/help/site-packages/package-server/the-index-xml-format.md).
- `eZPackageVersion::compareFull()` reads a release with a state word, the form the 7x site packages carry:
  `6.0-15-stable` is version `6.0`, release `15`, and meets `min-version="6.0"`. Before, it was read as the
  prerelease `15-stable` of `6.0.0`, lower than `6.0`. A stable word (`stable`, `final`, `release`, `ga`) is no
  prerelease, any other (`rc1`) sorts before the plain release; releases compare as numbers (`9-stable` <
  `15-stable`). New: `eZPackageVersion::splitReleaseState()`, `isStableWord()`.
  [Version numbers](../../eng-us/help/site-packages/requirements/version-numbers.md).
- Package creation wizard (`package/create`, admin4 and classic): each wizard's description fills its tile again;
  it showed one character per line. The cause was the help module's frame rule for the bare `.a4-help` class (a grid
  with `container-type: inline-size`), which also matched the shared hint text under form fields and made it 0px
  wide in a flex row. The help module's rules name the help pages' frame, `.a4-help-frame` (the same fix also
  landed separately).
- `doc/eng-us/install/04-choosing-an-install-method.md`: twelve site packages in the 6.0.15 index (with `cjw_multisite`),
  and the type element written as `package.xml` has it, `<type value="site" />`.

### Tests
- `eZPackageVersionTest` (releases with a state word, stable words after a legacy number),
  `ExponentialSDKPackageServerPathTest`, `SetupRemoteIndexSanitizingTest` and `AdminHelpFrameClassScopeTest`, all
  without a database.

## 19 October 2026: packages keep their install scripts on export, dependencies down the whole chain, more package servers, uninstalling files

### Updated
- `eZPackage::domStructure()` (used when a package is stored or exported) writes `<settings>` (the install scripts of
  a site package) and every attribute of a dependency item (`min-version`, `max-version` ...). Before, a site package
  re-exported from the repository lost both. `exportToArchive()` also takes every other file and folder at the top
  of the package (`eZPackage::extraTopLevelEntries()`, such as the `cjw-*.json` side files of
  `cjw_multisite_democontent`), not hidden ones or symbolic links.
  [package.xml reference](../../eng-us/help/site-packages/basics/package-xml-reference.md#what-is-lost-when-the-kernel-writes-a-package).
- The setup wizard, the kickstarter and `exp:install` download a site package's requirements down the whole chain,
  each package once (`eZStepSiteTypes::downloadDependantPackages()`); before only the direct ones. The Create sites
  step reads the content languages of the whole chain (`eZStepCreateSites::requirementsInInstallOrder()`). The
  `SitePackages` step stays out of `StepTable`, now with the reason in place: it depends on
  `eZSetupFunctionality()`, which no longer exists.
  [Dependencies and install order](../../eng-us/help/site-packages/basics/dependencies-and-install-order.md).
- More package servers: the three setup tools read, after `RemotePackagesIndexURL`, the servers of the new
  `package.ini [RepositorySettings] AdditionalRemotePackagesIndexURLs[]` (empty) and of the lists named by
  `RemotePackagesIndexSources[]`, by default `ezupdate.ini;PackageServerSettings;Servers`, the servers added on
  ezupdate's Package servers page. A package an earlier index lists is kept as it is; only http(s) addresses are
  read; a server that cannot be read is skipped. Empty `RemotePackagesIndexSources[]` to read only `package.ini`.
  [Package server settings](../../eng-us/help/site-packages/package-server/package-server-settings.md#further-package-servers).
- `extension` is a package type of `package.ini [PackageSettings] TypeList`, so the Type limitation of the package
  policies can name extension packages.
- Uninstalling a package removes the files its `ezfile` items created, when they are unchanged, inside the
  installation and one of the package's own files (`eZFilePackageHandler::uninstall()`, install data
  `installed_files`); a file the install replaced or the site changed stays. `ezsql` and `eziniaddon` items say in
  the debug output why they undo nothing.
  [Install items and handlers](../../eng-us/help/site-packages/basics/install-items-and-handlers.md).

### Tests
- `eZPackageSitePackageExportTest`, `eZFilePackageHandlerUninstallTest` and more cases in
  `SetupRemoteIndexSanitizingTest`, all without a database.

- Package server, 6.0.15 index: the four 7x site packages and `cjw_multisite` are listed with the version their
  `package.xml` states, `6.0-15-stable` (the text `eZPackage::getVersion()` returns); they were listed as `1.0.0-0`
  and `6.0.15-0`. The setup wizard compares the index version with an imported copy, so an imported copy now compares
  equal and is kept.
- `sevenx_multisite`, `sevenx_multisite_clean`: the `md5sum` of `logo.png` in `package.xml` matches the file.
- `sevenx_multisite_clean`, `sevenx_multisite_democontent`, `sevenx_multisite_democontent_clean`: `documents/LICENCE`
  names the package itself (it named the package it was copied from). The license is unchanged, GPL 2.0 or later.
- The source repositories `sevenx-ezpackage` and `cjw_multisite-ezpackage` have a README and a `LICENSE` with the full
  GPL version 2 text; `cjw_multisite-ezpackage` has the `packages/build.sh` of the 7x repository; both build scripts
  leave backup copies (`*.bak`, `*.bak.*`) out of every package.
- The package server repository has a `CONTRIBUTING.md` on submitting a site package by pull request
  ([Contribute your site package](../../eng-us/help/site-packages/contribute-your-site-package.md)).

### Removed
- `sevenx_multisite_democontent`: 113 unused backup copies of object files (`object-media-o-<n>.xml.bak.*`).
  Nothing read them; the archive is 355 KB smaller.

### Tests
- The rebuilt archives were imported into a throwaway installation (every `md5sum` checked, no backup copies,
  `LICENCE` names the package) and installed on SQLite with `exp:install` from a local copy of the package server
  with the new index: `sevenx_multisite`, `sevenx_multisite_clean` and `cjw_multisite` (with `ger-DE`) each
  INSTALLED with 0 errors and 0 warnings.

## 19 October 2026: the design extension wizard writes a theme that installs and registers its operators

### Updated
- The `composer.json` of every Setup > RAD extension wizard carries `extra.ezpublish-legacy-extension-name` (next to
  `installer-name`), the key the legacy extension installer reads. Before, a theme named `my_theme` installed into
  `extension/my-theme`, after the dashed package name.
- The design extension wizard's **Template operators** part writes `autoloads/eztemplateautoload.php`, so the
  example operator is registered once the extension is active. Before, only the class and the
  `ExtensionAutoloadPath[]` line were written, and a template using the operator got "unknown operator".
- The design extension wizard writes `FrontendCSSFileList[]` and `FrontendJavaScriptList[]` instead of the deprecated
  `CSSFileList[]` and `JavaScriptList[]`. The generated `page_head.tpl` and `pagelayout.tpl` load both lists, as the
  shipped designs do. The page head, title and script loading were corrected earlier the same week (the generated
  templates load their stylesheets and scripts with `ezcss_load` and `ezscript_load`).
- The designer help ([The design wizard's parts](../../eng-us/help/designers/extensions/design-wizard-parts-reference.md))
  says what is fixed from 6.0.15 on and keeps the hand corrections for themes generated by older wizards.

### Tests
- `expDesignExtensionWizardTest` (no database): the generated `composer.json` installs into a folder named after the
  extension, by the installer's own rule; the operator autoload file is written, and with the extension's autoloads
  directory handed to the template engine the operator is registered and works; `design.ini.append.php` has no
  deprecated keys. `expExtensionWizardBaseTest` checks the new `extra` key.

## 19 October 2026: more corrections in what the RAD wizards write, a view count without a warning, and no blank autoload entry

### Updated
- Template extension wizard: the README and `doc/examples.php` call an operator with its values by position
  (`{$value|op( 3, 'text' )}`); `name=value` in an operator's brackets is refused by the template parser. Template
  functions keep `name=value`. The generated `operatorTemplateHints()` no longer lists `input-as-parameter` twice.
- The READMEs of the template, datatype and settings extension wizards give `php bin/php/ezpgenerateautoloads.php
  --extension`; `--extension` takes no value, and `--extension=<name>` was refused.
- Datatype wizard: the templates go to `design/standard/templates/`, where `DesignExtensions[]` finds them, not to
  `templates/` in the extension root. The edit and collect templates post `_<type>_<column>_` for the storage column
  chosen (the class carries it as `VALUE_VARIABLE`, and `objectAttributeContent()` reads that column), not always
  `_data_text_`. `DataTypeGroup[]`, which nothing reads, is no longer written, and the form no longer asks for a group.
- Module wizard: the templates go to `design/standard/templates/<module>/`, not `design/<extension>/`, which is in no
  design chain. The view script casts only number parameters (`ID`, `NodeID`, `Offset`...) to `int`; text parameters
  stay text and are washed by the template.
- Design extension wizard: the README and `design.ini.append.php` say that a siteaccess has to name the design in
  `SiteDesign` or `AdditionalSiteDesignList[]`; `DesignExtensions[]` alone does not make a site use it.
- Handler wizard: the mail transport stub logs an error and returns `false`, with a TODO, instead of returning `true`
  and sending nothing.
- `eZContentObjectTreeNode::viewCount()` returns 0 for a node without a row in `ezview_counter`, without the warning
  "Trying to access array offset on null".
- The autoload generator no longer takes a method called `class` or `enum` (`function class()`, `$o->class()`) for a
  declaration. `expObjectServices::class()` in extension expservices gave an entry with the blank class name `' '` in
  `var/autoload/ezp_extension.php`.
- [RAD tools](../../eng-us/help/creators/app-creators/rad-tools.md) lists the corrections to make by hand in output from an
  older wizard.

### Tests
- `expRadWizardGeneratedCodeFaultsTest` (no database): one test per fault above, on the generated files.
- `eZContentObjectTreeNodeViewCountTest` (no database): 0 and no warning without a counter row, the count with one.
- `eZAutoloadGeneratorTest`: a class with methods called `class` and `enum` gives one entry.
- `expRadWizardListsTest` and `expModuleWizardTest` follow the operator call and the template path.

## 19 October 2026: the help index looks at its files once every few seconds

### Updated
- The help index looks at the size and time of every listed help file at most once every
  `[IndexSettings] StatFilesInterval` seconds (`exphelp.ini`, default `5`), for every request and process: the
  answer is kept in `signature.php` in the help cache directory. Before, each help request looked at all of them
  (6,195 files on a full installation), about 25 ms behind PHP-FPM and half a second under Velocity, whose file layer
  answers each look. An edited page now shows within the interval; a page added or removed (the git index) and
  `./console exp:help index` are seen at once. `StatFilesInterval=0` keeps the old behavior.
- Together with Exponential Velocity's change of the same day (a file that cannot need the include transform is
  not tokenized: the help index, a 14 MB data file, cost each worker 3 to 5 seconds on its first help request after
  it changed), this removes the two costs that made the help views on Velocity take 1 to 5 seconds where PHP-FPM
  took about half a second.

### Tests
- `expHelpDocumentIndexTest` (no database): HX-08 checks that a look at the files is reused within the interval and
  never after it, that `StatFilesInterval=0` sees an edit at once, that a rebuild always looks again and leaves its
  look for the others, and that a commit is seen at once.

## 19 October 2026: visitors can register on a new installation, and the top folder carries the current welcome text

### Updated
- The base data (`share/db_data.dba`, which the setup wizard and the kickstarter load, and the clean data
  `kernel/sql/common/cleandata.sql` and `kernel/sql/sqlite/cleandata.sql`) gives the Anonymous role the policy
  *User / Register* (policy 336, no limitation). A new installation takes `user/register` out of `PolicyOmitList[]`
  (`PolicyOmitListExclude[]=user/register`), and without the policy its visitors could not register and the login
  pages showed no register link. To close registration, remove the policy from the Anonymous role. An upgraded site
  is not changed. `user/activate`, `user/success` and `user/forgotpassword` stay in `PolicyOmitList[]`.
- The top folder (node 2) of a new installation carries the current *Welcome to Exponential* text in its short
  description and description, with the links written as addresses (`href`), since a new installation has none of
  the link rows of the site the text was written on.
- `share/db_data.dba` gives the Editor role the four policies the clean data files already gave it: *uiswitch /
  language*, *uiswitch / design*, *oauth / authorize* and *exphelp / read* (policies 332 to 335, no limitations). An
  installation through the setup wizard or the kickstarter, which load the `.dba`, gave Editors none of them: no
  interface language or design switch, no OAuth authorization and no help pages. The roles, policies, limitations,
  their values and the role assignments are now the same rows in all three files.
- The search index of the base data (`ezsearch_word`, `ezsearch_object_word_link`, in all three files) indexes the
  top folder's current text, so a new installation finds it without a reindex. The rows were still those of the
  first eZ plain site text. They were generated by the kernel's own `eZSearchEngine` (remove, then add the object)
  in a throwaway SQLite database loaded from the `.dba`, and are word for word the index alpha's live database
  holds for the same text: 1,531 rows for the top folder, 625 words, each word's `object_count` the number of
  objects linking it. The rows of the other objects are unchanged; words used only by the old text are gone.
- Every table now holds the same rows in `share/db_data.dba` and both clean data files:
  - `share/db_data.dba` seeds the content language eng-US "English (American)" (id 2), as the clean data files
    did. The setup wizard added it itself when the table was empty; it looks the language up first
    (`eZContentLanguage::fetchByLocale()`, and `addLanguage()` does too), so a seeded row is used and never
    doubled, and id 2 is the id every language mask of the base data is built on.
  - The site meta data (object 54, `ezinisetting`) in both clean data files names the Exponential Foundation and
    the Exponential keywords, as the `.dba` did, instead of eZ Systems.
  - Both clean data files say `ezpublish-version` 6.0.15stable (they said 6.0.10stable and 6.0.6stable), the value
    the `.dba` already had, the 6.0.15 database updates write and the upgrade checks (`ExponentialSDK::version()`,
    the LoveStack check) expect for this release.

### Tests
- `expBaseDataSeedTest` (no database): the Anonymous role has `user/register` without a limitation in all three
  files, policy ids are unique, and the top folder's two texts are the same in all three, well-formed, with every
  link an address. BD-03: every table of the `.dba` holds the same rows in both clean data files, which have no
  table the `.dba` lacks; the Editor role has the four policies in the `.dba`; the base data says 6.0.15stable and
  has the one language eng-US, id 2. BD-04: every
  word's `object_count` matches its links, the top folder's placements run without a gap with the previous and next
  words of each slice of 1,000 that `eZSearchEngine` writes, and each indexed word comes from its text.

## 19 October 2026: demo mode for public demos (EXP_DEMO_MODE=1)

### Added
- Demo mode: the environment variable `EXP_DEMO_MODE=1` (only the exact value `1`; never an INI setting or a request
  parameter) marks an installation as a public demo whose administrator sign-in is published.
  `expDemoMode` (`kernel/classes/expdemomode.php`) reads it on every call, so persistent Velocity and FrankenPHP
  workers follow the environment they have. See [the upgrade note](../../eng-us/bc/6.0/demo-mode.md).
- The template operator `exp_demo_mode()` (`expDemoModeOperator`) and the banner
  `design/{admin,admin4}/templates/parts/exp_demo_mode_banner.tpl`, shown on every page of admin4, admin4l and the
  classic admin while demo mode is on (the shared component `.a4-notice.is-warn`, outside the cache-blocks).
- The audit event `system.demo.mode` (warning, always on) and a `demo/mode` line in `var/log/security.log`, once per
  process that starts with demo mode on.
- Help pages: [Demo mode](../../eng-us/help/demo-mode.md), [Demo mode for hosts](../../eng-us/help/hosting/security/demo-mode-for-hosts.md)
  and [Demo mode for developers](../../eng-us/help/extension-development/part-8-reference/demo-mode-for-developers.md).

### Updated
- In demo mode the password of an administrator (unlimited access to every module and function) cannot be changed or
  reset by any path: `user/password`, "Forgot your password?" (no key and no mail; the same answer for every address;
  an old link is refused), the user account in content/edit, `exp:resetuserpassword` (also with
  `--allow-root-user`), the web services (`expaccount.reset`, `expaccount.changePassword`, `expaccount.forgotRequest`,
  `expuser.setPassword`: HTTP 403), the operations `user/password` and `user/forgotpassword`, and as the last line
  `eZUser::store()`, which keeps an administrator's stored password. Each refusal shows "This installation is a public
  demo: the passwords of administrators cannot be changed or reset." and is recorded with the reason `demo_mode`.
  Other accounts keep every rule.
- In demo mode the installers (setup wizard, kickstarter, `exp:install` and the demo installers built on them) accept
  a well-known administrator password of any length (`publish`, `publishing`), so a demo installs the password it
  publishes; an empty one is still refused, and every other account keeps `MinPasswordLength`. The setup wizard's
  administrator page says that demo mode is on. The
  installation sets the administrator password inside `expDemoMode::installing()` and
  `expDemoMode::settingInstallationAdministratorPassword()`, the only place `eZUser::validatePassword()` accepts a
  short one.
- Installation, security hardening (13.6 "Demo mode must be off" and the go-live checklist), the default
  administrator password and the demo pages of the help describe demo mode.

### Tests
- `expDemoModeTest` (no database): the value rules, the order getenv then `$_SERVER` and never the request, no
  caching, who is an administrator, the store guard's decision, the `installing()` scope, the installers' relaxation
  and its limits, the once-per-process record, the operator, the banner in the three pagelayouts, every password path,
  the audit catalogue and the catalogues. `expAuditRecordTest` counts the new event.

## 20 October 2026: the documentation moves to doc/eng-us/, and the help is read in the reader's language

### Renamed
- The documentation moves from `doc/` to `doc/eng-us/`, every file with its relative path and its history (`git mv`),
  so each language of the documentation has a directory of its own (`doc/<language>/`). `doc/changelogs/` stays: it is
  not translated. A short `doc/README.md` lists the language directories for readers of the repository.

### Updated
- Every reference to the moved files follows them: the root files' links, the templates' guide and GitHub links, the
  settings (`exphelp.ini` path rules, titles and license rules, the `Doc=` entries of the other INI files), code
  comments, command line scripts, tests, the CI workflows, `composer.json`, the translations whose source names a
  guide, and the file manifest. The links between the guides and the changelogs are rewritten for their new places.
  The released changelogs and the history pages keep the paths of their time.
- The help center reads the interface language (`site.ini [RegionalSettings] Locale`, set by the language switch) and
  shows each page in it: `exphelp.ini [DocLanguages] Map[]` maps `eng-US` and `eng-GB` to `en/us` and `ger-DE` to `de`,
  and a locale without an entry uses its language's (`ger-AT` reads German). A page that is not translated yet is shown
  in English with a translated note. Page ids are the paths below `doc/eng-us/` in every language, so every help address
  of before the move keeps working and shows the same page in each language; an address that names a file
  (`/exphelp/viewer/en/us/<id>`, a translated path) is redirected to the id. Translated file names are listed in
  `doc/<language>/.paths.json`. Each language has its own index, search, navigation and rendered pages
  (`lang-<language>/` in the help cache); the search finds the reader's language and the English pages not translated
  yet. Links inside a page are resolved against the file it was read from, as on GitHub.
- `./console exp:help index` builds every language's index and search and reports how much of each is translated;
  `--language=<directory>` limits it, and makes `search`, `show`, `check` and `pdf` read that language.

### Added
- `expHelpLanguage`, the languages of the documentation and the path maps; `exphelp.ini [DocLanguages]` (`Default`,
  `Directories[]`, `Map[]`, `Names[]`).
- [Translated documentation](../../eng-us/bc/6.0/translated-documentation.md): what moved, what to change in your own
  links, settings and scripts, and how a translator adds a language.
- [Reading the help in your language](../../eng-us/help/reading-help-in-other-languages.md), a help page.

### Tests
- `expHelpTranslatedDocumentationTest` (no database): TD-01 no tracked file below `doc/` outside a language
  directory, `doc/changelogs/` and `doc/README.md`; TD-02 no tracked text file names a path of before the move (the
  history excepted); TD-03 to TD-05 the locale map, the shipped settings and the path map; TD-06 to TD-09 the index
  per language with its fallback, one id per page in every language, links, the search per language and the
  classification of a translation. The help tests follow the new paths.

### Operator note
- Links or scripts of your own that name a documentation file need `en/us/` after `doc/`
  (`doc/eng-us/INSTALL.md`); settings overrides that name one in a path rule need it too. Help addresses and page ids
  do not change. Run `./console exp:help index --rebuild` once after the deploy.

## 20 October 2026: the project files (README, security policy, contributing) in the help

### Added
- The project files of the repository are help pages: the tracked root `*.md` (`README.md`, `SECURITY.md`,
  `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `COPYRIGHT.md`, `LICENSE.md`) and the `README.md` of `bin/`, `design/<design>/` and
  `kernel/`. Their ids are
  `project/<path in the repository>` (`/exphelp/viewer/project/README.md`; `/exphelp/viewer/README.md` redirects
  there). They have the category **Project files** and the type **Project file** (dashboard, search filters), a
  place at the top of the page tree of the contents page and every help page (no folder), audiences by
  `[ProjectFiles] AudienceRules[]` (developers and administrators at least), and the title of their first heading.
- `expHelpProjectFiles` and `exphelp.ini [ProjectFiles]` (`Enabled`, `Include[]`, `Exclude[]`, `Category`, `Type`,
  `Audiences[]`, `AudienceRules[]`, `LicenseName`, `LicensePage`). Test fixtures and the copies of other projects (a
  bundled JavaScript library's README) are excluded by default.
- [Project files](../../eng-us/help/project-files.md), a help page; the section "Project files" of
  [The help system](../../eng-us/features/6.0/help-system.md#project-files).

### Updated
- Links of the documentation to a project file (`../../../../README.md`) open it in the help instead of leaving the
  link out, and the project files' links into `doc/eng-us/` open the help pages; both are resolved against the file, as
  on GitHub.
- The project files are English only, like the changelogs: a reader of another language sees them with the note that
  the page is not translated. The license notice under them names the license of the software
  (GPL 2.0 or later), not the documentation license.

### Security
- Only files git tracks can be project files: the list comes from the git index, `git ls-files` or the release
  manifest, never from the directory, so the private notes an installation keeps in its root without committing them
  are never shown, searched, linked or redirected to, whatever a setting asks for. A tracked file in a place
  `.gitignore` or `.git/info/exclude` ignores is refused, and so are anything but Markdown and every path below
  `doc/`, `extension/`, a `tests/` directory, `var/`, `vendor/`, `settings/override/`, `settings/siteaccess/` and
  `node_modules/`, and every hidden file or directory (`.github/` included). These rules are not settings.

### Tests
- `expHelpProjectFilesTest` (no database; fixtures are real git repositories): PF-01 the patterns; PF-02 the names
  and the paths that are never project files; PF-03 untracked root notes are never listed, indexed, searched or
  linked, from the git index, `git ls-files` and the manifest alike, even when `Include[]` names them; PF-04 ignored
  locations; PF-05 to PF-09 ids, classification, links both ways, the language fallback and the navigation; PF-10
  this installation's tracked root files and the shipped settings; PF-11 the license; PF-12 `Enabled=disabled`; PF-13
  the translations.

### Operator note
- Run `./console exp:help index --rebuild` once after the deploy.

## 20 October 2026: the contributing guidelines state the copyright assignment (CLA)

### Updated
- Contributing guidelines state the copyright assignment (CLA): `CONTRIBUTING.md` has a section
  [Copyright assignment (CLA)](../../../CONTRIBUTING.md#copyright-assignment-cla). 7x and the Exponential Foundation
  require a signed Contributor Licensing Agreement for every code contribution to the core kernel, whether it is sent
  as a pull request, a patch, an issue or by mail, before it is merged; users' own custom extensions are not
  affected. The README, the pull request template (new, `.github/PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE.md`, with a CLA checkbox),
  the contribution and pull request pages of the help, the glossaries and the FAQs link to it.
- The contributing guidelines state plainly that a signed CLA gives control of a contribution to 7x: the section is
  now called "Copyright and control of your contribution (CLA)" and opens with what a contributor gives up (a
  perpetual, worldwide, irrevocable, royalty-free license, patents included, under which 7x may use, change,
  sublicense, relicense and distribute the contribution without asking or paying, and which cannot be withdrawn),
  with only a short note that formally the CLA is a license (section 4 of the agreement). The FAQ answers "Do I give
  up control of my code?" with yes. Every page that mentions the CLA says the same.
- The "Code Reviews" section of `CONTRIBUTING.md` warns that 7x reviews in many more rounds than usual, sometimes
  hundreds of revisions, and says how to get through faster (small focused pull requests, the coding standards, tests,
  answers in the pull request, a branch rebased on `main`). The pull request template and the pages that walk through
  a pull request link to it. Its link to the missing `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md` now points at the project's conduct page.
- Contributing guidelines state that kernel contributions are a copyright assignment to 7x: by signing the CLA and
  submitting a contribution to the core kernel, the contributor assigns (transfers) its copyright to 7x, which
  becomes the copyright owner and may use, change, relicense and distribute it in any way; the contribution cannot be
  withdrawn and no conditions can be set. Users' own custom extensions are not affected. The section is called
  "Copyright assignment (CLA)" again (`#copyright-assignment-cla`; the anchor of the previous name still works), the
  FAQ answers "Do I give away my copyright?" with yes, and every page that mentions the CLA says the same. The
  agreement form on the CLA page is being updated to the assignment.
- Contributors squash and rebase their pull requests: 7x requires a pull request to be squashed into logical
  commits (usually one) and rebased onto `main` while it is open (`git fetch origin`, `git rebase -i origin/main`,
  `git push --force-with-lease` to the pull request branch only), so the merge is clean. `CONTRIBUTING.md` ("Code
  Reviews") states it with the commands, the pull request template has a checkbox for it, and the pages that said a
  contributor's commits are kept as written with fixes on top now say that `main`'s history is never rewritten after
  the merge and later fixes come as new pull requests ([Continuous integration](../../eng-us/bc/6.0/ci.md), the
  source control standard, the pull request pages and the press, partner, advocate and leadership pages).
- `CONTRIBUTING.md` is titled "Exponential Contributors Guidelines".

## 20 October 2026: help notices and client help_ids checked, the help standards, the help classes as a package

The [help system plan](../../eng-us/specifications/6.0/help-system.md) and the
[help style guide](../../eng-us/specifications/6.0/help-style-guide.md) (pull request #288) compared with what the
help does, item by item: [help maintained as content, compared with the specifications](../../eng-us/features/6.0/help-content.md#compared-with-the-specifications).

### Added
- `./console exp:help:content check` reports the notice levels of the style guide (IEC/IEEE 82079-1): an Attention or
  Data protection notice outside the step of a task (`notice.position`), after the action in its step
  (`notice.before-action`), a second notice in one step (`notice.per-step`), a notice of more than three sentences
  (`notice.too-long`).
- The same command reports the help_ids that clients call and no topic has (`client.help-id-unknown`), or whose topic
  is not Released (`client.help-id-not-released`), from `exphelp.ini [ContentSettings] ContextHelpIds[]` and the new
  `ClientHelpIds[]`.
- `./console exp:help:content package -s <siteaccess> [--output=<dir>] [--version=]`: the eight help classes as the
  content class package `exp_help_classes` (directory and `.ezpkg`); `verify-package --file=<.ezpkg or directory>`
  compares a package with the classes of the release without a database (`expHelpContentPackage`). Build and handoff:
  [the help classes as a package](../../eng-us/specifications/6.0/help-content-package.md).
- [Help standards](../../eng-us/specifications/6.0/help-standards.md): what the help takes from DITA 1.3,
  IEC/IEEE 82079-1:2019, ISO/IEC/IEEE 26514:2022, ISO 3864-2:2016, EPUB Accessibility 1.1 and WCAG 2.2 AA, what it
  does differently and how far it goes (no conformance claimed). Pull request #288 announced the page; it was not
  in the merge.

### Updated
- The help viewer shows `> **Attention:**`, `> **Data protection:**` and `:::data-protection` as callouts with that
  title; Data protection has its own icon.

### Tests
- `expHelpContentTest` (notices, client help_ids), `expHelpMarkdownRendererTest` (the notice callouts),
  `expHelpContentPackageTest` (the package's definition, a package and an `.ezpkg` compared with the schema), no

## 20 October 2026: the documentation of the active extensions in the help center

### Added
- The help center shows the documentation of every active extension, found when it looks
  (`exphelp.ini [IndexSettings] ActiveExtensionDocs=enabled`): the files of `extension/<name>/doc/` that the
  extension's repository tracks (or its `share/filelist.md5` lists) and its README, CHANGELOG, LICENSE, INSTALL, USAGE
  and COPYING at the top of the extension (`ExtensionTopFiles[]`), nothing else: no PHP or other source file, no
  settings, no untracked file, nothing of an inactive extension, and a text file without a suffix that starts like a
  program is left out. The pages have the ids `ext/<name>/<path>`, the type "Extension documentation" and the category
  and navigation folder **Extensions**, and are read, searched and printed like every help page.
- `exphelp/extensions`: the extensions with documentation, and `exphelp/extensions/<name>`, one extension's index with
  its pages by folder and a search limited to them. The search takes `(extension)/<name>` (view parameter, form and
  JSON). The contents page links to the index; the breadcrumb of an extension's page goes through it.
- `expHelpDocumentIndex::extensions()`, `expHelpAllowlist::extensions()` and `isExtensionTopFile()`.

### Updated
- `ExtensionDocDirs[]` now adds extensions whose documentation is shown whether they are active or not. An extension's
  page has no "Edit on GitHub" link (`EditBaseURL` names the installation's repository).
- `exphelp.ini [ContentSettings]` carries a commented `ClientHelpIds[]` example.

### Tests
- `expHelpExtensionDocsTest` (no database): a fixture installation with extensions in their own repositories and in
  the installation's, active and inactive; the pages shown, the files refused (source, settings, scripts without a
  suffix, untracked, inactive, traversal), ids both ways, the overview, the per-extension list, the search filter, the
  viewer's breadcrumb and edit link. `expHelpSearchViewParametersTest` covers `(extension)`.

### Operator note
- After the deploy, run `./console exp:help index --rebuild` once. An extension's documentation shows only while it is
  active and only when its files are tracked by its repository or listed in its `share/filelist.md5`. expai 0.0.1.14
  moves its own help pages here (`expai/help` redirects); release it after this.

## 20 October 2026: the user manual, one question per page

The [user manual](../../eng-us/help/manual/README.md) of the administration with the Admin 4 design: more than fifty
pages, each answering one question with who may do it (the role or policy), numbered steps, what can go wrong and the
deeper pages to read next.

### Added
- The admin tabs: the Store dashboard, order statuses, archiving orders, VAT, discounts, currencies and the products
  overview; what the Design tab holds; the Setup overview and why a tile is missing; who changed what in the Audit
  console; My account, drafts, pending items and notification settings; switching the administration design; turning
  on two-step sign-in, and what an administrator does when a user lost their phone.
- The daily tasks: translating and finding what is not translated, sending for approval and approving, following
  content, finding content, the trash, the order of sub items, changing the class and the owner, tags and the tag tree,
  images, links, tables and embeds in the Online Editor, the SEO title and description, the sitemap, form submissions,
  object states, a page with Layouts, a newsletter and the AI assistant (which points to the extension's own manual).
- The tasks of administrators and site managers: edit rights on one section, disabling and unlocking accounts, which
  cache to clear, broken links, URL aliases and wildcards, adding a content language, the log viewer, the content
  checks, extensions and system information, RSS export and import, and API access for another application.
- Twenty-five Admin 4 screenshots (light mode, WebP), taken with a throwaway account; names, addresses and keys are
  replaced by examples.
- The help category **User manual** (`manual`, `exphelp.ini [CategorySettings]`, translated), first after Getting
  started; the folder's audience is editors, administrators and users.
- Pages that already answered a question of the list are linked from the manual's index instead of written again
  (adding a product, changing your own and another user's password).


## 20 October 2026: help maintained as content leaves Exponential as DITA 1.3

Guide: [help maintained as content](../../eng-us/features/6.0/help-content.md#dita-export).

### Added
- `./console exp:help:content dita -s <siteaccess> [--manual=<remote_id>] [--output=<dir>] [--dry-run] [--json]`
  (also `export --format=dita`, the plan's name): the Released manuals as DITA 1.3 bookmaps with a DITAVAL file of
  their audiences, the Released topics as `concept`, `task`, `reference` and `troubleshooting` topics named by their
  help_id, the chapters, the figures, the glossary as `glossentry` topics, and a map of the whole topic pool. Only
  changed files are written and files of an earlier export that are gone are removed, so the directory can be a git
  repository of the help's history; a second run prints "No change.". The command checks the result: well-formed
  XML, the DITA 1.3 DOCTYPE of each root element, unique topic ids, and every reference resolves (exit 1 otherwise).
- **Download DITA** on a manual's page in the help and **Download all as DITA** on the list of manuals
  (`exphelp/manual[/<remote_id>]?format=dita`): the same export of what the user may read, as a zip file, checked
  before it is sent.
- `expHelpContentDita` (the mapping, the check, writing and zip) and `expHelpDitaMarkup` (the help's Markdown HTML as
  DITA elements: bold UI labels as `uicontrol`, callouts as typed notes, tables as `simpletable`, one numbered list of
  steps as `steps` with `cmd` and `info`).

### Tests
- `expHelpContentDitaTest` (no database, HCD-01 to HCD-11): a fixed set of topics, a chapter, a manual, a figure and
  glossary entries through the export; the files, DOCTYPEs and references, each topic type, ids and filter
  attributes, Released only, the bookmap and DITAVAL, informal steps, figures, the glossary and pool map, one manual,
  the check finding broken files, an idempotent write that keeps a `.git` directory, and the markup. The export of the
  test set and of the shipped seed topics is valid against the OASIS DITA 1.3 DTDs (`xmllint --valid` with the
  DITA-OT catalog).

## 20 October 2026: the e-books of help manuals are stored as content and rebuilt on release

Guide: [help maintained as content](../../eng-us/features/6.0/help-content.md#stored-e-books).

### Added
- `./console exp:help:content ebooks -s <siteaccess> [--manual=<remote_id>] [--dry-run] [--json]`: the EPUB and PDF
  of every Released manual stored as an object of the class file below E-books (`help-ebooks`), remote_id
  `help-ebook-<manual remote_id>-<format>`, state Released, section `help_public` for a public manual and
  `help_internal` otherwise (on the object only), so the help's policies decide who may download it. A new build is a
  new version of the object; the same book changes nothing ("No change." on a second run).
- A release rebuilds the books: setting an object of the help to Released builds the stored e-books of every
  Released manual that contains it again (`expHelpContentEbooks::onStateAssign()`, called from
  `eZContentOperationCollection::updateObjectState()`; `exphelp.ini [ContentSettings] EbooksOnRelease`, default
  `enabled`). A book that cannot be made is logged; the release stands.
- The book of a public manual carries only public objects, whoever releases (`expHelpContentEditionProvider`).
- The manual's page in the help lists its stored e-books (file, version, size, date) for who may read them.

### Updated
- The entries of an EPUB are dated by the book's content (the newest manual or topic change), and so is its
  `dcterms:modified`: the same content gives the same file.

### Tests
- `expHelpContentEbooksTest` (no database, HCB-01 to HCB-05): the public and internal editions, the manuals a release
  rebuilds, remote_ids, the EPUB dated by its content, and the release hook. In a sandbox copy of a site: a topic set
  to Review leaves the books, set to Released stores a new version of each.

## 20 October 2026: the order of the top-level pages of the help's page tree

### Added
- `exphelp.ini [TreeSettings] PageOrder[<page id>]=<integer>` sets the place of a page in the help's page tree, the
  folder lists and the category lists, and wins over the page's front matter `order`; the project files and plain text
  pages, which cannot carry front matter, can be placed too. The key is the page id, the same in every language.
  Exponential ships the order of the top level: the README, installing, languages, branch history, release history,
  then contributing, copyright, the documentation license, the GFDL, the GPL, the security policy, and the glossary
  last. `branch-overview.md` and `README-LICENSE.md` no longer carry an `order` of their own.

  without front matter, other pages keep theirs, invalid entries are left out, and the shipped `exphelp.ini` lists
  the same order as the built-in default, for pages that exist.

## 20 October 2026: the documentation's language directories are named by the locale codes

A language directory of the documentation is now the Exponential locale code of its language in lower case, so the
interface, the content languages and the documentation name a language the same way and a new translation needs no
mapping. Upgrade notes: [Translated documentation](../../eng-us/bc/6.0/translated-documentation.md), "Upgrading a
site that links the old directories".

### Renamed
- `en/us/` to `eng-us/` (the original, `eng-US`) and `de/` to `ger-de/` (German, `ger-DE`) below `doc/`, with
  `.paths.json` and `.dirnames.json`, as pure renames (`git mv`) in a commit of their own; `git log --follow` reaches
  the history before it. `doc/changelogs/` and `doc/README.md` stay where they are. Page ids do not change, so
  help addresses, `[TreeSettings] PageOrder[]`, context help and bookmarks keep working.
- The per-language help caches: `var/<site>/cache/exphelp/lang-<directory>/` (`lang-ger-de/`, was `lang-de/`); the
  formats were raised (the index to 4, the search index to 5, the viewer's rendered pages to 5), so the caches are
  built again on their own.

### Added
- `exphelp.ini [DocLanguages] Legacy[]` (`Legacy[en/us]=eng-us`, `Legacy[de]=ger-de`, also built in): the old
  directory names of 6.0.14 and before, for old addresses only; no file is read from an old directory
  (`expHelpLanguage::resolveDirectory()`, `splitLegacy()`).

### Updated
- `expHelpLanguage` and `exphelp.ini [DocLanguages]`: a locale reads an alias of `Map[]` (now aliases only:
  `eng-GB` to `eng-us`, `ger-AT` and `ger-CH` to `ger-de`), else the directory of its own lowercased code when
  `Directories[]` lists it (`ger-DE` reads `ger-de`), both tried with and without the variant, else the first
  directory of its language (any other `ger-*` reads `ger-de`), else `Default=eng-us`; `Directories[]` lists `eng-us`
  and `ger-de`, `Names[]` is keyed by the new names.
- The help keeps every old address working: `/exphelp/viewer/en/us/<id>`, `/exphelp/viewer/doc/en/us/<id>` and
  `/exphelp/viewer/de/<translated path>` are redirected to the page id, like the new `/exphelp/viewer/doc/eng-us/<id>`
  and `/exphelp/viewer/ger-de/<translated path>`; `?lang=` takes the directory name (`eng-us`, `ger-de`) or a locale,
  and still accepts `en-us` and `de`.
- Every reference to the old directories in the repository: kernel code, templates, settings (`exphelp.ini` path
  rules and the `Doc=` entries of the other INI files), translations, tests and fixtures, command line scripts, CI,
  the root files and the links of the documentation itself, including GitHub links into `doc/eng-us/`. The released
  changelogs and the history pages keep the paths of their time.
- [Translated documentation](../../eng-us/bc/6.0/translated-documentation.md): the naming rule, the alias settings
  and an upgrade table for sites, overrides and scripts written for the old `en/us/` and `de/` directories;
  [help system](../../eng-us/features/6.0/help-system.md) and
  [Reading the help in your language](../../eng-us/help/reading-help-in-other-languages.md) follow it.

### Tests
- `expHelpTranslatedDocumentationTest`: TD-01 the tracked files below `doc/` are in `doc/eng-us/`, `doc/ger-de/`,
  `doc/changelogs/` or `doc/README.md`; TD-02 no tracked text file names the old directories below `doc/` outside the
  history; TD-03 and TD-04 the lowercased locale, the aliases and the fallbacks; TD-11 `?lang=` with the new and the
  old keys. The redirects of the old addresses are tested with the viewer.

### Operator note
- After the deploy, remove the old copies (`en/us/` and `de/` below `doc/`) if the deploy copied files without deleting
  (the help does not read them), deploy with `--kernel`, and run `./console exp:help index --rebuild` so every
  language's index and search are built at once; the old `lang-de/` cache directory can be removed.

## 20 October 2026: the system card: exp:expfetch, exp:info and exp:about

Guide: [The system card](../../eng-us/features/6.0/system-card.md). For hosts:
[The system card for hosts](../../eng-us/help/hosting/operations/system-card-for-hosts.md); for extension developers:
[Facts, themes and logos for the system card](../../eng-us/help/extension-development/part-3-extension-developer/system-card-providers.md);
for partners: [Verifying a customer installation](../../eng-us/help/partners/verifying-a-customer-installation.md);
for demos: [The system card in demos](../../eng-us/help/advocates/the-system-card-in-demos.md); for trainers:
[Lesson 5.5](../../eng-us/help/trainers/hosting-operations/module-05-monitoring-and-logs/system-card-exercise.md).
Reference: [exp:expfetch](../../eng-us/examples/commands/expfetch.md), [syscard.ini](../../eng-us/examples/ini-settings/syscard/README.md).

### Added
- `./console exp:expfetch`: the system card. The orange Exponential "e" in block art (three sizes, and ASCII
  drawings) next to the facts of the installation, grouped in sections: Exponential (version, line, git branch and
  commit of a checkout), System (operating system, kernel, host, uptime, load, CPU, memory, disk, shell, terminal),
  PHP (version and SAPI, OPcache, JIT, the extensions that matter, `memory_limit`), Engine (Exponential Velocity:
  version, running, live and configured workers, ports and TLS, envvars, demo mode; Apache, nginx, Caddy,
  FrankenPHP, PHP-FPM masters and pool workers), Database (type, version, host only) and Site (siteaccesses,
  extensions, demo mode, debug, the mail transport's type, caches, help, cron, Composer). A Hints block closes it,
  worst first, and the color bars (the terminal's 16 colors and a gradient bar).
- `./console exp:info`: the card plus an inventory: every active extension with its version (`extension.xml`,
  `ezinfo.php`, `composer.json`), each siteaccess with its design, language and SiteURL host and the URL mode, the
  settings of an allowlist (`syscard.ini [Inventory] Settings[]`), the cronjob parts and their scripts, the expqueue
  service and workers, the size of the cache directory and of its largest caches, the help coverage per language,
  every local disk, the Velocity instances and worker model, and the PHP-FPM pools.
- `./console exp:about`: the card plus the project: what Exponential is, the Exponential Foundation and 7x,
  Exponential Velocity as part of 6.0.15, the license (GPL 2.0 or later, documentation GFDL 1.3 or later), links,
  how to contribute (CONTRIBUTING.md, the CLA), "Become an Exponential Partner" and the credits. The text is
  `share/syscard/about.txt`, written from the documentation, each block naming its source.
- Options of all three: `--json` (sections, hints, how each provider ran), `--plain` (no logo, no colors, for
  pasting), `--no-logo`, `--section=`, `--no-color`, `--color=auto|truecolor|256|16|none`, `--theme=`, `--width=`,
  `--ascii`, `--no-hints`, `--no-bars`, `--watch[=seconds]` with `--count=` (refreshes in place like `top`: load,
  memory, disk and the Velocity workers; Ctrl-C stops), `--animate` (a reveal of the logo and its gradient in under a
  second, only on a color terminal), `--mode=card|info|about`, `--list-providers`, `--list-themes`,
  `--completion=bash|zsh`.
- The layout follows the terminal: the big "e" with the rainbow wordmark EXPONENTIAL from 82 columns, then the big,
  small and mini "e", and stacked below 41 columns. Colors only on a terminal and never with `NO_COLOR`; the depth is
  detected (truecolor from `COLORTERM`, 256 from `TERM`, else 16) with the nearest color at each depth. Themes:
  `rainbow` (default), `orange`, `mono`, `high-contrast` and `light` (for a light background), each with
  `<Role>16` fallbacks for 16-color terminals.
- Health hints: load above twice the cores, memory or a disk nearly full, a PHP branch near or past the end of its
  security support (`syscard.ini [Php] SecuritySupportEnds[]`), OPcache off, a recommended PHP extension missing,
  `memory_limit` below 256M, no database connection, demo mode on, debug output for everyone, the view cache off,
  a stale help index, no recent cronjob run, Composer packages that disagree (ezupdate's inventory, local files only,
  cached), a queue without workers.
- The extension API: providers implement `expSysCardProvider` (most extend `expSysCardBaseProvider`), read through an
  `expSysCardContext` (a fake one for tests) and return `expSysCardFact` rows with a severity and an optional hint.
  `settings/syscard.ini [Providers] ProviderClasses[]` lists them in card order; an extension adds its own in its
  `syscard.ini.append.php`, and `Enabled[<id>]`, `Priority[<id>]` and `Budget[<id>]` switch them off and tune them.
  Themes are `[Theme-<name>]` blocks; logos are text files in `share/syscard/logos/` and in the directories of
  `[Logo] Directories[]`.
- Time budgets: each provider has one, and all together `[Runtime] TotalBudget`; with `[Runtime] Isolation=enabled`
  each provider runs in a child process that is killed when its time is up, so a hanging database or disk shows
  `n/a (timeout)` and never hangs the card.
- Classes: `expSysCardFact`, `expSysCardContext`, `expSysCardProvider`, `expSysCardBaseProvider`,
  `expSysCardRegistry`, `expSysCardColor`, `expSysCardTheme`, `expSysCardLogo`, `expSysCardRenderer` and sixteen
  providers in `kernel/classes/syscard/`; the command in `kernel/private/classes/commands/syscard.php`, its entry point
  `bin/php/expfetch.php`; `settings/syscard.ini`; `share/syscard/`.
- No secrets: the card never prints a password, key, token, database user, database name or full DSN, nor the values
  of Velocity's envvars. Every fact passes `expSysCardRegistry::safe()`: a fact named like a secret is dropped and a
  password, token or key inside a value is masked, whatever a provider (also an extension's) returns.
- Naming: only the command is called "expfetch", after the tools it is modeled on. Its code is the "system card"
  (`expSysCard*`, `syscard.ini`), because "fetch" already names the template fetch functions, the fetch definitions of
  modules, function collections and the fetch methods of persistent objects.

### Updated
- The console (`bin/php/console`) passes the name it was called by, an alias too, to the command in the environment
  variable `EXP_CONSOLE_COMMAND`, so a command can show more under an alias (`exp:info`, `exp:about`).
- The help: the canonical page, pages for hosts, extension developers, trainers, advocates and press, supporters and
  partners, the installation and upgrade chapters (run `exp:expfetch` after installing or upgrading), the glossary
  and the FAQs of supporters, partners and advocates.

### Tests
- `tests/tests/kernel/classes/syscard/` (no database): each provider with a fake context; the registry (the order of
  `ProviderClasses[]`, a provider added by an extension, `Enabled[]=disabled`, priorities and budgets, a provider
  that hangs shown as a timeout); the renderer at 40, 80 and 120 columns, at each color depth and with `NO_COLOR`;
  `--json` is valid JSON and carries no secret; the alias set of the command; and a check that the new code has no
  "fetch" outside the command name.

## 20 October 2026: Exponential Velocity is required again and runs on PHP 8.0

### Updated
- Updated: `composer.json` requires Exponential Velocity (`se7enxweb/exponential-velocity`) at `~0.0.4.54` again,
  instead of only suggesting it. From release `v0.0.4.54` (2026-10-20) the engine declares `"php": ">=8.0"` (it was
  `>=8.1`) and runs on PHP 8.0 to 8.5 with nothing extra, so a `composer install` on PHP 8.0, the stock PHP of Red
  Hat Enterprise Linux 9, installs the engine too. The el9 rpm depends on `php-cli >= 8.0` and installs without
  enabling a module stream; on RHEL 8, enable `php:8.0` and use the phar or Composer. On PHP 8.0, AVIF image output
  (needs 8.1) and the dashboard's per-request memory peak (needs 8.2) are not available. Engine changelog:
  [CHANGELOG.md](https://github.com/se7enxweb/exponential-velocity/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md); details:
  [PHP 8.0 support](../../eng-us/bc/6.0/php-8.0-support.md#exponential-velocity-on-php-80) and
  [Velocity engines](../../eng-us/bc/6.0/velocity-engines.md#php-80-and-red-hat-enterprise-linux).
- The README, the installation guide (chapters 1, 2, 3, 8, 11, 12, 14 and 15), the PHP 8.0 and PHP 8 pages, the
  Velocity feature pages and the help pages for hosts, businesses, governments, partners, the press and trainers
  state PHP 8.0 as Velocity's minimum, with the RHEL notes and what differs on 8.0.

## 20 October 2026: the administration views take their options as view parameters only

As the help center does, every administration view and endpoint below takes its options as view parameters,
`/(name)/value`, and never as GET query parameters: a `?name=` in the address is not read, there is no fallback and
no redirect of a GET. Filter forms without scripts are sent with POST (with the form token) and answered with a 303
to the canonical `/(name)/value` address. Each `module.php` declares the parameters as `unordered_params`.

### Added
- `expViewParameters` (`kernel/classes/expviewparameters.php`): reads a view parameter off `$Params`, builds the
  `/(name)/value` part of an address, and answers a posted filter form with a 303 to it.

### Updated
- Updated: content/job (`(json)/1`, `(log)/1`), content/jobs (`(state)`, `(type)`, `(user)`, `(sort)`, `(order)`,

## 20 October 2026: one reversible encoding for the values of view parameters

A view parameter, `/(name)/value`, cannot carry a `/` as it is: Apache refuses `%2F`, and the kernel decodes the
address (twice unless `DecodeOnce` is enabled) before it splits it. The views handled that in three ways: most of
them turned `/`, `(` and `)` into spaces (a link list or URL alias search for `example.com/a` lost its slash, and a
`+` became a space), the content finder and the settings page wrote `~` and a base64url, the template list and the
log viewer `~XX`. They now share one encoding. Guide:
[View parameters: how a value travels in an address](../../eng-us/guides/urls-and-aliases.md#9-view-parameters-how-a-value-travels-in-an-address).

### Updated
- Updated: `expViewParameters` (`kernel/classes/expviewparameters.php`) writes a value with `encode()`: `/`, `(`, `)`,
  `~`, `%`, `+`, `\`, `?`, `#` and control characters as `~` and two hexadecimal digits (`~2F` for `/`), a value of
  only `.` or `..` as `~2E` each, everything else readable (letters, digits, `, : @ = ! *` as they are, a space
  `%20`). `decode()` is the inverse; `get()` and `text()` return the value decoded, `raw()` as the kernel handed it
  over, `escape()` what the kernel hands over for a value. `suffix()` encodes, and `clean()` no longer turns `/`, `(`
  and `)` into spaces.
- Updated: every converted view writes and reads its values that way: the searches of url/list, content/urltranslator,
  content/urlwildcards, search/stats, content/bookmark, content/draft, role/list, user/unactivated (its search keeps
  `/` and brackets now), oauthadmin/keys, mailpreferences/admin and pdf/list; contentfinder/search `(text)` and
  settings/view `(q)`; visual/templatelist `(filter)`; the log viewer's permalinks and feed (`expLogPermalink::encode()`
  and `decode()` call it); the search of package/list, package/compare and package/view (written once instead of
  twice); the content check options.
- Updated: templates write a value with the new template operator `view_parameter` (`expViewParameterOperator`,
  `kernel/common/expviewparameteroperator.php`): the setting links of the settings page and the link back to the
  content finder. Scripts write it with `window.a4ViewParameter.encode()` (and read it with `decode()`), added to
  `a4_url.js` of the admin and admin4 designs: the log viewer (`page/setup_logs.js`, its permalinks, feed, content
  finder and audit links), the subitems export and the static cache stream.
- Updated: links written before keep working: contentfinder/search and settings/view still read the `~` + base64url
  form (`Contentfinder\Search::textFromParameter()`, when the value is not one the new encoding writes), the `~XX`
  form of the log viewer and the template list (a space `~20`) is read by the same `decode()`, and the package views
  still read their search encoded twice (`demo%2520content`).
- Updated: the guides of URLs and aliases (section 9, English and German), the settings page, the content finder, the
  log viewer (English and German) and finding an error by its reference; the kernel operators page and the operator
  index list `view_parameter`; the generated module view and operator pages.

### Tests
- `expViewParametersTest`: the encoding character by character, its inverse and the `~XX` links of before, round trips
  through the kernel's decoding once and twice for `/`, `(`, `)`, `~`, spaces, `%`, `+`, unicode, emoji, dots, control
  characters and quotes, how the kernel splits an encoded address, and the template operator.
- `AdminViewParametersOnlyTest` VP-05 (no hand-written encoding in the converted views, templates and scripts:
  no base64url, no double `rawurlencode()`, no `|urlencode` or bare `encodeURIComponent()` after `/(name)/`) and VP-06
  (`a4_url.js` the same in admin and admin4; in node, `a4ViewParameter.encode()` writes exactly what
  `expViewParameters::encode()` writes and `decode()` gives the value back).
- The content finder (CF-08, both forms), log viewer, package, PDF export, bookmark and unactivated users tests follow
  the new addresses.

### Operator note
- Deploy with `--kernel` (a new kernel class, `expViewParameterOperator`, and a new template operator) and
  `--packer` (`a4_url.js` and the admin scripts changed). Bookmarked links keep working.

## 20 October 2026: the help shows external images from a local copy

### Updated
- Updated: the help shows the external images of a page (README.md's screenshots on `github.com/user-attachments`) as images
  again, without widening the administration's Content Security Policy: `./console exp:help index` stores a copy of
  every `https://` image a page references in `var/<site>/cache/exphelp/images/<sha256 of the address>.<suffix>`
  (`expHelpRemoteImages`), and the new view `exphelp/image/(id)/<sha256>` (policy `exphelp/read`, the id a view
  parameter, never GET) serves it with its content type, `nosniff` and a long private cache lifetime, so `img-src
  'self'` allows it. Fetching follows redirects over https only, never to a private or reserved address, stops at
  5 MB and 15 seconds, and keeps only PNG, JPEG, GIF and WebP whose bytes match the type (no SVG). An image without a
  copy keeps the labeled link of before. The `width`, `height` and `alt` of an `<img>` tag are kept as attributes.
  `exp:help index --no-images` skips the fetching; the rendered pages are cached again (`RENDER_VERSION` 6, and the
  key follows the image directory). Guide: [help system](../../eng-us/features/6.0/help-system.md#external-images).

### Tests
- `expHelpRemoteImagesTest` (no database, a fake fetcher): redirects followed and the copy stored readable by the
  other server user; plain http, a redirect to http or `file:`, another port, too many redirects, a status other than
  200, a body over the limit, SVG and bytes of another type refused; the references of a page; the renderer's
  `<img>` with the local address and the size and alt of the tag; the view refuses unknown ids, upper case, path
  tricks, a further path and a symbolic link.

### Operator note
- After the deploy (`--kernel`: a new class and a new view), run `./console exp:help index` as the user the help cache
  belongs to, so the copies are made; the server needs to reach `github.com` over https once for it.
## 20 October 2026: Exponential Velocity is part of Exponential

As of 6.0.15 the Velocity Internet / Web Application Server is part of Exponential. This marks a turning point for the
project, a cornerstone and a capstone at once: a new level of application system integration, which provides even
greater features out of the box in the core product, by default. Exponential is now a whole working unit, not just a
framework: a rapid application development (RAD) stack for Internet applications that is a content management system,
a digital experience platform and an e-commerce system in one installation, from the server to the rendered page.

The story, for every reader: [Velocity is part of Exponential 6.0.15](../../eng-us/help/velocity-is-part-of-exponential-6-0-15.md).
Every feature page: [Exponential 6.0 features](../../eng-us/features/6.0/README.md).

### Updated
- `composer.json` requires `se7enxweb/exponential-velocity ~0.0.4.54`; it moved from `suggest` to `require`. A
  `composer install` on any supported PHP installs the server with the rest of the product.
- Velocity 0.0.4.54 runs on PHP 8.0 and later (it needed 8.1 before), so the kernel and the server now share one
  minimum: PHP 8.0, the stock PHP of Red Hat Enterprise Linux 9 included. It needs `ext-sockets`; `pcntl` and
  `openssl` are recommended for the worker pool and HTTPS.
- Velocity runs next to Apache with PHP-FPM, nginx and FrankenPHP. Its own server (`[ServerSettings] Engine=qbix`) is
  the recommended engine for every stage; FrankenPHP is the production-ready alternative; Apache and nginx keep working
  as before. The shipped `settings/velocity.ini` still starts PHP's built-in server (`Engine=php`) until a site chooses.
- The commands: `./console exp:velocity deploy` (autoloads, settings, caches, PHP-FPM and Velocity in one ordered run,
  stopping at the first failure), `start`, `restart` and `cache clear`.
- The documentation tells it throughout: the README, the installation chapters (requirements, getting the code,
  serving the site, upgrading, troubleshooting), the Velocity feature pages, the BC notes on the engines and on PHP
  8.0, the help start page, the project FAQ, the press kit and the Velocity stories, and the start pages for hosting
  companies, extension developers and decision makers. Every page that said Velocity needs PHP 8.1, or that it is
  only suggested, now says it runs on 8.0 and is required.

### Added
- The help page [Velocity is part of Exponential 6.0.15](../../eng-us/help/velocity-is-part-of-exponential-6-0-15.md),
  linked from the help start page and from the new features index
  [Exponential 6.0 features](../../eng-us/features/6.0/README.md), which lists every 6.0 feature page by subject.

### Upgrading
- Run `composer install` as usual; it installs `se7enxweb/exponential-velocity`. Nothing switches engines on its own:
  a site served by Apache, nginx or FrankenPHP is served the same way after the upgrade.
## 20 October 2026: Velocity's opcode cache has room for the help center

### Updated

- Updated: `settings/velocity.ini` gives the Velocity server process 512 MB of opcode cache
  (`opcache.memory_consumption`, was 256) and 64 MB for interned strings (`opcache.interned_strings_buffer`, was 32).
  The help center's index files are 18 to 24 MB per language, and with the old values the interned strings buffer
  was full after a few help requests. The memory is shared by the workers of an instance and reserved, not used,
  until it fills. The PHPSettings reference, the FrankenPHP and opcode cache pages and the hosting page on PHP
  settings show the new values.
## 20 October 2026: the help center takes its options as view parameters only

### Updated
- Updated: The help center takes every option as a view parameter (`/(name)/value`), never as a GET query parameter,
  with no fallback and no redirect: `/(lang)/<key>` instead of `?lang=` (the original of a translated page),
  `/(format)/json` instead of `?format=json` (the instant search), `/(format)/epub|pdf|dita` instead of `?format=` (the
  manuals' downloads) and `/(tracker)/<key>` instead of `?tracker=` (the error report); a page id is no longer read
  from `?p=`. `kernel/exphelp/module.php` declares them as `unordered_params`, the templates and the help script build
  only view parameter addresses, and the help search forms (the dashboard, the pages, the header menus, the audience
  pages, the extension report) are sent with POST, which the search view redirects (303) to its canonical address.
  `expHelp::url()` appends its options as view parameters.

### Tests
- `AdminHelpViewParametersOnlyTest`: no help template, header help template or help script builds `?lang=`,
  `?format=`, `?q=`, `?p=` or `?tracker=`, the help views read no `$_GET`, module.php declares the parameters and no help
  form is a GET; `AdminHelpScriptTest`, `expHelpLinksTest` and `expHelpSearchViewParametersTest` follow the view
  parameter addresses.

## 21 October 2026: CORS for the REST API, PKCE and state for OAuth

### Added
- CORS for everything `index_rest.php` serves (the content API v1 and v2, the OAuth token endpoint and the routes of
  every REST provider), off by default: `rest.ini [CorsSettings]` with `Cors`, an allowlist of exact origins
  (`AllowedOrigins[]`; `*` only without credentials, `null` never), `AllowedMethods[]`, `AllowedHeaders[]`,
  `ExposedHeaders[]`, `MaxAge` and `AllowCredentials`. A preflight is answered by the REST kernel without running the
  request (204, or 403 when the origin, method or a header is not allowed); every response carries `Vary: Origin`
  while CORS is on, and only an allowed origin gets `Access-Control-*` headers (`expRestCors`,
  [CORS for the REST API](../../eng-us/bc/6.0/rest-cors.md)).
- PKCE (RFC 7636) for the OAuth authorization code flow: `oauth/authorize` takes `code_challenge` and
  `code_challenge_method` and stores them with the code, the token endpoint checks `code_verifier` and uses the code
  up on a mismatch. `S256` is accepted, `plain` only with `AllowPlainPkce=enabled`. A client stored without a secret
  is a public client: it sends no `client_secret` and must use PKCE (`RequirePkceForPublicClients`, enabled by
  default); `RequirePkceForConfidentialClients` (disabled by default) requires it for every client
  (`expOAuthPkce`, [PKCE and state](../../eng-us/bc/6.0/oauth-pkce.md)).

### Updated
- `oauth/authorize` sends the `state` of the request back unchanged with the code, the token and every error that
  goes to the client's end point, and carries `state` and the PKCE parameters through the authorization form.
- An authorization code or refresh token is redeemed only by the client it was issued to.
- An unknown `response_type` is answered with `error=unsupported_response_type`; an end point with a query string of
  its own keeps it when the parameters are added.
- The REST and OAuth guides, the app creator and business help pages, the API standards page and the procurement
  requirements describe CORS, PKCE and `state`, in English and German.

### Upgrading
- Run the `ezprest_authcode` statements of `update/database/<engine>/6.0/dbupdate-6.0.0-6.0.15.sql` (two nullable
  columns). Kernel schemas and `share/db_schema.dba` have them for new installations.

### Tests
- `expRestCorsTest`, `expOAuthPkceTest`, `expOAuthPkceSchemaTest`, and new cases in `ezpOauthAuthorizeRedirectTest`
  (state returned, challenge stored, plain and malformed challenges refused before the login, PKCE required for a
  public client). All without a database.
## 21 October 2026: the administration's site links of host-matched siteaccesses

### Updated
- Fixed: the administration's site menu and "Open the site" link (`siteaccess_url()`, `siteaccess_menu()`,
  `ezpSiteAccessURL::root()`) gave a host-matched siteaccess its URI as well when the page was on another host: with
  `MatchOrder=host_uri;host;uri`, the administration on its own host linked the site to `https://example.com/site/`,
  where the host map picks the site and `/site/` is read as a module, so the link answered 404. `HostUriMatchMapItems`
  were not taken into account at all. The address is now the first one the matching rules (`host_uri`, `host`, `uri`
  in `MatchOrder` order) lead to the siteaccess with nothing of the path left over: this host with its host and URI
  entry or its URI, its own `[SiteSettings] SiteURL` (host, port and path), a host the host map gives it, then one of
  its host and URI entries; the URI on this host remains the fallback. Siteaccesses matched by URI on the host the
  administration runs on keep the address they had. Documented in the `siteaccess_url` operator reference.

### Tests
- `ezpSiteAccessURLTest`: host-matched, host and URI-matched and URI-matched siteaccesses seen from the
  administration's own host, `SiteURL` used only when the rules lead there, and the address check itself. No database.
## 21 October 2026: the audit syslog sink under open_basedir

### Updated
- Fixed: the audit syslog sink (`expAuditSyslogSink`) called `file_exists()` on `/run/systemd/journal/socket` and
  `/dev/log`, so on a host with `open_basedir` (PHP limited to the site folder and `/tmp`) every request wrote an
  open_basedir warning into the error log. The sink now reads `open_basedir` first (parsed as PHP does, decided once
  per process, `expAuditSyslogSink::pathAllowed()`), never touches a socket outside it and sends through PHP's
  `syslog()` instead. Without `open_basedir` nothing changes.

### Tests
- `expAuditSinksTest` SK-08: the open_basedir parsing, the fallback with an injected setting, and a PHP process
  started with a real `open_basedir` that raises no warning. No database.
## 21 October 2026: layout mappings by route and time, imports and helpers

### Fixed
- Fixed: A layout mapping with a **Route** target matches on the site. The value is a module view (`content/search`,
  `user/*`, `content/view` for every content page, `*`); the media design lays out its pages that are not content
  pages through such a mapping only (explayouts 1.4.42, sevenx_themes_media 1.5.75).
- Fixed: A **Time** condition switches the layout at its start and end. The resolver kept the answer for
  `[ResolverSettings] CacheTTL` (an hour) and the page cache kept the page as long again; the answer now expires at
  the condition's next start or end, and the page is kept out of the page caches when that comes within
  `[HttpCacheSettings] MaxAge`. A **Query parameter** condition is evaluated on every request, so `/page` and
  `/page?variant=b` can show different layouts (explayouts 1.4.42).
- Fixed: `exp:layouts import --publish` (and an import that writes rules) purges the page cache itself; the pages
  kept the old layout until the cache was cleared by hand (explayouts_core 1.0.10).
- Fixed: `./console ext:explayouts:block_list`, `layout_info`, `collection_list`, `layout_copy`, `layout_list`,
  `rule_list`, `share_create` and the other helpers of explayouts ended with `Class "..." not found`; they run on
  their own now (explayouts 1.4.42).
- Fixed: A block an extension adds with `AvailableBlocks[]` is offered wherever the extension is listed in
  `ActiveExtensions[]`: explayouts' lists no longer start with a reset line (explayouts 1.4.42). The media design's
  Product highlight block appears in the block list for the first time.

### Updated
- Updated: The "Two ways to build" help pages no longer describe these as limitations or workarounds (English and
  German).

### Upgrading
- Update `se7enxweb/explayouts` to 1.4.42, `se7enxweb/explayouts-core` to 1.0.10 and
  `se7enxweb/sevenx-themes-media` to 1.5.75. An installation that relied on explayouts' reset line to drop a block an

## 21 October 2026: a payment gateway made in Setup > RAD is found

### Updated
- The handler wizards for payment gateways (`/setup/handlerextension/paymentgateway` and `paymentgatewaydirect`,
  `kernel/setup/exphandlerwizard.php`) write the class to
  `extension/<name>/paymentgateways/<alias>/classes/<alias>gateway.php`, which is where
  `eZPaymentGatewayType` looks (`<GatewaysDirectories>/<gateway>/classes/<gateway>gateway.php` for each name in

  page on payment gateways for website creators give the new path; the services API's gateway list

  `extension/<name>/paymentgateways/<alias>/classes/<alias>gateway.php` and change the second argument of its
  `eZPaymentGatewayType::registerGateway()` call to the class name. The settings it came with need no change.

## 21 October 2026: downloads on Velocity carry their type again, and an EPUB is application/epub+zip

### Updated
- Updated: The HTTP cache contract (`kernel/private/classes/httpcache/ezphttpcachecontract.php`) no longer loads
  `eZFile` and `eZDir`. Velocity's parent process loads the contract before it installs its source transform, and every
  worker inherits the classes compiled there: an `eZFile` compiled that way kept PHP's own `header()`, which does
  nothing under the CLI SAPI. Every `content/download` on Velocity (:8080) went out as `text/html; charset=utf-8`,
  without `Content-Disposition`, and a range request was answered 200 instead of 206; Apache was not affected. The
  contract limits the modes of the files it writes itself (`ezpHttpCacheContract::fileMode()`, `dirMode()`), with
  `eZFile` and `eZDir` when they are loaded and by the same rule (`EZP_FILE_MODE_MAX`, `EZP_DIR_MODE_MAX`) otherwise.
- Updated: `settings/mime.ini` knows `.epub` as `application/epub+zip`, so the help e-books and every stored EPUB are
  downloaded with that type instead of `application/octet-stream`.

### Tests
- `expFileModeLimitsTest` (FM-08): loading the contract (with the repair queue, before the autoloader) declares neither
  `eZFile` nor `eZDir`, and the contract limits modes exactly as they do for ten ways of setting the constants.
  `eZUtilsHelpersTest` looks up an `.epub` file. Without a database.

## 21 October 2026: the VAT rules page says when no handler applies the rules

### Updated
- VAT charging rules are applied only by a VAT handler (`shop.ini [VATSettings] Handler`, `ezdefault` for the shipped
  `eZDefaultVATHandler`), and the shipped `settings/shop.ini` sets none. The **VAT rules** page
  (`shop/vatrules`, design/admin and design/admin4) now says so when rules exist and no handler applies them, and
  when the handler that is set cannot be loaded (`Exponential\View\Kernel\Shop\Vatrules::handlerNotice()`).
- The shipped file keeps `Handler` commented out on purpose. With a handler set, `eZVATManager` reports dynamic VAT
  charging as enabled, and an existing store would change at checkout: `eZShopOperationCollection::handleUserCountry()`
  stops an order whose customer has no known country (with `RequireUserCountry=true`, the shipped value), the
  "Determined by VAT charging rules" VAT type appears on price fields, and that type is charged 0 percent instead of
  "unknown" where no rule matches. Fixed VAT types are charged the same with or without a handler.
- The help pages on VAT rules and VAT types and on products, prices and VAT (English and German) explain the setting.

### Upgrading
- Nothing to do unless the new notice appears. To apply your VAT rules, set `Handler=ezdefault` in `[VATSettings]` of
  `settings/override/shop.ini.append.php`, check that the customer's country is known at checkout
  (`UserCountryAttribute`, or the country of the shop account handler), and test an order before going live.

### Tests
- `eZShopPricesTest`: the shipped `shop.ini` sets no VAT handler, a fixed VAT type is charged the same with and
  without one, and the VAT rules page's notice (no rules, no handler, a handler, a handler that cannot be loaded).
  Without a database.

## 21 October 2026: the media design loads the stylesheets of design.ini

### Updated
- sevenx_themes_media 1.5.74: the media design (Fit & Healthy) loads the stylesheets extensions add to
  `design.ini [StylesheetSettings] CSSFileList[]` and `FrontendCSSFileList[]`, after its own files and in the same
  packed bundle. Before, its page layout listed its stylesheets in `pagelayout/head/style.tpl` only, and a file an
  extension added (cjw_newsletter.css, eztags.css, ezgmaplocation.css ...) never reached a media page. The entries
  the theme replaces are named in its new `MediaReplacedCSSFileList[]` and stay off the page, as before.
- The help pages **Styling your blocks** and the examples **Move inline styles into the stylesheet** and
  **Restyle blocks without touching markup** (English and German) add a stylesheet with `FrontendCSSFileList[]`
  instead of a copy of `style.tpl`.

### Upgrading
- Repin `se7enxweb/sevenx-themes-media` to 1.5.74, then clear the INI, packer and template-block caches. A site that
  copied `pagelayout/head/style.tpl` into its own extension keeps working; it can drop the copy and list its file in
  `FrontendCSSFileList[]`.
